Note that additional
C^{3,\alpha}
regularity is needed on the filament centerline to show Lemma
1.14
. Furthermore, the
\epsilon
-dependence in the bounds (
75
) and (
76
) is not explicit. The proof of Lemma
1.14
appears in section
6
.

2.
Proofs of Theorems
1.5
and
1.2

Given the decomposition (
66
) of
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{-1}
along with Lemmas
1.9
,
1.10
,
1.11
,
1.12
,
1.13
, and
1.14
, we may proceed immediately to the proof of Theorem
1.5
in section
2.1
. Given Theorem
1.5
and Lemma
1.4
, we may then prove Theorem
1.2
for the simple evolution (
20
) in section
2.2
. The remainder of this paper is devoted to the proofs of each of the above key lemmas.

2.1.
Proof of Theorem
1.5
: operator decomposition

We begin by stating some properties of the map
\Phi
(
27
) used to identify the tangential and normal directions along curved
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
with the tangential and normal directions along the straight filament
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
.
Note that the map
\Phi
commutes with derivatives in
\theta
along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
but does not commute with derivatives in
s
. In particular, we have

(77)

\displaystyle\partial_{s}(\Phi\bm{g})

\displaystyle=\Phi(\partial_{s}\bm{g})-\bm{g}\cdot(\kappa_{1}\bm{e}_{x}+\kappa_{2}\bm{e}_{y})\bm{e}_{\rm t}+\bm{g}\cdot(\kappa_{1}\bm{e}_{z}-\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{y})\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}

\displaystyle+\bm{g}\cdot(\kappa_{2}\bm{e}_{z}+\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{x})\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}

\displaystyle\partial_{s}(\Phi^{-1}\bm{h})

\displaystyle=\Phi^{-1}(\partial_{s}\bm{h})+\bm{h}\cdot(\kappa_{1}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}+\kappa_{2}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}})\bm{e}_{z}+\bm{h}\cdot(\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}-\kappa_{1}\bm{e}_{\rm t})\bm{e}_{x}

\displaystyle-\bm{h}\cdot(\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}+\kappa_{2}\bm{e}_{\rm t})\bm{e}_{y}\,.

From (
77
), we can see that

(78)

\displaystyle\left\lVert[\partial_{s},\Phi]\,\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\qquad\left\lVert[\partial_{s},\Phi]\,\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert[\partial_{s},\Phi^{-1}]\,\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\qquad\left\lVert[\partial_{s},\Phi^{-1}]\,\bm{h}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Furthermore, given two nearby filaments with centerlines
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, we note the following form of the difference between the corresponding maps
\Phi^{(a)}
and
\Phi^{(b)}
:

(79)

\displaystyle\Phi^{(a)}\bm{g}-\Phi^{(b)}\bm{g}

\displaystyle=(\bm{g}\cdot\bm{e}_{z})(\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(b)})+(\bm{g}\cdot\bm{e}_{x})(\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}^{(b)})+(\bm{g}\cdot\bm{e}_{y})(\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}^{(b)})

\displaystyle(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{h}-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{h}

\displaystyle=\bm{h}\cdot(\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(b)})\bm{e}_{z}+\bm{h}\cdot(\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}^{(b)})\bm{e}_{x}+\bm{h}\cdot(\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}^{(b)})\bm{e}_{y}\,.

Recalling the definition (
28
) of
\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi}
, we note that the subtracted mean differs slightly for different curves. However, using the definition (
27
) of
\Phi
, we may write

(80)

\displaystyle(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(b)}}

\displaystyle=(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{h}-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{h}-\bm{e}_{z}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{h}\cdot\big(\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(b)}\big)\,ds

\displaystyle-\bm{e}_{x}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{h}\cdot\big(\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}^{(b)}\big)\,ds-\bm{e}_{y}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{h}\cdot\big(\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}^{(b)}\big)\,ds\,.

Combining (
79
) and (
80
), we thus have

(81)

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(b)}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(b)}}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha}^{(b)})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

In addition, from (
77
), we obtain

(82)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\big[\partial_{s},(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\big]\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}-\big[\partial_{s},(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\big]\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(b)}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\big[\partial_{s},(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\big]\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}-\big[\partial_{s},(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\big]\bm{h}_{0}^{\Phi^{(b)}}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha}^{(b)})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

We may now proceed with the decomposition (
29
) of
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{-1}
. Recalling the representation (
66
) of
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{-1}
, we first separate the remainder terms into terms which are small in
\epsilon
and terms which are smoother. Using the decompositions
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon/+}
,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon/+}
,
\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon/+}
and
\bm{w}_{\epsilon/+}
from Lemmas
1.9
,
1.10
,
1.12
, and
1.13
, respectively, we define the terms
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}
in (
29
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}[\bm{v}(s)]

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}(s,\theta)\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}\,d\theta-\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon})_{0}^{\Phi}]\big]-\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{01}\Phi^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}\bigg[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}\,ds\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}[\bm{v}(s)]

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bm{w}_{+}(s,\theta)\,\epsilon^{2}\widehat{\kappa}\,d\theta+\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bm{w}(s,\theta)\,\epsilon\,d\theta ds-\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}[\bm{v}]\big]

\displaystyle\qquad-\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{w}_{0}^{\Phi}]\big]-\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},+}[\bm{w}_{0}^{\Phi}]\big]-\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[(\bm{w}_{+})_{0}^{\Phi}]\big]

\displaystyle\qquad-\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{01}\Phi^{-1}\mathcal{S}\displaystyle\bigg[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}\,ds\bigg]\bigg]-\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{01}\Phi^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{+}\displaystyle\bigg[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}\,ds\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad-\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{01}\Phi^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}\displaystyle\bigg[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}_{+}\,ds\bigg]\bigg]\,.

We first bound
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}
.
Using Lemmas
1.9
,
1.10
, and
1.13
, we make note of the following estimates for the second term:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon})_{0}^{\Phi}]\big]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*})\big(\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon})_{0}^{\Phi}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{0,\alpha}}+\epsilon\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon})_{0}^{\Phi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\epsilon}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Phi^{(a)}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}]\big]-\Phi^{(b)}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(b)}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(b)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(b)}}]\big]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\big(\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}]\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{0,\alpha}}+\epsilon\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}]\big]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}]-\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(b)}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(b)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(b)}}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{0,\alpha}}\ +\epsilon\left\lvert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}]-\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(b)}[(\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(b)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(b)}}]\right\rvert_{C_{s}^{1,\alpha}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\big(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}-\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

For the third term of
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}
, we may use Lemmas
1.9
,
1.12
, and
1.13
to estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{01}\Phi^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}\bigg[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}\,ds\bigg]\bigg]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\kappa_{*})\bigg(\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}\bigg[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}\,ds\bigg]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{0,\alpha}}+\epsilon\left\lvert\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}\bigg[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}\,ds\bigg]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\epsilon}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\Phi^{(a)}-\Phi^{(b)})\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{01}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\bigg[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\,ds\bigg]\bigg]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\Phi^{(b)}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{01}\bigg((\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\bigg[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\,ds\bigg]-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\bigg[\Phi^{(b)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\,ds\bigg]\bigg)\bigg]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\bigg[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\,ds\bigg]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}}\,\epsilon\left\lvert\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\bigg[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\,ds\bigg]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\bigg[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\,ds\bigg]-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\bigg[\Phi^{(b)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\,ds\bigg]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\epsilon\left\lvert(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\bigg[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\,ds\bigg]-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\bigg[\Phi^{(b)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\,ds\bigg]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\big(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}-\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Altogether, we may bound
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}
as

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha})\epsilon^{2-\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\epsilon}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}^{(a)}-\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}^{(b)})[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\,\widehat{\kappa}^{(a)}-\bm{w}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\,\widehat{\kappa}^{(b)}\big)\,d\theta\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\quad+c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

We next consider
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}
. For
0<\alpha<\gamma<\beta<1
, we may use Lemmas
1.9
,
1.11
, and
1.13
to estimate the third term involving the double layer remainder as

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Phi\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}[\bm{v}]\big]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*})\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Phi^{(a)}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]\big]-\Phi^{(b)}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\big]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\,\epsilon^{-1}\big(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}+\left\lVert(\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}^{(a)}-\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}^{(b)})[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Letting
\Phi\mathcal{M}_{1}=\Phi(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{w}_{0}^{\Phi}]\big]+\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},+}[\bm{w}_{0}^{\Phi}]\big]+\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[(\bm{w}_{+})_{0}^{\Phi}]\big])
denote the middle three terms of
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}
, we may use Lemmas
1.9
,
1.10
, and
1.13
to estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Phi\mathcal{M}_{1}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*})\big(\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{w}_{0}^{\Phi}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},+}[\bm{w}_{0}^{\Phi}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}[(\bm{w}_{+})_{0}^{\Phi}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\,\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\big(\left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\bm{w}_{+}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\big)\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Phi^{(a)}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{(a)}-\Phi^{(b)}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\bigg(\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{0}^{(a)})^{\Phi^{(a)}}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},+}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{0}^{(a)})^{\Phi^{(a)}}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\bigg)+\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{0}^{(a)})^{\Phi^{(a)}}]-\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S}}^{(b)}[(\bm{w}_{0}^{(b)})^{\Phi^{(b)}}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},+}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{0}^{(a)})^{\Phi^{(a)}}]-\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},+}^{(b)}[(\bm{w}_{0}^{(b)})^{\Phi^{(b)}}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(a)}[(\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(a)}}]-\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{S},\epsilon}^{(b)}[(\bm{w}_{+}^{(b)})_{0}^{\Phi^{(b)}}]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\big(\left\lVert\bm{w}_{0}^{(a)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\bm{w}_{0}^{(a)}-\bm{w}_{0}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)}-\bm{w}_{+}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Finally, letting
\Phi\mathcal{M}_{2}=\Phi\big(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\Phi^{-1}\mathcal{S}\big[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}\,ds\big]\big]

+\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\Phi^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{+}\big[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}\,ds\big]\big]

+\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\big[\mathbb{P}_{01}\Phi^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}\big[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}_{+}\,ds\big]\big]\big)
denote the final three terms of
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}
, we may use Lemmas
1.9
,
1.12
, and
1.13
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Phi\mathcal{M}_{2}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*})\bigg(\left\lVert\mathcal{S}\big[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}\,ds\big]\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{+}\big[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}\,ds\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}\big[\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{w}_{+}\,ds\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\big(\left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\bm{w}_{+}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\big)\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Phi^{(a)}\mathcal{M}_{2}^{(a)}-\Phi^{(b)}\mathcal{M}_{2}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\bigg(\left\lVert\mathcal{S}^{(a)}\big[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}^{(a)}\,ds\big]\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{+}^{(a)}\big[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}^{(a)}\,ds\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\big[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)}\,ds\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\mathcal{S}^{(a)}\big[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}^{(a)}\,ds\big]\big]-\mathcal{S}^{(b)}\big[\Phi^{(b)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{w}^{(b)}\,ds\big]\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{+}^{(a)}\big[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}^{(a)}\,ds\big]-\mathcal{H}_{+}^{(b)}\big[\Phi^{(b)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{w}^{(b)}\,ds\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\big[\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)}\,ds\big]-\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\big[\Phi^{(b)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{w}_{+}^{(b)}\,ds\big]\right\rVert_{C_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\big(\left\lVert\bm{w}^{(a)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\bm{w}^{(a)}-\bm{w}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)}-\bm{w}_{+}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Altogether, we may estimate
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}
as

(83)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma})\,\epsilon^{2-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{w}_{+}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}+\epsilon\left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}^{(a)}-\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}^{(b)})[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)}(\widehat{\kappa}^{(a)}-\widehat{\kappa}^{(b)})\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}+\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\widehat{\kappa}^{(b)}(\bm{w}_{+}^{(a)}-\bm{w}_{+}^{(b)})\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\bigg(\Phi^{(a)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}-\Phi^{(b)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bigg)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bm{w}^{(a)}\,\epsilon\,d\theta ds\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle+c(\kappa_{*,\gamma}^{(b)})\,\epsilon\left\lVert\bm{w}^{(a)}-\bm{w}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

In total we obtain the estimates (
30
) and (
31
) for the slender body DtN map decomposition (
29
).

We next turn to the decomposition (
32
) of the slender body NtD operator
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}
. We will rely on the DtN decomposition (
29
) to prove the subsequent bounds (
33
) and (
34
). In particular, given
\bm{X}(s)
and corresponding map
\Phi
, for
\bm{f}(s)\in C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
and
\bm{f}_{0}^{\Phi}(s)=\bm{f}(s)-\Phi\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Phi^{-1}\bm{f}(s)\,ds
, we may use (
29
) to write

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}\bm{v}(s)

\displaystyle=({\bf I}+\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}\Phi)_{0}^{\Phi})^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}[\Phi^{-1}\bm{f}_{0}^{\Phi}(s)]+({\bf I}+\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}\Phi)_{0}^{\Phi})^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\big[(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}[\bm{v}(s)])_{0}^{\Phi}\big]

\displaystyle=({\bf I}+\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}\Phi)_{0}^{\Phi})^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}[\Phi^{-1}\bm{f}_{0}^{\Phi}(s)]+({\bf I}+\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}\Phi)_{0}^{\Phi})^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\big[(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}[\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}[\bm{f}(s)]])_{0}^{\Phi}\big]\,.

Now, using Lemma
1.4
and the estimate (
30
) for
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}
, for any
\overline{\bm{g}}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}[\Phi\overline{\bm{g}}])_{0}^{\Phi}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\big(\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}[\Phi\overline{\bm{g}}]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\epsilon^{-1}\big|\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}[\Phi\overline{\bm{g}}]\big|_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lVert\Phi\overline{\bm{g}}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Thus for
\epsilon
sufficiently small, we may expand
({\bf I}+\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}\Phi)_{0}^{\Phi})^{-1}
as a Neumann series:

(84)

({\bf I}+\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}\Phi)_{0}^{\Phi})^{-1}={\bf I}+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\big(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}\Phi)_{0}^{\Phi}\big)^{j}=:{\bf I}+\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,\epsilon}\,,

with

(85)

\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,\epsilon}[\bm{g}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

We thus obtain the first part of the decomposition (
32
).
Furthermore, using the Lipschitz estimate (
31
) for
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}
, we also have

(86)

\left\lVert(\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,\epsilon}^{(a)}-\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,\epsilon}^{(b)})[\bm{g}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}(\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

In addition, defining

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,+}

\displaystyle:=({\bf I}+\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,\epsilon}\Phi)_{0}^{\Phi})^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\big[(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}[\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}[\cdot]])_{0}^{\Phi}\big]

\displaystyle=({\bf I}+\mathcal{R}_{\rm n0,\epsilon})\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\big[(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}[\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}[\cdot]])_{0}^{\Phi}\big]\,,

we may use (
85
), Lemma
1.4
, (
83
), and Lemma
1.14
to estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,+}[\bm{f}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\big[(\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}[\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}[\bm{f}]])_{0}^{\Phi}\big]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}[\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}[\bm{f}]]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}[\bm{f}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}\leq c(\epsilon,\|\bm{X}_{ss}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Using the Lipschitz estimates (
86
), (
31
), and (
76
) for
\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,\epsilon}
,
\mathcal{R}_{\rm d,+}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,+}^{(a)}-\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,+}^{(b)})[\bm{f}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(a)}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}},\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(b)}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}(\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

In total, we obtain the decomposition (
32
) and estimates (
33
), (
34
) for the slender body NtD map
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1.6
.

Given slender body force data of the form
\bm{f}(s)=\partial_{s}^{4}\bm{F}
, we may first use the commutator bounds (
78
) for
\Phi
to write

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}\partial_{s}^{4}\bm{F}

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}^{2}\big(\Phi^{-1}\partial_{s}^{2}\bm{F}\big)+\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},1}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},1}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\partial_{s}\big([\partial_{s},\Phi^{-1}]\partial_{s}^{2}\bm{F}\big)+[\partial_{s},\Phi^{-1}]\partial_{s}^{3}\bm{F}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}})\left\lVert\bm{F}\right\rVert_{C^{3,\alpha}}\,.

For two nearby filaments with centerlines
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, using the commutator estimates (
82
), we additionally have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},1}^{(a)}-\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},1}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\|\bm{X}^{(a)}\|_{C^{3,\alpha}},\|\bm{X}^{(b)}\|_{C^{3,\alpha}})\,\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{3,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{F}\right\rVert_{C^{3,\alpha}}\,.

We next note the following commutator estimate for
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{2}
.

Proposition 2.1

(Commutator estimate for
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{2}
)
.

Given a matrix-valued function
\bm{A}\in C^{n+1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
and a vector-valued function
\bm{g}\in C^{n,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
for
0\leq n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the commutator
[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{2},\bm{A}]\bm{g}
satisfies

(87)

\left\lVert[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{2},\bm{A}]\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{n,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{C^{n+1,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{n,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

The proof of Proposition
2.1
appears in Appendix
C
.
Applying Proposition
2.1
in the case
\bm{A}=\Phi^{-1}=\bm{e}_{z}\otimes\bm{e}_{\rm t}+\bm{e}_{x}\otimes\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}+\bm{e}_{y}\otimes\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}
and
n=1
, we have

\displaystyle(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{2})[\Phi^{-1}\partial_{s}^{2}\bm{F}]

\displaystyle=\Phi^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}\bm{F}+\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},2}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},2}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{2},\Phi^{-1}]\partial_{s}^{2}\bm{F}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}\leq c(\epsilon,\|\bm{X}\|_{C^{3,\gamma}})\left\lVert\bm{F}\right\rVert_{C^{3,\gamma}}\,.

Furthermore, for two nearby curves
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},2}^{(a)}-\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},2}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\|\bm{X}^{(a)}\|_{C^{3,\gamma}},\|\bm{X}^{(b)}\|_{C^{3,\gamma}})\,\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{3,\gamma}}\left\lVert\bm{F}\right\rVert_{C^{3,\gamma}}\,.

In total, we may rewrite

(88)

\Phi\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}[\Phi^{-1}(\partial_{s}^{4}\bm{F})_{0}^{\Phi}]=\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}[\partial_{s}^{4}\bm{F}]+\Phi\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},1})_{0}^{\Phi}+\Phi\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},2}\,.

Defining
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\rm n,+}=\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,+}+({\bf I}+\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,\epsilon})\Phi\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},1})_{0}^{\Phi}+({\bf I}+\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,\epsilon})\Phi\mathcal{R}_{\bm{X},2}
, we obtain Corollary
1.6
.
∎

2.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.2
: local well-posedness

We return to the notation
\bm{X}^{\sigma}(\sigma,t)
of section
1.2.1
for an evolving filament that is possibly changing length over time.
Using Corollary
1.6
, the evolution (
20
) may be written as

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bm{X}^{\sigma}}{\partial t}(\sigma,t)

\displaystyle=-\lambda^{-1}\big(({\bf I}+\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,\epsilon})\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}\bm{X}]+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\rm n,+}[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}\bm{X}]\big)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{X}^{\sigma}(\sigma,0)

\displaystyle=(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}(\sigma)\,,\qquad\left\lvert\partial_{\sigma}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert=1\,,

\displaystyle\lambda(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\left\lvert\partial_{\sigma}\bm{X}^{\sigma}\right\rvert\,d\sigma\,.

We will consider the evolution of the difference
\bm{Y}(\sigma,t)=\bm{X}^{\sigma}(\sigma,t)-(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}(\sigma)
, which satisfies

(89)

\frac{\partial\bm{Y}}{\partial t}(\sigma,t)=-\lambda^{-1}\bigg(\big({\bf I}+\mathcal{R}_{\rm n,\epsilon}\big)\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\big[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})\big]+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\rm n,+}\big[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})\big]\bigg)\,.

Using Duhamel’s formula, we may write
\bm{Y}(\sigma,t)
as

(90)

\displaystyle\bm{Y}(\sigma,t)

\displaystyle=e^{-\int_{0}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}-(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\,\lambda^{-1}\bigg(\mathcal{R}_{\epsilon}\big[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})\big]+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{+}\big[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})\big]\bigg)\,dt^{\prime}\,.

We will rely on the following two lemmas to estimate
\bm{Y}
given by (
90
).

Lemma 2.2

(Semigroup estimates)
.

Let
n,m
be nonnegative integers and
\alpha,\gamma\in(0,1)
with
n+\alpha\geq m+\gamma
.
For
\bm{V}(s)\in C^{m,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})
, we have

(91)

\left\lVert e^{-t\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{V}\right\rVert_{C^{n,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c\,t^{-\frac{n+\alpha-(m+\gamma)}{3}}\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert_{C^{m,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

In each case,
c
is an absolute constant bounded independent of
\epsilon
as
\epsilon\to 0
.

For the next lemma, given
T>0
and
0\leq k\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define the function spaces

(92)

\mathcal{Y}_{k}=\big\{\bm{V}\in L^{\infty}\big(0,T;C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})\big)\,:\,\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{k}}<\infty\}\,,\qquad\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{k}}=\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

We have the following.

Lemma 2.3

(Maximal regularity bound)
.

Given
\bm{g}\in\mathcal{Y}_{1}
, let

\displaystyle\bm{h}(s,t)=\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-t^{\prime})\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}\;\bm{g}(s,t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}\,.

Then, for some absolute constant
c
,

(93)

\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\leq\big(\epsilon+T^{1/4}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-3/4}+T\big)\,c\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\,.

The proofs of Lemmas
2.2
and (
2.3
) appear in section
3.5
.

Now, to track the non-self-intersection property (
2
), we will define the quantity

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{X}^{\sigma}(\cdot,t)\right\rvert_{\star}:=\inf_{\sigma\neq\sigma^{\prime}}\frac{\left\lvert\bm{X}^{\sigma}(\sigma,t)-\bm{X}^{\sigma}(\sigma^{\prime},t)\right\rvert}{\left\lvert\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\right\rvert}\,.

Using the definition (
92
) of the spaces
\mathcal{Y}_{k}
, we consider the closed ball

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{M,\frac{1}{2}}(T)=\big\{\bm{Y}\in\mathcal{Y}_{4}\,:\,\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\leq M\,,\;\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\leq\textstyle\min\{\frac{1}{2}\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star},\frac{1}{2}\}\big\}\,.

The
\mathcal{Y}_{1}
bound will be used to propagate a non-self-intersection condition and to consider only situations where the filament length remains close to its initial unit length. Note that this bound ensures that

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lvert\bm{X}^{\sigma}(\cdot,t)\right\rvert_{\star}\geq\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star}-\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star}\,.

Furthermore, if
\bm{Y}
satisfies
\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, then, using that
\left\lvert\partial_{\sigma}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert=1
and

(94)

\lambda=\int_{0}^{1}\left\lvert\partial_{\sigma}\bm{Y}+\partial_{\sigma}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert\,d\sigma\,,

we have
\frac{1}{2}\leq\lambda\leq\frac{3}{2}
and, in particular,

(95)

\frac{2}{3}(t-t^{\prime})\leq\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)\,d\tau\leq 2(t-t^{\prime})\,.

Now, for
\bm{Y}\in\mathcal{B}_{M,\frac{1}{2}}(T)
, let
\Lambda[\bm{Y}]
denote the map

(96)

\displaystyle\Lambda[\bm{Y}]

\displaystyle=e^{-\int_{0}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}-(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\,\lambda^{-1}\bigg(\mathcal{R}_{\epsilon}\big[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})\big]+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{+}\big[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})\big]\bigg)\,dt^{\prime}\,.

We show that the map
\Lambda
takes
\mathcal{B}_{M,\frac{1}{2}}(T)
to itself for some choice of
\epsilon,T>0
.

We first estimate the
\mathcal{Y}_{4}
contribution from the initial condition. Note that by (
95
) and Lemma
2.2
, we have

(97)

\left\lVert e^{-\int_{0}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}-(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

for some absolute constant
c
. We will take
M=3c\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}
.

Next, using (
95
), Lemma
2.2
and Corollary
1.6
, we may estimate the smooth remainder term involving
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{+}
as

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\,\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{+}[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]\,dt^{\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{0}^{t}c\,(t-t^{\prime})^{-(1-\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3})}\left\lVert\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{+}[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{0}^{t}(t-t^{\prime})^{-(1-\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3})}c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{3,\gamma}},\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\big(\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\big)\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq t^{\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3}}\,c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}},\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}},\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\big(\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}+\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq t^{\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3}}\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\,M\,.

In addition, we may estimate the smooth remainder term in
\mathcal{Y}_{1}
using

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\,\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{+}[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]\,dt^{\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{+}[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq t\,c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}},\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}},\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\big(\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}+\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\big)\leq t\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\,M\,.

Furthermore, for the small-in-
\epsilon
remainder term, we may use (
95
) and Lemma
2.3
along with Corollary
1.6
and Lemma
1.4
to estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\,\lambda^{-1}\mathcal{R}_{\epsilon}[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]\,dt^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\big(\epsilon+T^{1/4}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-3/4}+T\big)\,c\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\epsilon}[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,\big(\epsilon+T^{1/4}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-3/4}+T\big)\,c(\left\lVert\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}},\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\left\lVert\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,\big(\epsilon+T^{1/4}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-3/4}+T\big)\,c(M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\;\big(\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,M\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{2}\,c(M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\,M+\big(T^{1/4}+T\big)\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\,M\,.

In addition, by (
95
) and Lemma
2.2
, we have the
C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\,\lambda^{-1}\mathcal{R}_{\epsilon}[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]\,dt^{\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\epsilon}[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq t\,c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{Y}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}},\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}},\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\,\left\lVert\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\leq t\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\,M\,.

Finally, to estimate the
\mathcal{Y}_{1}
contribution from the initial condition, we consider the equation

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bm{Z}}{\partial t}(\sigma,t)=-\lambda(t)^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}\bm{Z}\,,\qquad\bm{Z}^{\rm in}=(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}(\sigma)\,.

Using the mapping properties of
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
(Lemma
1.4
), at each
t
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{t}\bm{Z}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c(\epsilon)\left\lVert\bm{Z}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c(\epsilon)\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Thus

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{Z}-(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}=\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\bm{Z}\,dt^{\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c(\epsilon)\,t\,\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,;

in particular, we have

(98)

\left\lVert e^{-\int_{0}^{t}\lambda^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}-(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\leq c(\epsilon)\,T\left\lVert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c(\epsilon)\,T\,M\,.

Altogether, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Lambda[\bm{Y}]\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{3}+\big(T^{\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3}}+T^{1/4}+T\big)\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})+\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{2}\,c(M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\bigg)M\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Lambda[\bm{Y}]\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq T\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\,M\,.

We first take
\epsilon
small enough that

(99)

\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{2}\,c(M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\leq\frac{1}{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\epsilon\leq\min\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\textstyle r_{*}(\frac{|\bm{X}^{\rm in}|_{\star}}{2},M)\,,\epsilon_{\rm n}(\frac{|\bm{X}^{\rm in}|_{\star}}{2},M)\bigg\}\,,

where
r_{*}
is as in (
6
) and
\epsilon_{\rm n}
is as in Theorem
1.5
.
Then, taking
T
small enough that

(100)

\displaystyle(T^{\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3}}+T^{1/4}+T)\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\leq\frac{1}{3}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle T\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\leq\min\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star},\frac{1}{2}\bigg\}\,,

we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Lambda[\bm{Y}]\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\leq M\,,\qquad\left\lVert\Lambda[\bm{Y}]\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\leq\min\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star},\frac{1}{2}\bigg\}\,.

We next show that the map
\Lambda[\bm{Y}]
admits a unique fixed point on
\mathcal{B}_{M,\frac{1}{2}}(T)
.
We now consider two nearby curves
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
, and define
\bm{Y}^{(a)}=\bm{X}^{(a)}-(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}
,
\bm{Y}^{(b)}=\bm{X}^{(b)}-(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}
.
Note that the lengths
\lambda^{(a)}
and
\lambda^{(b)}
of curve (a) and (b), respectively, satisfy

\displaystyle\lambda^{(a)}-\lambda^{(b)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\left\lvert\partial_{\sigma}\bm{Y}^{(a)}+\partial_{\sigma}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert-\left\lvert\partial_{\sigma}\bm{Y}^{(b)}+\partial_{\sigma}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert\bigg)\,d\sigma

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial_{\sigma}(\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)})\cdot(\partial_{\sigma}\bm{Y}^{(a)}+\partial_{\sigma}\bm{Y}^{(b)}+2\partial_{\sigma}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})}{|\partial_{\sigma}\bm{Y}^{(a)}+\partial_{\sigma}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}|+|\partial_{\sigma}\bm{Y}^{(b)}+\partial_{\sigma}(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}|}\,d\sigma\,;

in particular, in
\mathcal{B}_{M,\frac{1}{2}}(T)
, we have

(101)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\lambda^{(a)}-\lambda^{(b)}\right\rvert\leq 3\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{4}{9}\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\frac{1}{\lambda^{(a)}}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{(b)}}\right\rvert\leq 12\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Let

\displaystyle\bm{W}_{+}^{(j)}

\displaystyle=(\lambda^{(j)})^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{+}^{(j)}[\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}^{(j)}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]

\displaystyle\bm{W}_{\epsilon}^{(j)}

\displaystyle=(\lambda^{(j)})^{-1}\mathcal{R}_{\epsilon}^{(j)}[\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}^{(j)}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})]\,,\qquad j=a,b\,.

Using (
101
) and (
36
), we may estimate

(102)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{W}_{+}^{(a)}-\bm{W}_{+}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\beta}}\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}}\bigg)\leq c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\,.

Furthermore, using (
101
), (
36
), and Lemma
1.4
, we have

(103)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{W}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}-\bm{W}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,c(M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\beta}}\left\lVert\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}^{(a)}+(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in})\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}(\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)})\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{2}\,c(M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\,.

Now, integrating (
101
) in time, we have

(104)

\frac{4}{9}(t-t^{\prime})\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\leq\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{(a)}(\tau)}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{(b)}(\tau)}\bigg)\,d\tau\leq 12(t-t^{\prime})\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\,.

In particular, combining (
104
), (
102
), and Lemma
2.2
, we may estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}(\lambda^{(a)})^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\bm{W}_{+}^{(a)}-e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}(\lambda^{(b)})^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\bm{W}_{+}^{(b)}\bigg)\,dt^{\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{4,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{0}^{t}c\,(t-t^{\prime})^{-(1-\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3})}\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\left\lVert\bm{W}_{+}^{(a)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert\bm{W}_{+}^{(a)}-\bm{W}_{+}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\bigg)\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq t^{\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3}}\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\,\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\,.

Furthermore, combining (
104
), (
103
), and Lemma
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}(\lambda^{(a)})^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\bm{W}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}-e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}(\lambda^{(b)})^{-1}(\tau)d\tau\;\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{\sigma}^{4}}\bm{W}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\bigg)\,dt^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\big(\epsilon+T^{1/4}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-3/4}+T\big)\,c\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\left\lVert\bm{W}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}+\left\lVert\bm{W}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}-\bm{W}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\epsilon^{-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{2}\big(\epsilon+T^{1/4}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-3/4}+T\big)\,c(M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\,\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\,.

Altogether, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Lambda[\bm{Y}^{(a)}]-\Lambda[\bm{Y}^{(b)}]\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\big(T^{\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3}}+T^{1/4}+T\big)\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})

\displaystyle\qquad+\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{2}\,c(M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\bigg)\left\lVert\bm{Y}^{(a)}-\bm{Y}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{4}}\,.

Taking
\epsilon
small enough that
\epsilon^{1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{2}\,c(M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\leq\frac{1}{3}
in addition to (
99
), and then taking
T
small enough that
\big(T^{\frac{\gamma-\alpha}{3}}+T^{1/4}+T\big)\,c(\epsilon,M,\left\lvert(\bm{X}^{\sigma})^{\rm in}\right\rvert_{\star})\leq\frac{1}{3}
in addition to (
100
), we have that the map
\Lambda
admits a unique fixed point in
\mathcal{B}_{M,\frac{1}{2}}(T)
.
∎

3.
Mapping properties about the straight cylinder

This section is devoted to the proofs of Lemmas
1.4
and
1.9
regarding the mapping properties of the slender body NtD and DtN operators
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
and
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}
and the angle-averaged single layer operator
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}
about the straight filament
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
.

3.1.
Preliminaries

As in the Laplace setting
[
59
, section 2]
, we will consider the Hölder spaces
C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
and
C^{k,\alpha}_{s}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
as subsets of
C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
and
C^{k,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{R}\times 2\pi\mathbb{T})
and will rely on a Besov space characterization of
C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
and
C^{k,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{R}\times 2\pi\mathbb{T})
.

Following
[
5
, Chapter 2.2]
, we consider a smooth cutoff function
\phi(\xi)
supported on the annulus
\{\frac{3}{4}<\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<2\}
and satisfying
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\phi(2^{-j}\xi)=1
for
\xi\neq 0
. We define

(105)

\phi_{j}(\xi)=\phi(2^{-j}\xi)

and note that

\displaystyle{\rm supp}(\phi_{j})\cap{\rm supp}(\phi_{k})

\displaystyle=\emptyset\,,\qquad\left\lvert j-k\right\rvert\geq 2\,.

For a function
\bm{g}(s,\theta)
, we will use
\mathcal{F}[\bm{g}]
to denote the Fourier transform in
s
only:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\bm{g}](\xi,\theta)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bm{g}(s,\theta)\,e^{-2\pi i\xi s}\,ds\,.

We define the Littlewood-Paley projection in
s
,
P_{j}\bm{g}
, by

(106)

\mathcal{F}[P_{j}\bm{g}](\xi)=\phi_{j}(\xi)\mathcal{F}[\bm{g}](\xi,\theta)\,;

i.e. the projection of
\bm{g}
onto frequencies in the
s
-variable which are supported within annulus
j
. In addition, we will use the notation

\displaystyle P_{\leq j_{*}}:=\sum_{j\leq j_{*}}P_{j}\,.

Given
\nu\in\mathbb{R}
, we define the seminorm

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{\nu}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{j\nu}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Note that if
\bm{g}
is a function of
s
only, then this is just the Besov seminorm
\left\lvert\cdot\right\rvert_{\dot{B}^{\nu}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
.
We define the full norm

(107)

\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{(B^{\nu}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}=\sup\bigg(\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\,\sup_{j>0}2^{j\nu}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg)\,.

Letting
\lfloor\nu\rfloor
denote the integer part of
\nu
, it may be shown that the
(B^{\nu}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}
norm (
107
) is equivalent to the
C^{\lfloor\nu\rfloor,\nu-\lfloor\nu\rfloor}_{s}
Hölder norm (
46
). Furthermore, if
\bm{g}
is a function of
s
only, then
\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{(B^{\nu}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}=\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{B^{\nu}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
is equivalent to
\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{\lfloor\nu\rfloor,\nu-\lfloor\nu\rfloor}(\mathbb{R})}
.

Given a matrix-valued Fourier multiplier
\widehat{\bm{M}}(\xi)
, we denote

(108)

T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\widehat{\bm{M}}\,\mathcal{F}[\bm{g}]]\,,\qquad P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\phi_{j}\widehat{\bm{M}}\,\mathcal{F}[\bm{g}]]\,.

For
\phi_{j}
as in (
105
), we may define
\bm{M}_{j}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\phi_{j}\widehat{\bm{M}}]
so that we may write
P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g}=\bm{M}_{j}*\bm{g}
, where the convolution is with respect to
s
only:

\displaystyle\bm{M}_{j}*\bm{g}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bm{M}_{j}(s-s^{\prime})\,\bm{g}(s^{\prime},\theta)\,ds^{\prime}\,.

By Young’s inequality for convolutions, we may estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}=\left\lVert\bm{M}_{j}*\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left\lVert\bm{M}_{j}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Using the definition (
107
) of the
(B^{\nu}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}
norm and its equivalence with
C^{\lfloor\nu\rfloor,\nu-\lfloor\nu\rfloor}_{s}
, we may thus obtain the mapping properties of an operator
T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g}
by obtaining
L^{1}
bounds for the function
\bm{M}_{j}
in physical space. One way to prove such bounds is via the following lemma, which is just the matrix-valued version of
[
59
, Lemma 2.1]
.

Lemma 3.1

(Physical space
L^{1}
bounds for multipliers)
.

For fixed
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
\widehat{\bm{M}}_{j}(\xi)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
be a matrix-valued Fourier multiplier supported in the annulus
2^{j-1}<\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<2^{j+1}
and satisfying

(109)

\left\lvert\widehat{\bm{M}}_{j}\right\rvert\leq A\,,\qquad\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{2}\widehat{\bm{M}}_{j}\right\rvert\leq B

for some numbers
A
,
B
. Then
\bm{M}_{j}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\widehat{\bm{M}}_{j}]
satisfies the
L^{1}
bound

(110)

\left\lVert\bm{M}_{j}\right\rVert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim 2^{j}\sqrt{AB}\,.

The strategy for showing the mapping properties in Lemmas
1.4
and
1.9
will thus be to prove
L^{\infty}
bounds for the zeroth and second derivatives of the components of the Fourier multipliers corresponding to
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}
,
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}
, and
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}
.

3.2.
Bounds for SB DtN and NtD multipliers

We begin by considering the tangential and normal direction multipliers
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}
and
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}
corresponding to
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}
, given by Proposition
1.3
. Given the form of these multipliers, it will be essential to have bounds for ratios of second-kind modified Bessel functions
K_{0}
and
K_{1}
as well as their derivatives. We will make use of the following proposition.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
K_{0}(z)
and
K_{1}(z)
denote zeroth and first order modified Bessel functions of the second kind. The following bounds hold:

(111)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<\frac{K_{1}(z)}{K_{0}(z)}-1-\frac{1}{2z}+\frac{1}{8z^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{8z^{3}}\,,\qquad z\geq 1

(112)

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{11z^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{0}(z)}{K_{1}(z)}-1+\frac{1}{2z}-\frac{3}{8z^{2}}<0\,,\hskip 35.56593ptz\geq 1

(113)

\displaystyle\frac{c}{z\left\lvert\log(z/3)\right\rvert}

\displaystyle<\frac{K_{1}(z)}{K_{0}(z)}<\frac{c}{z\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\hskip 64.01869pt0<z<1

(114)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{z}\,,\hskip 93.89418pt\text{all }z>0\,.

A proof of the quantitative bounds (
111
) and (
112
) for
z\geq 1
appears below. These quantitative bounds will be useful for bounding the complicated functions (
23
), (
24
) of the ratios
\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}
and
\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}
at intermediate
z\sim 1
. For small
0<z<1
, such quantitative bounds are not necessary, and (
113
) follows from the well-known small-
z
asymptotics of
K_{0}(z)
and
K_{1}(z)

[
21
, Chapter 10]
. The general bound (
114
) is due to estimate (77) in
[
50
]
.

Noting that the first and second derivatives of the ratios in Proposition
3.2
are given by

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{1}^{2}}{K_{0}^{2}}-\frac{K_{1}}{zK_{0}}-1\,,\qquad\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}=2\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}+\frac{1}{z^{2}}\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{1}{z}\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{0}^{2}}{K_{1}^{2}}+\frac{K_{0}}{zK_{1}}-1\,,\qquad\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}=2\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}-\frac{1}{z^{2}}\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}+\frac{1}{z}\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}\,,

as an immediate consequence of Proposition
3.2
, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 3.3
.

For
z\geq 1
, the following bounds hold for derivatives of ratios of
K_{1}(z)
and
K_{0}(z)
:

(115)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2z^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{4z^{3}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{9}{2z^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2z^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{z^{3}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{25}{2z^{3}}\,.

For
0<z<1
, we have the bounds

(116)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lvert\log(z/3)\right\rvert\,,\qquad\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\left\lvert\log(z/3)\right\rvert\,.

Proof of Proposition
3.2
, estimates (
111
) and (
112
).

We first outline a general strategy for obtaining bounds of this form.
Consider a function
B(z)
satisfying the ODE

(117)

B^{\prime}(z)=F(z,B(z))\,,\qquad B(z_{0})=B_{0}

for some
F
. To obtain an upper bound on
B
, we seek
b(z)
satisfying

(118)

\begin{cases}b^{\prime}(z)<F(z,b(z))\quad\text{ for }z\geq z_{0}\geq 0\\
\exists z_{1}>z_{0}\text{ such that }b(z)>B(z)\text{ for }z\geq z_{1}\,.\end{cases}

Then
b(z)>B(z)
for all
z>z_{0}
.
Similarly, to obtain a lower bound for
B
, we seek
h(z)
satisfying

(119)

\begin{cases}h^{\prime}(z)>F(z,h(z))\quad\text{ for }z\geq z_{0}\geq 0\\
\exists z_{1}>z_{0}\text{ such that }h(z)<B(z)\text{ for }z\geq z_{1}\,.\end{cases}

Then
h(z)<B(z)
for all
z>z_{0}
.

Consider the function
B_{K}(z)=z\frac{K_{1}(z)}{K_{0}(z)}-z-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{8z}
. It can be seen that
B_{K}
satisfies the ODE

(120)

B_{K}^{\prime}(z)=\frac{1}{z}\bigg(B_{K}^{2}+B_{K}-\frac{1}{4z}B_{K}+\frac{1}{64z^{2}}-\frac{1}{4z}\bigg)+2B_{K}\,,\qquad B_{K}(1)=\frac{K_{1}(1)}{K_{0}(1)}-\frac{11}{8}\approx 0.055\,.

Let
b(z)=\frac{1}{8z^{2}}
. We have that

\displaystyle b^{\prime}(z)-\frac{1}{z}\bigg(b^{2}+b-\frac{1}{4z}b+\frac{1}{64z^{2}}-\frac{1}{4z}\bigg)-2b=-\frac{1}{64z^{5}}+\frac{1}{32z^{4}}-\frac{25}{64z^{3}}<0

for
z\geq 1
. Using the large-
z
asymptotics of
K_{0}(z)
and
K_{1}(z)

[
21
]
, we have

(121)

z\frac{K_{1}(z)}{K_{0}(z)}=z+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{8z}+\frac{1}{8z^{2}}-\frac{25}{128z^{3}}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{z^{4}}\bigg)\,,\quad z\to\infty\,.

In particular, for sufficiently large
z
, we have
B_{K}(z)<b(z)
. Thus by (
118
),
B_{K}(z)<b(z)
for all
z\geq 1
.

Now let
h(z)=0
. We have

\displaystyle h^{\prime}(z)-\frac{1}{z}\bigg(h^{2}+h-\frac{1}{4z}h+\frac{1}{64z^{2}}-\frac{1}{4z}\bigg)-2h=\frac{1}{4z^{2}}-\frac{1}{64z^{3}}>0

for
z\geq 1
. Again by the large-
z
asymptotics (
121
) for
B_{K}
, we have that for sufficiently large
z
,
B_{K}>0
. By (
119
), we thus have
B_{K}>0
for all
z\geq 1
.

Note that using (
111
) we immediately obtain the bound (
112
). In particular, we have

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{2z}+\frac{3}{8z^{2}}-\frac{4}{11z^{3}}\leq\frac{1}{1+\frac{1}{2z}-\frac{1}{8z^{2}}+\frac{1}{8z^{3}}}<\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{1+\frac{1}{2z}-\frac{1}{8z^{2}}}\leq 1-\frac{1}{2z}+\frac{3}{8z^{2}}\,.

∎

We may use the bounds of Proposition
3.2
and Corollary
3.3
to obtain the following estimates for the tangential direction multiplier
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}
given by Proposition
1.3
.

Lemma 3.4

(Bounds for
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}
and
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}
)
.

Let
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}(\xi)
be as in Proposition
1.3
. For
\ell=0,1,2
, the following bounds hold:

(122)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}(\xi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{1-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\\
c\,\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-1}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

Furthermore, we have that
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}(\xi)=\frac{1}{m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}(\xi)}
satisfies

(123)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}(\xi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell-1}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\\
c\,\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

Consider the function

(124)

\displaystyle m_{\rm t}^{-1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{\rm t1}(z)}{m_{\rm t2}(z)}\,;

\displaystyle m_{\rm t1}(z):=z\frac{K_{1}(z)}{K_{0}(z)}\,,

\displaystyle m_{\rm t2}(z):=1+\frac{z}{2}\bigg(\frac{K_{0}(z)}{K_{1}(z)}-\frac{K_{1}(z)}{K_{0}(z)}\bigg)

and note that
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}(\xi)=2\pi\,m_{\rm t}^{-1}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
for
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}(\xi)
as in Proposition
1.3
.
We will additionally consider

(125)

m_{\rm t}(z)=\frac{1}{m_{\rm t}^{-1}(z)}=\frac{m_{\rm t2}(z)}{m_{\rm t1}(z)}\,.

The first and second derivatives of
m_{\rm t}^{-1}(z)
and
m_{\rm t}(z)
satisfy

(126)

\displaystyle(m_{\rm t}^{-1})^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{\rm t1}^{\prime}m_{\rm t2}-m_{\rm t1}m_{\rm t2}^{\prime}}{m_{\rm t2}^{2}}\,,\qquad(m_{\rm t}^{-1})^{\prime\prime}(z)=\frac{m_{\rm t2}m_{\rm t1}^{\prime\prime}-m_{\rm t1}m_{\rm t2}^{\prime\prime}}{m_{\rm t2}^{2}}+2\frac{m_{\rm t2}^{\prime}}{m_{\rm t2}}(m_{\rm t}^{-1})^{\prime}

\displaystyle m_{\rm t}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{\rm t2}^{\prime}m_{\rm t1}-m_{\rm t2}m_{\rm t1}^{\prime}}{m_{\rm t1}^{2}}\,,\qquad\qquad m_{\rm t}^{\prime\prime}(z)=\frac{m_{\rm t1}m_{\rm t2}^{\prime\prime}-m_{\rm t2}m_{\rm t1}^{\prime\prime}}{m_{\rm t1}^{2}}+2\frac{m_{\rm t1}^{\prime}}{m_{\rm t1}}\,m_{\rm t}^{\prime}\,.

From Proposition
3.2
, for
z\geq 1
we have

(127)

\left\lvert m_{\rm t1}-z-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{8z}\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{8z^{2}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm t2}-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{8z}\right\rvert\leq\frac{5}{16z^{2}}\,.

Furthermore, using Corollary
3.3
, for
z\geq 1
the first and second derivatives of
m_{\rm tA}
and
m_{\rm tB}
satisfy

(128)

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t1}^{\prime}-1\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}+z\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}-1\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{z^{2}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t2}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)+\frac{z}{2}\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{7}{4z^{2}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t1}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert 2\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}+z\bigg(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{7}{z^{2}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t2}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}+\frac{z}{2}\bigg(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{13}{z^{2}}\,.

Using (
127
) and (
128
) in the expressions (
124
), (
125
), and (
126
), we obtain the following bounds for
m_{\rm t}^{-1}
and
m_{\rm t}
when
z\geq 1
:

(129)

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t}^{-1}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,z\,,\qquad\left\lvert(m_{\rm t}^{-1})^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert(m_{\rm t}^{-1})^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm t}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm t}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}\,.

For
0<z<1
, we may use the bounds (
113
) and (
114
) to obtain

(130)

\displaystyle\frac{c}{\left\lvert\log(z/3)\right\rvert}\leq m_{\rm t1}(z)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\frac{1}{2}\leq m_{\rm t2}(z)\leq 1

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t1}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm t2}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t1}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm t2}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,.

Using (
130
) in the expressions (
124
), (
125
), and (
126
), for
0<z<1
we obtain the bounds

(131)

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t}^{-1}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\left\lvert(m_{\rm t}^{-1})^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\left\lvert(m_{\rm t}^{-1})^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm t}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm t}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm t}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}{z^{2}}\,.

Combining the
z\geq 1
bounds (
129
) with the
0<z<1
bounds (
131
) and using that
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}(\xi)=2\pi\,m_{\rm t}^{-1}(2\pi\epsilon\,\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
, we obtain Lemma
3.4
.
∎

We next consider the normal direction multiplier
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}
given by Proposition
1.3
and use the bounds of Proposition
3.2
and Corollary
3.3
to obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 3.5

(Bounds for
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}
and
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}
)
.

Let
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}(\xi)
be as in Proposition
1.3
. For
\ell=0,1,2
, the following bounds hold:

(132)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}(\xi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{1-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\\
c\,\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-1}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

Furthermore, we have that
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}(\xi)=\frac{1}{m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}(\xi)}
satisfies

(133)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}(\xi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell-1}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\\
c\,\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
3.5
.

We consider the function

(134)

\displaystyle m_{\rm n}^{-1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{\rm n1}(z)}{m_{\rm n2}(z)}\,,

\displaystyle m_{\rm n1}(z)

\displaystyle=2+\frac{8}{z}\frac{K_{1}(z)}{K_{0}(z)}+z\bigg(\frac{K_{1}(z)}{K_{0}(z)}-\frac{K_{0}(z)}{K_{1}(z)}\bigg)\,,

\displaystyle m_{\rm n2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z^{2}}+2\bigg(1-\frac{K_{0}^{2}(z)}{K_{1}^{2}(z)}\bigg)+\frac{2}{z}\bigg(\frac{K_{1}(z)}{K_{0}(z)}-\frac{K_{0}(z)}{K_{1}(z)}\bigg)\,.

Using the Bessel function identity

(135)

K_{2}(z)=K_{0}(z)+\frac{2}{z}K_{1}(z)\,,

it can be seen that in fact
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}(\xi)=2\pi\,m_{\rm n}^{-1}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
where
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}(\xi)
is given by Proposition
1.3
. In addition, we define

(136)

m_{\rm n}(z)=\frac{1}{m_{\rm n}^{-1}(z)}=\frac{m_{\rm n2}(z)}{m_{\rm n1}(z)}\,.

The first and second derivatives of
m_{\rm n}^{-1}
and
m_{\rm n}
then satisfy

(137)

\displaystyle(m_{\rm n}^{-1})^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{\rm n1}^{\prime}m_{\rm n2}-m_{\rm n1}m_{\rm n2}^{\prime}}{m_{\rm n2}^{2}}\,,\qquad(m_{\rm n}^{-1})^{\prime\prime}(z)=\frac{m_{\rm n2}m_{\rm n1}^{\prime\prime}-m_{\rm n1}m_{\rm n2}^{\prime\prime}}{m_{\rm n2}^{2}}+2\frac{m_{\rm n2}^{\prime}}{m_{\rm n2}}(m_{\rm n}^{-1})^{\prime}

\displaystyle m_{\rm n}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{\rm n2}^{\prime}m_{\rm n1}-m_{\rm n2}m_{\rm n1}^{\prime}}{m_{\rm n1}^{2}}\,,\qquad\qquad m_{\rm n}^{\prime\prime}(z)=\frac{m_{\rm n1}m_{\rm n2}^{\prime\prime}-m_{\rm n2}m_{\rm n1}^{\prime\prime}}{m_{\rm n1}^{2}}+2\frac{m_{\rm n1}^{\prime}}{m_{\rm n1}}\,m_{\rm n}^{\prime}\,.

Using Proposition
3.2
and Corollary
3.3
, for
z\geq 1
we may obtain the following bounds for the components
m_{\rm nA}
and
m_{\rm nB}
:

(138)

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n1}-3-\frac{15}{2z}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{8}{z}\left(1+\frac{1}{2z}-\frac{1}{8z^{2}}+\frac{1}{8z^{3}}\right)-\frac{8}{z}\right\rvert+\left\lvert z\left(\frac{1}{z}-\frac{1}{2z^{2}}+\frac{5}{8z^{3}}\right)-1+\frac{1}{2z}\right\rvert\leq\displaystyle\frac{7}{z^{2}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n2}-\frac{2}{z}-\frac{4}{z^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\textstyle\left\lvert\left(\frac{1}{z}-\frac{3}{4z^{2}}\right)\left(2-\frac{1}{2z}+\frac{3}{8z^{2}}\right)-\frac{2}{z}+\frac{2}{z^{2}}\right\rvert+\frac{1}{z^{3}}+\frac{5}{4z^{4}}\leq\displaystyle\frac{3}{z^{3}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n1}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lvert-\frac{8}{z^{2}}\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}+\frac{8}{z}\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}+\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)+z\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq\displaystyle\frac{32}{z}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n2}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lvert-\frac{8}{z^{3}}-4\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{2}{z^{2}}\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)+\frac{2}{z}\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq\displaystyle\frac{47}{z^{2}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n1}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\bigg|\frac{16}{z^{3}}\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{16}{z^{2}}\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}+\frac{8}{z}\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime\prime}+2\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime}+z\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{103}{z^{2}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n2}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\bigg|\frac{24}{z^{4}}-4\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime\;2}-4\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}+\frac{4}{z^{3}}\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle-\frac{4}{z^{2}}\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime}+\frac{2}{z}\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{222}{z^{3}}\,.

In particular, using (
138
) in (
137
), for
z\geq 1
we have

(139)

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n}^{-1}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,z\,,\qquad\left\lvert(m_{\rm n}^{-1})^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert(m_{\rm n}^{-1})^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm n}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm n}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}\,.

For
0<z<1
, using Proposition
3.2
, Corollary
3.3
, and the forms of the derivatives in (
138
), we may calculate

(140)

\displaystyle\frac{c}{z^{2}\left\lvert\log(z/3)\right\rvert}\leq\left\lvert m_{\rm n1}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\frac{4}{z^{2}}\leq\left\lvert m_{\rm n2}\right\rvert\leq\frac{8}{z^{2}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n1}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm n2}^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n1}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{4}\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm n2}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{4}}\,.

Using (
140
) with the forms of
m_{\rm n}^{-1}(z)
,
m_{\rm n}(z)
, and their derivatives (
137
), we obtain

(141)

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n}^{-1}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\left\lvert(m_{\rm n}^{-1})^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}\,,\qquad\left\lvert(m_{\rm n}^{-1})^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm n}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm n}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm n}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}{z^{2}}\,.

Again combining the
z\geq 1
bounds (
139
) with the
0<z<1
bounds (
141
) and using that
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}(\xi)=2\pi\,m_{\rm n}^{-1}(2\pi\epsilon\,\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
, we obtain Lemma
3.5
.
∎

3.3.
Bounds for single layer multipliers

We now turn to the angle-averaged inverse single layer operator
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}
, defined in (
56
), and its corresponding multipliers, given by Corollary
1.8
. To bound these multipliers, in addition to Proposition
3.2
and Corollary
3.3
