, we will need bounds on ratios of first-kind modified Bessel functions
I_{0}
and
I_{1}
.

We first make note of a useful identity that is a consequence of a more general property of the Wronskian between first and second kind modified Bessel functions
[
21
]
. In particular, first- and second-order modified Bessel functions of the first and second kind may be related via the following:

(142)

K_{1}(z)I_{0}(z)+K_{0}(z)I_{1}(z)=\frac{1}{z}\,,\qquad z>0\,.

In addition, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
I_{0}(z)
and
I_{1}(z)
denote zeroth and first order modified Bessel functions of the first kind. The following bounds each hold for some
c>0
:

(143)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-1+\frac{1}{2z}+\frac{1}{8z^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}\,,\qquad z\geq 1

(144)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-1-\frac{1}{2z}-\frac{3}{8z^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}\,,\qquad z\geq 1

(145)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{z}{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq cz^{3}\,,\qquad 0<z<1

(146)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{2}{z}-\frac{z}{4}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq cz^{3}\,,\qquad 0<z<1\,.

Each of the bounds in Proposition
3.6
may be seen immediately using the large and small-
z
asymptotics of
I_{0}(z)
and
I_{1}(z)
; in particular, from
[
21
, Chap. 10]
, we have

(147)

\displaystyle I_{0}(z)

\displaystyle\sim\frac{e^{z}}{\sqrt{2\pi z}}\big(1+\frac{1}{8z}+\frac{9}{128z^{2}}+\frac{75}{124z^{3}}+\cdots\big)\,,

\displaystyle I_{1}(z)

\displaystyle\sim\frac{e^{z}}{\sqrt{2\pi z}}\big(1-\frac{3}{8z}-\frac{15}{128z^{2}}-\frac{105}{1025z^{3}}-\cdots\big)\,,\quad z\to\infty\,;

\displaystyle I_{0}(z)

\displaystyle\sim 1+\frac{z^{2}}{4}+\frac{z^{4}}{64}+\cdots\,,\quad I_{1}(z)\sim\frac{z}{2}+\frac{z^{3}}{16}+\cdots\,,\quad z\to 0\,.

Note that the bounds (
143
) and (
144
) are stated with an unknown constant
c
, unlike in Proposition
3.2
. Because of this, we will need an additional refinement in order to prove all of the bounds we will eventually need (see Proposition
3.8
). Before stating and proving this refinement, however, we note an immediate corollary of Proposition
3.6
.

Calculating the first and second derivatives of the quantities in Proposition
3.6
yields

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{I_{1}^{2}}{I_{0}^{2}}-\frac{I_{1}}{zI_{0}}+1\,,\qquad\bigg(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}=-2\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\bigg(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}+\frac{1}{z^{2}}\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{1}{z}\bigg(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{I_{0}^{2}}{I_{1}^{2}}+\frac{I_{0}}{zI_{1}}+1\,,\qquad\bigg(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}=-2\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\bigg(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}-\frac{1}{z^{2}}\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}+\frac{1}{z}\bigg(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}\,,

from which we may again immediately obtain the following.

Corollary 3.7
.

For
z\geq 1
, the following bounds hold for derivatives of ratios of
I_{1}(z)
and
I_{0}(z)
:

(148)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2z^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2z^{2}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}\,.

For
0<z<1
, we have the bounds

(149)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,z^{2}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq c\,z

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime}+\frac{2}{z^{2}}-\frac{1}{4}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,z^{2}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\bigg(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\bigg)^{\prime\prime}-\frac{4}{z^{3}}\right\rvert\leq c\,z\,.

Since we do not have precise values for the constants
c
appearing in (
143
) and (
144
), we will additionally need the following refinement.
Using the small-
z
asymptotics (
145
) and (
146
), note that we may choose
0<\delta<\frac{1}{4}
small enough that

(150)

\left\lvert\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}-\frac{z}{2}\right\rvert\leq\frac{z^{2}}{8}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lvert\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}-\frac{2}{z}-\frac{z}{4}\right\rvert\leq\frac{z^{2}}{8}\,,\qquad 0<z\leq\delta\,.

With this choice of
\delta
, we may show the following proposition.

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
\delta
be as in (
150
). For
z\geq\delta
, the following bound holds between the difference of ratios of first kind modified Bessel functions:

(151)

\frac{1}{z}\leq\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}-\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\leq\frac{c_{\delta}}{z}\,,\qquad c_{\delta}<2.

In addition, for
z\geq\delta
, we may show that the following function is bounded away from zero:

(152)

\frac{2}{z}+\bigg(1-z\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}-\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\bigg)<-c

for some
c>0
.

Proof.

We consider the function

(153)

B(z):=z\bigg(\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}-\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\bigg)\,,

which satisfies the ODE

\displaystyle B^{\prime}(z)=-B(z)\bigg(\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}+\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\bigg)+2\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}

with initial condition
B(\delta)=\delta\frac{I_{0}(\delta)}{I_{1}(\delta)}-\frac{I_{1}(\delta)}{I_{0}(\delta)}
. Using the bounds (
150
), we have that

(154)

2-\frac{\delta^{2}}{4}(1+\delta)\leq B(\delta)\leq 2-\frac{\delta^{2}}{4}(1-\delta)\,.

Noting that
I_{0}(z)>I_{1}(z)
for all
z

[
21
]
, we have

\displaystyle B^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle\leq\big(-B(z)+2\big)\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}\,,

\displaystyle B^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle\geq\big(-B(z)+1\big)\bigg(\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}+\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\bigg)\,.

Let
b(z)
be the solution to the ODE

\displaystyle b^{\prime}(z)=\big(-b(z)+2\big)\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}\,,\qquad b(\delta)=B(\delta)\,.

Defining
s=\log(zI_{1}(z))
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{db}{ds}=-b(s)+2\,,\qquad b(s(\delta))=B(\delta)\,,

which we can integrate directly to yield
b(s)=2+(B(\delta)-2)e^{-(s-s(\delta))}
, or

(155)

b(s(z))=2+(B(\delta)-2)\frac{\delta I_{1}(\delta)}{zI_{1}(z)}\,.

Using the large-
z
asymptotics (
143
) and (
144
) of
\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}
and
\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}
, we note that there exists
\eta>1
such that

(156)

B(z)\leq\frac{3}{2}\,,\qquad z\geq\eta\,.

Meanwhile,
b(s(z))\to 2
as
z\to\infty
, so for
z
sufficiently large we have
b(s(z))>B(z)
. Thus by (
118
), we have
B(z)\leq b(s(z))
for
z\geq\delta
. In particular, for
\delta\leq z\leq\eta
, we have

(157)

B(z)\leq b(s(\eta))\leq 2-\frac{\delta}{8}\frac{\delta I_{1}(\delta)}{\eta I_{1}(\eta)}<2\,.

Combining (
157
) and (
156
), we obtain the upper bound of (
151
).

Similarly, let
h(z)
be the solution to the ODE

\displaystyle h^{\prime}(z)=\big(-h(z)+1\big)\bigg(\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}+\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\bigg)\,,\qquad h(\delta)=B(\delta)\,.

Defining
t=\log(zI_{1}(z)I_{0}(z))
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{dh}{dt}=-h(t)+1\,,\qquad h(t(\delta))=B(\delta)\,.

This can again be directly integrated to yield
h(t)=1+(B(\delta)-1)e^{-(t-t(\delta))}
, or

(158)

h(t(z))=1+(B(\delta)-1)\frac{\delta I_{0}(\delta)I_{1}(\delta)}{zI_{0}(z)I_{1}(z)}\,.

Since both
I_{1}(z)
and
I_{0}(z)
grow exponentially in
z

[
21
]
, for sufficiently large
z
, we have
h(t(z))<B(z)
. Thus by (
119
), we have

(159)

B(z)\geq h(t(z))\geq 1\,,\qquad z\geq\delta\,.

This yields the lower bound of (
151
).

To show the second bound (
152
), we let

\displaystyle G(z)=\frac{2}{z}+\bigg(1-z\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}-\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\bigg)\,.

For
z\geq 3
, since
\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}>1
for all
z
, we may use (
151
) to immediately obtain

(160)

G(z)\leq\frac{2}{z}+\bigg(1-z\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}\bigg)\frac{1}{z}=\frac{3}{z}-\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}\leq-c<0\,.

For
\delta\leq z\leq 3
, it suffices to show that
H(z)=zG(z)
satisfies
H(z)\leq-c
for some
c>0
. Writing

\displaystyle H(z)=2+\bigg(1-z\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}\bigg)B(z)

for
B(z)
as in (
153
), we may calculate that
H(z)
satisfies

\displaystyle H^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\bigg(-H(z)+\frac{B(z)(2-\frac{B(z)}{2})-2}{B(z)-1}\bigg)\frac{B(z)-1}{B(z)}\bigg(2\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}+\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\bigg(1-\frac{B(z)}{2}\bigg)-z(B(z)-1)\,.

Noting that
B(z)\leq c_{\delta}<2
implies
B(z)(2-\frac{B(z)}{2})\leq c<2
, we have
\frac{B(z)(2-\frac{B(z)}{2})-2}{B(z)-1}\leq-c_{B}
for some constant
c_{B}>0
. In particular,

\displaystyle H^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle\leq\big(-H(z)-c_{B}\big)\frac{B(z)-1}{B(z)}\bigg(2\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}+\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\bigg)\,.

Now, using the bounds (
150
), for
0<z\leq\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert H(z)+\frac{z^{2}}{4}\right\rvert\leq\frac{3z^{3}}{4}\,.

In particular,
H(\delta)\leq-\frac{\delta^{2}}{16}<0
, since
\delta\leq\frac{1}{4}
.
Letting
s(z)
be such that
s^{\prime}(z)=\frac{B(z)-1}{B(z)}\big(2\frac{I_{0}(z)}{I_{1}(z)}+\frac{I_{1}(z)}{I_{0}(z)}\big)>0
for all
z
, we define
w
to be the solution to the ODE

\displaystyle\frac{dw}{ds}=-w(s)-c_{B}\,,\qquad w(s(\delta))=H(\delta)\,.

We may solve for
w
explicitly as
w(s)=-c_{B}+(c_{B}+H(\delta))e^{-s-s(\delta)}
. Using the large-
z
asymptotics (
143
) and (
144
) of
\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}
and
\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}
, along with (
118
), we thus have
H(z)\leq w(s(z))\leq-c<0
for all
z\geq\delta
; in particular, for
\delta\leq z\leq 3
. Combined with the bound (
160
) for
z\geq 3
, we obtain the bound (
152
).
∎

Using Propositions
3.6
,
3.8
, and Corollary
3.7
, we may obtain bounds for the tangential multipliers
m_{\epsilon,z}^{z}
and
m_{\epsilon,r}^{z}
for the angle-averaged single layer operator
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}
, defined in Corollary
1.8
.

Lemma 3.9

(Bounds for
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}
multipliers, tangential direction)
.

Consider the multipliers
m_{\epsilon,z}^{z}(\xi)
and
m_{\epsilon,r}^{z}(\xi)
given by (
58
) in Corollary
1.8
.
For
\ell=0,1,2
, these multipliers satisfy:

(161)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}m_{\epsilon,z}^{z}(\xi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{1-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\\
c\,\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,;\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}m_{\epsilon,r}^{z}(\xi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\;\ell=0\,,\\
c\,\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-1}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\;\ell=1,2\,,\\
c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-1-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

We begin by defining

\displaystyle m_{{\rm tA}}(z)=\frac{Q_{\rm tA}(z)}{Q_{\rm tD}(z)}\,,\quad m_{{\rm tB}}(z)=\frac{Q_{\rm tB}(z)}{Q_{\rm tD}(z)}

where
Q_{{\rm t}j}
are as in (
50
) of Lemma
1.7
. Note that
m_{{\rm tA}}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)=\frac{1}{2}m_{\epsilon,z}^{z}(\xi)
and
m_{{\rm tB}}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)=\frac{i}{2}m_{\epsilon,r}(\xi)
.
For both
j={\rm A,B}
, we have

(162)

m_{{\rm t}j}^{\prime}(z)=\frac{Q_{{\rm t}j}^{\prime}Q_{\rm tD}-Q_{{\rm t}j}Q_{\rm tD}^{\prime}}{Q_{\rm tD}^{2}}\,,\qquad m_{{\rm t}j}^{\prime\prime}(z)=\frac{Q_{\rm tD}Q_{{\rm t}j}^{\prime\prime}-Q_{{\rm t}j}Q_{\rm tD}^{\prime\prime}}{Q_{\rm tD}^{2}}+2\frac{Q_{\rm tD}^{\prime}}{Q_{\rm tD}}\,m_{{\rm t}j}^{\prime}\,.

It thus remains to bound each of
Q_{{\rm tA}}
,
Q_{{\rm tB}}
,
Q_{{\rm tD}}
, and their derivatives.

We begin with
Q_{\rm tD}
. Using Proposition
3.8
and Proposition
3.2
, we may obtain a lower bound for
Q_{\rm tD}(z)
when
z\geq 1
: in particular,

(163)

Q_{\rm tD}(z)=\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\right)\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\right)\geq c>0\,,\qquad z\geq 1\,.

Furthermore, using Propositions
3.2
and
3.6
, for
z\geq 1
we have

(164)

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm tD}(z)-\frac{1}{4}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm tD}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\bigg|\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z^{2}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm tD}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\bigg|\left(\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}+z\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\right)\left(\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,.

In addition, for
0<z<1
, using cancellations in the asymptotics (
146
), (
149
) for
\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}
, we have

(165)

\frac{z^{2}}{c}\leq\left\lvert Q_{\rm tD}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,z^{2}\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm tD}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,z\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm tD}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,.

For
Q_{\rm tA}(z)
and
Q_{\rm tB}(z)
, we first note that, using the identity (
142
), we may write

(166)

\frac{1}{I_{0}K_{0}}=z\bigg(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\bigg)\,,\qquad\frac{1}{I_{1}K_{1}}=z\bigg(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\bigg)\,.

For
z\geq 1
, we may then use Propositions
3.6
and
3.2
to obtain

(167)

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm tA}(z)-z\right\rvert=\textstyle\left\lvert z\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\right)-z\right\rvert\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z}

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm tA}^{\prime}(z)-1\right\rvert=\textstyle\bigg|\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\right)+z\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+z\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)-1\bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z}

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm tA}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert=\textstyle\bigg|2\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\right)+2\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+z\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\left(1+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\right)+2z\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+z\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\left(\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}+\frac{z}{2}\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z}\,.

Similarly, for
0<z<1
, using the small-
z
asymptotics of Proposition
3.6
and the bounds of Proposition
3.2
, we have

(168)

\left\lvert Q_{\rm tA}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,z^{2}\,,\quad\left\lvert Q_{\rm tA}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,z\,,\quad\left\lvert Q_{\rm tA}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,.

For
Q_{\rm tB}
, again using (
166
) and Propositions
3.6
and
3.2
, for
z\geq 1
we have

(169)

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm tB}(z)-\frac{1}{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{z^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\right\rvert\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z}

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm tB}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lvert z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)+\frac{z^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z}

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm tB}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\textstyle\bigg|\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)+2z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\textstyle\frac{z^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z}\,.

For
0<z<1
, we have

(170)

\left\lvert Q_{\rm tB}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,z\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm tB}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm tB}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,.

Using the lower and upper bounds (
163
) and (
164
) for
Q_{\rm tD}
, along with the bounds (
167
) and (
169
) for
Q_{\rm tA}
and
Q_{\rm tB}
, for
z\geq 1
the formulas (
162
) yield

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm tA}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,z\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm tA}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm tA}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,;

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm tB}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm tB}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm tB}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,.

Similarly, using (
165
) with each of (
168
), (
170
) in the formulas (
162
), for
0<z<1
we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm tA}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm tA}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm tA}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,;

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\rm tB}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm tB}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,,\qquad\left\lvert m_{\rm tB}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{3}}\,.

Using that
m_{{\rm tA}}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)=\frac{1}{2}m_{\epsilon,z}^{z}(\xi)
and
m_{{\rm tB}}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)=\frac{i}{2}m_{\epsilon,r}(\xi)
, we obtain Lemma
3.9
.
∎

We next consider the normal direction multipliers
m_{\epsilon,r}^{x}
,
m_{\epsilon,\theta}^{x}
,
m_{\epsilon,z}^{x}
for the angle-averaged single layer operator
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}
, defined in Corollary
1.8
. Using Propositions
3.2
,
3.6
,
3.8
, and Corollaries
3.3
and
3.7
, we may obtain the following bounds for the normal direction multipliers and their derivatives.

Lemma 3.10

(Bounds for
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}
multipliers, normal direction)
.

Consider the multipliers
m_{\epsilon,r}^{x}(\xi)
,
m_{\epsilon,\theta}^{x}(\xi)
, and
m_{\epsilon,z}^{x}(\xi)
given by (
59
) in Corollary
1.8
. For
\ell=0,1,2
, these multipliers satisfy:

(171)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}m_{\epsilon,r}^{x}(\xi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{1-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\\
c\,\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,;\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}m_{\epsilon,\theta}^{x}(\xi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{1-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\\
c\,\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,;\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}m_{\epsilon,z}^{x}(\xi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert\geq\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,,\\
c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{1-\ell}\,,&\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert<\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

We begin by defining

(172)

m_{r}^{x}(z)=\frac{Q_{\rm nA}(z)-Q_{\rm nB}(z)}{Q_{\rm nG}(z)}\,,\quad m_{\theta}^{x}(z)=\frac{Q_{\rm nB}(z)-Q_{\rm nD}(z)}{Q_{\rm nG}(z)}\,,\quad m_{z}^{x}(z)=\frac{Q_{\rm nC}(z)+Q_{\rm nE}(z)}{Q_{\rm nG}(z)}\,,

where the functions
Q_{{\rm n}j}(z)
are as in (
55
). Note that
m_{\epsilon,r}^{x}(\xi)=m_{r}^{x}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
,
m_{\epsilon,\theta}^{x}(\xi)=m_{\theta}^{x}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
, and
m_{\epsilon,z}^{x}(\xi)=i\,m_{z}^{x}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
for the expressions (
59
) in Corollary
1.8
.

The first and second derivatives of
m_{r}^{x}
are given by

(173)

\displaystyle(m_{r}^{x})^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(Q_{\rm nA}^{\prime}-Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime})Q_{\rm nG}-(Q_{\rm nA}-Q_{\rm nB})Q_{\rm nG}^{\prime}}{Q_{\rm nG}^{2}}

\displaystyle(m_{r}^{x})^{\prime\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(Q_{\rm nA}^{\prime\prime}-Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime\prime})Q_{\rm nG}-(Q_{\rm nA}-Q_{\rm nB})Q_{\rm nG}^{\prime\prime}}{Q_{\rm nG}^{2}}+2\frac{Q_{\rm nG}^{\prime}}{Q_{\rm nG}}(Q_{\rm nA}^{\prime}-Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime})\,,

and the first and second derivatives of
m_{\theta}^{x}
and
m_{z}^{x}
may be written analogously. It thus remains to obtain bounds for the numerators and denominator of each multiplier.

We begin with bounds for
Q_{\rm nG}
. Using Proposition
3.8
and Proposition
3.2
, we may obtain the following lower bound for
\left\lvert Q_{\rm nG}(z)\right\rvert
when
z\geq 1
:

(174)

\left\lvert Q_{\rm nG}(z)\right\rvert=\left\lvert\textstyle\left(\frac{2}{z}+\left(1-z\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)\right)\left(\frac{2}{z}-\left(1+z\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\right)\right\rvert\geq c>0\,.

In addition, by Propositions
3.6
and
3.2
, for
z\geq 1
we have the upper bounds

(175)

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nG}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nG}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg|\textstyle\left(\frac{2}{z^{2}}+\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}+z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)-\left(1-z\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\times

\displaystyle\times\textstyle\left(\frac{2}{z}-\left(1+z\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\right)+\textstyle\left(\frac{2}{z}+\left(1-z\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)\right)\times

\displaystyle\times\textstyle\left(\frac{2}{z^{2}}+\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}+z\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)+\left(1+z\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z}

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nG}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg|\textstyle\bigg(-\frac{4}{z^{3}}+\left(2\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}+\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)+2\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}+z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)^{\prime}

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle-\left(1-z\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\bigg)\textstyle\left(\frac{2}{z}-\left(1+z\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\textstyle\left(\frac{2}{z}+\left(1-z\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)\right)\textstyle\bigg(-\frac{4}{z^{3}}+\left(2\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime}+\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle+\textstyle 2\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}+z\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}+\left(1+z\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\textstyle 2\left(\frac{2}{z^{2}}+\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}+z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)-\left(1-z\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\times

\displaystyle\times\textstyle\left(\frac{2}{z^{2}}+\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}+z\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)+\left(1+z\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)^{\prime}\right)\bigg|\leq\displaystyle\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,.

For
0<z<1
, we may use cancellations in the small-
z
asymptotics (
146
) for
\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}
along with the small-
z
bounds of Propositions
3.2
and
3.6
to obtain

(176)

\frac{1}{c}\leq\left\lvert Q_{\rm nG}(z)\right\rvert\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nG}^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nG}^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,,\qquad 0<z<1\,.

We next consider the numerator of
m_{r}^{x}(z)
in (
172
). Using the form (
55
) of
Q_{\rm nA}
and
Q_{\rm nB}
, and using (
166
) to rewrite
\frac{1}{I_{1}K_{1}}
, for
z\geq 1
we may use Propositions
3.2
,
3.6
and Corollaries
3.3
,
3.7
to obtain:

(177)

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nA}-Q_{\rm nB}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg|\textstyle z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\bigg(6+\left(\frac{8}{z^{2}}+2\right)\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}+\frac{4}{z}\left(\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle-2z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)-4\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}+\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\bigg)\bigg|\leq c\,z\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nA}^{\prime}-Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nA}^{\prime\prime}-Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,.

We have suppressed the full expressions for the first and second derivatives above, but note that they can easily be computed from the first expression.

For
0<z<1
, we may use cancellations in the small-
z
asymptotics (
146
), (
145
) of
\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}
and
\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}
along with Proposition (
3.2
) to obtain

(178)

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nA}-Q_{\rm nB}\right\rvert\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nA}^{\prime}-Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nA}^{\prime\prime}-Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,.

Combining (
174
), (
175
), (
176
), (
177
), and (
178
) with the definition (
172
) of
m_{r}^{x}(z)
and the expression (
173
) of its derivatives, we obtain

(179)

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{r}^{x}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,z\,,&z\geq 1\\
c\,,&0<z<1\,,\end{cases}\qquad\left\lvert(m_{r}^{x})^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\begin{cases}c\,,&z\geq 1\\
\frac{c}{z}\,,&0<z<1\,,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lvert(m_{r}^{x})^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\frac{c}{z}\,,&z\geq 1\\
\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,,&0<z<1\,.\end{cases}

We now turn to the numerator of
m_{\theta}^{x}(z)
from (
172
). We recall the form (
55
) of
Q_{\rm nB}
and
Q_{\rm nD}
and again use (
166
) to rewrite
\frac{1}{I_{1}K_{1}}
. By Propositions
3.2
,
3.6
and Corollaries
3.3
,
3.7
, for
z\geq 1
we have

(180)

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nB}-Q_{\rm nD}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg|\textstyle z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\bigg(4\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-6\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}+4\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-2+\frac{4}{z}\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle-\frac{8}{z^{2}}\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}-z^{2}\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\bigg)\bigg|\leq c\,z\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime}-Q_{\rm nD}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime\prime}-Q_{\rm nD}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,.

Again we have suppressed the full expressions for the first and second derivatives, but again they may be easily computed from the first expression.
For
0<z<1
, again using Proposition (
3.2
) and cancellations in the small-
z
asymptotics (
146
), (
145
) of
\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}
and
\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}
, we have

(181)

\left\lvert Q_{\rm nB}-Q_{\rm nD}\right\rvert\leq c\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime}-Q_{\rm nD}^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nB}^{\prime\prime}-Q_{\rm nD}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,.

Combining (
174
), (
175
), (
176
), (
180
), and (
181
) with the definition (
172
) of
m_{\theta}^{x}(z)
and the analogous expression (
173
) of its derivatives, we obtain

(182)

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{\theta}^{x}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,z\,,&z\geq 1\\
c\,,&0<z<1\,,\end{cases}\qquad\left\lvert(m_{\theta}^{x})^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\begin{cases}c\,,&z\geq 1\\
\frac{c}{z}\,,&0<z<1\,,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lvert(m_{\theta}^{x})^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\frac{c}{z}\,,&z\geq 1\\
\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,,&0<z<1\,.\end{cases}

Finally, we consider the numerator of
m_{z}^{x}(z)
from (
172
). Using the form (
55
) of
Q_{\rm nC}
and
Q_{\rm nE}
along with the identity (
166
), for
z\geq 1
we have

(183)

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nC}+Q_{\rm nE}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg|\textstyle z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}+\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)\bigg(\frac{8}{z}\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}+6\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}\right)+2\left(\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)+2z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-1\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle-\frac{12}{z}+z\left(\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}-\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}-\frac{K_{1}}{K_{0}}\right)\bigg)\bigg|\leq c\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nC}^{\prime}+Q_{\rm nE}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{z}\,,\qquad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nC}^{\prime\prime}+Q_{\rm nE}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,.

Again we suppress the full expressions for the first and second derivatives, as they are straightforward to compute. For
0<z<1
, we again use Proposition
3.2
along with cancellations in the small-
z
asymptotics (
146
), (
145
) of
\frac{I_{0}}{I_{1}}
and
\frac{I_{1}}{I_{0}}
to obtain

(184)

\textstyle\left\lvert Q_{\rm nC}+Q_{\rm nE}\right\rvert\leq c\,z\left\lvert\log(\frac{z}{2})\right\rvert\,,\quad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nC}^{\prime}+Q_{\rm nE}^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq c\,\left\lvert\log(\frac{z}{2})\right\rvert\,,\quad\left\lvert Q_{\rm nC}^{\prime\prime}+Q_{\rm nE}^{\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq\displaystyle c\,\frac{\left\lvert\log(\frac{z}{2})\right\rvert}{z}\,.

Combining (
174
), (
175
), (
176
), (
183
), and (
184
) with the definition (
172
) of
m_{z}^{x}(z)
and the analogous expression (
173
) of its derivatives, we obtain

(185)

\displaystyle\left\lvert m_{z}^{x}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,,&z\geq 1\\
c\,z\left\lvert\log(\frac{z}{2})\right\rvert\,,&0<z<1\,,\end{cases}\qquad\left\lvert(m_{z}^{x})^{\prime}(z)\right\rvert\leq\begin{cases}\frac{c}{z}\,,&z\geq 1\\
c\,\left\lvert\log(\frac{z}{2})\right\rvert\,,&0<z<1\,,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lvert(m_{z}^{x})^{\prime\prime}(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\frac{c}{z^{2}}\,,&z\geq 1\\
c\,\frac{\left\lvert\log(z/2)\right\rvert}{z}\,,&0<z<1\,.\end{cases}

Using the bounds (
179
), (
182
), and (
185
) along with the fact that
m_{\epsilon,r}^{x}(\xi)=m_{r}^{x}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
,
m_{\epsilon,\theta}^{x}(\xi)=m_{\theta}^{x}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
, and
m_{\epsilon,z}^{x}(\xi)=i\,m_{z}^{x}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert)
, we obtain Lemma
3.10
.
∎

3.4.
Proofs of Lemmas
1.4
and
1.9

Given the multiplier bounds in Lemmas
3.4
,
3.5
,
3.9
,
3.10
, we may now use Lemma
3.1
to prove Lemmas
1.4
and
1.9
. We begin with Lemma
1.4
.

3.4.1.
Proof of Lemma
1.4

Recalling the definitions (
23
) and (
24
) of
m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}
and
m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}
, we define the matrix-valued Fourier multipliers

(186)

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}(\xi)

\displaystyle=m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}(\xi)\,\bm{e}_{z}\otimes\bm{e}_{z}+m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}(\xi)\,(\bm{e}_{x}\otimes\bm{e}_{x}+\bm{e}_{y}\otimes\bm{e}_{y})\,,\qquad\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon,j}^{(1)}(\xi)=\phi_{j}(\xi)\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}(\xi)

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}(\xi)

\displaystyle=m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}^{-1}(\xi)\,\bm{e}_{z}\otimes\bm{e}_{z}+m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}^{-1}(\xi)\,(\bm{e}_{x}\otimes\bm{e}_{x}+\bm{e}_{y}\otimes\bm{e}_{y})\,,\qquad\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon,j}^{(2)}(\xi)=\phi_{j}(\xi)\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}(\xi)\,.

Here
\phi_{j}(\xi)
is as in (
105
). Letting
j_{\epsilon}=\frac{\left\lvert\log(2\pi\epsilon)\right\rvert}{\log(2)}
, by Lemmas
3.4
and
3.5
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon,j}^{(1)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon^{-1}\,2^{-j(\ell+1)}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,2^{-j\ell}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,,\end{cases}\qquad\ell=0,1,2\,;

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon,j}^{(2)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\,2^{j(1-\ell)}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-1}2^{-j\ell}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,,\end{cases}\qquad\ell=0,1,2\,.

We may then use Lemma
3.1
to obtain physical space estimates for
\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(1)}
and
\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(2)}
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(1)}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon^{-1}\,2^{-j}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,,\end{cases}\qquad\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(2)}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\,2^{j}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-1}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

Given
\bm{h}\in C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
with
\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{h}(s)\,ds=0
and
\bm{g}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
, we may use the dyadic partition of unity (
105
) to write

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}[\bm{h}]

\displaystyle=T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\bm{h}=\sum_{j}P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\bm{h}\,,\qquad\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}[\bm{g}]=T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\bm{g}=\sum_{j}P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\bm{g}\,.

On
\mathbb{T}
, we have
P_{\leq 0}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\bm{h}=P_{0}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\bm{h}
and
P_{\leq 0}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\bm{g}=P_{0}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\bm{g}
. We may then estimate

(187)

\displaystyle\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,0}^{(1)}*\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,0}^{(1)}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,0}^{(2)}*\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,0}^{(2)}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

For
j>0
we introduce the notation
\widetilde{P}_{j}=P_{j-1}+P_{j}+P_{j+1}
and note that
P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\bm{h}=P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{h}
and
P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\bm{g}=P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{g}
. For low frequencies
0<j<j_{\epsilon}
, we estimate

(188)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(1)}*(\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{h})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{j_{\epsilon}}\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(1)}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\,2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{h}\right\rvert_{\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}

\displaystyle\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(2)}*(\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{g})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(2)}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\,2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-1}\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}\,.

For high frequencies
j\geq j_{\epsilon}
, we have

(189)

\displaystyle\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(1)}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\bm{h}\right\rvert_{\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}

\displaystyle\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(2)}}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{\epsilon,j}^{(2)}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon\,\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}\,.

Combining the estimates (
187
), (
188
), and (
189
) and using the norm definition (
107
), we obtain Lemma
1.4
.
∎

3.4.2.
Proof of Lemma
1.9

The mapping properties of the operator
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{01}
may be shown by similar means to the previous section.
Recall the definition (
105
) of
\phi_{j}
and the forms of the five distinct multipliers
m_{\epsilon,z}^{z}
,
m_{\epsilon,r}^{z}
,
m_{\epsilon,z}^{x}
,
m_{\epsilon,r}^{x}
,
m_{\epsilon,\theta}^{x}
appearing in Corollary
1.8
as the components of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{01}
. We define the scalar-valued Fourier multipliers

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\phi_{j}(\xi)m_{\epsilon,z}^{z}(\xi)\,,\quad\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}(\xi)=\phi_{j}(\xi)m_{\epsilon,r}^{z}(\xi)\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\phi_{j}(\xi)m_{\epsilon,z}^{x}(\xi)\,,\quad\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}(\xi)=\phi_{j}(\xi)m_{\epsilon,r}^{x}(\xi)\,,\quad\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}(\xi)=\phi_{j}(\xi)m_{\epsilon,\theta}^{x}(\xi)\,.

As in the proof of Lemma
1.4
, we define
j_{\epsilon}=\frac{\left\lvert\log(2\pi\epsilon)\right\rvert}{\log(2)}
. Using Lemmas
3.9
and
3.10
, we have that three of the multipliers
\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}
,
\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}
, and
\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}
each satisfy the same set of estimates, namely

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\,2^{j(1-\ell)}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\,2^{-j\ell}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,,\end{cases}\quad\ell=0,1,2\,.

These multipliers will comprise
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}
, the leading order behavior (with respect to regularity) of the angle-averaged single layer operator
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{01}
. The remaining multipliers

\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}
and
\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}
satisfy a different set of estimates:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\,,\;\ell=0\\
c\,2^{-j}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\,,\;\ell=1,2\\
c\,\epsilon^{-1}2^{-j(1+\ell)}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,,\;\ell=0,1,2\,,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,2^{-j\ell}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{j(1-\ell)}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

These multipliers will make up the operator
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}
, which has ‘bad’
\epsilon
-dependence but is smoother.

Using the scalar-valued version of Lemma
3.1
(see
[
59
, Lemma 2.1]
), we may obtain the following physical space estimates for the inverse Fourier transforms of each
\widehat{M}_{\epsilon,j}
:

(190)

\displaystyle\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\,,\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\,,\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,\epsilon\,2^{j}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,2^{-j/2}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\,\epsilon^{-1}2^{-j}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{j}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

It will now be useful to further decompose the projection operator
\mathbb{P}_{01}
as follows. Given
\bm{g}(s,\theta)
defined along
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
, we may consider
\mathbb{P}_{01}\bm{g}
as in (
62
) and we may further define the following projections:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\,\bm{g}

\displaystyle=g_{0}^{z}\bm{e}_{z}\,,\qquad\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}=g_{0}^{r}\bm{e}_{r}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\,\bm{g}

\displaystyle=g_{\rm c,1}^{z}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{z}+g_{\rm s,1}^{z}\sin\theta\bm{e}_{z}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{1}^{r}\,\bm{g}

\displaystyle=g_{\rm c,1}^{r}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r}+g_{\rm s,1}^{r}\sin\theta\bm{e}_{r}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{1}^{\theta}\,\bm{g}

\displaystyle=g_{\rm s,1}^{\theta}\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}+g_{\rm c,1}^{\theta}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}\,.

We then decompose
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{01}
as
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}\mathbb{P}_{01}=\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}+\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}
where

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}(\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}+\mathbb{P}_{1}^{r}+\mathbb{P}_{1}^{\theta})\,,\qquad\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}=\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}(\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}+\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z})\,.

For
\bm{g}\in C_{s}^{1,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we use the dyadic partition of unity (
105
) to write

(191)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\bm{g}

\displaystyle=T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}+T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{r}\bm{g}+T_{M_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{\theta}\bm{g}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}P_{j}\big(T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}+T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{r}\bm{g}+T_{M_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{\theta}\bm{g}\big)\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}\bm{g}

\displaystyle=T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}+T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}P_{j}\big(T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}+T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\big)\,.

We may now obtain bounds for
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\bm{g}
and
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}\bm{g}
. For each multiplier
M
, we have
P_{\leq 0}T_{M}\bm{g}=P_{0}T_{M}\bm{g}
, so using the
L^{1}
bounds (
190
) we may estimate each of the components of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}
as

\displaystyle\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,0}^{z}*(\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,0}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\qquad\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}T_{M_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{\theta}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Here and throughout, the convolution
*
is with respect to
s
only. The components of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}
may be similarly estimated as

\displaystyle\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,r,0}^{z}*(\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,r,0}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,0}^{x}*(\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,0}^{x}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

We next bound the low frequencies
0<j<j_{\epsilon}
. As in the proof of Lemma
1.4
, we will use the notation
\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{g}=(P_{j-1}+P_{j}+P_{j+1})\bm{g}
and again note that
P_{j}T_{m}\bm{g}=P_{j}T_{m}\widetilde{P}_{j}\bm{g}
.

Using (
190
), we may estimate the first component of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}
as

\displaystyle\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}}\widetilde{P}_{j}(\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}*(\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,.

Likewise, the remaining two terms of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}
satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,,\qquad\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{\theta}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,.

In addition, the two components of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}
satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(3/2+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(3/2+\alpha)}\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1/2+\alpha)}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{1/2+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,,

\displaystyle\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(2+\alpha)}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon\,2^{j_{\epsilon}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,.

Here we have use that
2^{j_{\epsilon}}=\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon}
.

Finally, for high frequencies
j\geq j_{\epsilon}
, we may use the
L^{1}
estimates (
190
) to show that the components of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}
each satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}}\widetilde{P}_{j}(\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}*(\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,,

\displaystyle\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,,\qquad\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\alpha}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{\theta}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,.

For
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(3/2+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(3/2+\alpha)}\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,,

\displaystyle\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert\widetilde{P}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert_{(\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty})_{s}}\,.

Returning to the definitions (
191
) of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}
and
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}
, we may combine the above estimates and use the Besov characterization (
107
) of
C^{0,\alpha}_{s}
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{\epsilon}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert T_{M_{\epsilon,\theta,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{\theta}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{s}(\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon})}+c\epsilon\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}_{s}(\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon})}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\epsilon}^{+}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert T_{M_{\epsilon,r,j}^{z}}\mathbb{P}_{0}^{r}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert T_{M_{\epsilon,z,j}^{x}}\mathbb{P}_{1}^{z}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}_{s}(\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon})}\,.

∎

3.5.
Proofs of semigroup lemmas

We may additionally use the Littlewood-Paley decomposition (
106
) to show the estimates (
91
) and (
93
) for the semigroup
e^{-t\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}
stated in Lemmas
2.2
and
2.3
.

Proof of Lemma
2.2
.

We will require bounds for the Littlewood–Paley projection
P_{j}e^{-t\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{V}=e^{-t\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}P_{j}\bm{V}
for each
j
. Using the notation of section
3.4.1
, we first recall the definition of the matrix-valued Fourier multiplier

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}(k)=m_{\epsilon,{\rm t}}(k)\bm{e}_{z}\otimes\bm{e}_{z}+m_{\epsilon,{\rm n}}(k)(\bm{e}_{x}\otimes\bm{e}_{x}+\bm{e}_{y}\otimes\bm{e}_{y})\,.

At each time
t
, we may write the semigroup
e^{-t\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}
as a Fourier multiplier in
s
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[e^{-t\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{V}](k)

\displaystyle=e^{-t\,k^{4}\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}(k)}\mathcal{F}[\bm{V}](k)\,.

Recalling the definition (
105
) of
\phi_{j}
, at each
t
we consider

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\epsilon,j}(\xi,t)=e^{-t\,\xi^{4}\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon,j}(\xi)}\,,\qquad\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon,j}=(\phi_{j-1}(\xi)+\phi_{j}(\xi)+\phi_{j+1}(\xi))\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}(\xi)\,.

Using the growth bounds of Lemmas
3.4
and
3.5
, for
j_{\epsilon}=\frac{\left\lvert\log(2\pi\epsilon)\right\rvert}{\log(2)}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\epsilon,j}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}e^{-c\,t\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j}}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
e^{-c\,t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{4j}}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{2}\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\epsilon,j}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c\big(t\epsilon^{-1}2^{j}+(t\epsilon^{-1}2^{2j})^{2}\big)e^{-c\,t\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j}}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\big(t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{2j}+(t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{3j})^{2}\big)e^{-c\,t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{4j}}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

Using Lemma
3.1
, we thus obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{L}_{\epsilon,j}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq\begin{cases}c\,\big(t\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j}(1+t\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j})\big)^{1/2}e^{-c\,t\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j}}\,,&j\geq j_{\epsilon}\\
c\,\big(t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{4j}(1+t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{4j})\big)^{1/2}e^{-c\,t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{4j}}\,,&j<j_{\epsilon}\,.\end{cases}

For
\bm{V}\in C^{m,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})
, we use the definitions (
108
), (
106
) of
T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}
and
P_{j}
to write

\displaystyle e^{-t\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}\bm{V}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}T_{\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\epsilon,j}}P_{j}\bm{V}\,.

We will let
\omega=n+\alpha
and
\nu=m+\gamma
and use the characterization (
107
) of
C^{n,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
and
C^{m,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})
. Noting that
P_{\leq 0}=P_{0}
on
\mathbb{T}
, we may first estimate the lowest part of the norm (
107
) as

(192)

\displaystyle\left\lVert T_{\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\epsilon,0}}P_{\leq 0}\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\bm{L}_{\epsilon,0}*(P_{0}\bm{V})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq\left\lVert\bm{L}_{\epsilon,0}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\big(t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert(1+t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert)\big)^{1/2}e^{-c\,t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert}\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

For the low frequencies
0<j<j_{\epsilon}
, we have

(193)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\omega}\left\lVert T_{\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\epsilon,j}}P_{j}\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}=\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\omega}\left\lVert\bm{L}_{\epsilon,j}*(P_{j}\bm{V})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(\omega-\nu)}\left\lVert\bm{L}_{\epsilon,j}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}2^{j\nu}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0<j<j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(\omega-\nu)}\bigg(\big(t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{4j}(1+t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{4j})\big)^{1/2}e^{-c\,t\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert 2^{4j}}\bigg)\,2^{j\nu}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,t^{-\frac{(\omega-\nu)}{4}}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-\frac{(\omega-\nu)}{4}}\left\lvert\bm{V}\right\rvert_{\dot{B}^{\nu}_{\infty,\infty}}\,.

For the high frequencies
j\geq j_{\epsilon}
, we may similarly estimate

(194)

\displaystyle\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\omega}\left\lVert T_{\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\epsilon,j}}P_{j}\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j\omega}\left\lVert\bm{L}_{\epsilon,j}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\geq j_{\epsilon}}2^{j(\omega-\nu)}\bigg(\big(t\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j}(1+t\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j})\big)^{1/2}e^{-c\,t\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j}}\bigg)2^{j\nu}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{V}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,t^{-\frac{(\omega-\nu)}{3}}\epsilon^{\frac{\omega-\nu}{3}}\left\lvert\bm{V}\right\rvert_{\dot{B}^{\nu}_{\infty,\infty}}\,.

Combining the estimates (
192
), (
193
), and (
194
) with the characterization (
107
) of
C^{n,\alpha}
and
C^{m,\gamma}
, we obtain Lemma
2.2
.
∎

We next show Lemma
2.3
regarding a maximal regularity estimate for the semigroup
e^{-t\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}
.

Proof of Lemma
2.3
.

We first note that, using the characterization (
107
) of
C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
and the definition (
92
) of the spaces
\mathcal{Y}_{k}
, we may write
\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}
as

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\sup\bigg(\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}\bm{g}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\,\sup_{j>0}\,2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sup\bigg(\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}\bm{g}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,\,\sup_{j>0}\,2^{j(1+\alpha)}\;\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg)\,.

Again taking
j_{\epsilon}=\frac{\left\lvert\log(2\pi\epsilon)\right\rvert}{\log(2)}
and using Lemmas
3.4
and
3.5
, for
j\geq j_{\epsilon}
we have

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\;

\displaystyle 2^{j(4+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{h}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle=\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\;2^{j(4+\alpha)}\left\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-t^{\prime})\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}P_{j}\bm{g}(\cdot,t^{\prime})\,dt^{\prime}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\;2^{j(4+\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c\,(t-t^{\prime})\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j}}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}(\cdot,t^{\prime})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\bigg(\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\;2^{3j}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c\,(t-t^{\prime})\epsilon^{-1}2^{3j}}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)\bigg(\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c\,\epsilon\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\,.

In the first line we have used that
P_{j}
commutes with the semigroup
e^{-t\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{4}}
.

For low modes with
0<j<j_{\epsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}

\displaystyle\;2^{j(4+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{h}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c\,\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\;2^{j(4+\alpha)}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c\,(t-t^{\prime})\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,2^{4j}}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}(\cdot,t^{\prime})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\bigg(\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\;2^{3j}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c\,(t-t^{\prime})\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,2^{4j}}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)\bigg(\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}2^{j(1+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c\,\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-3/4}\bigg(\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\,\int_{0}^{t}(t-t^{\prime})^{-3/4}\,dt^{\prime}\bigg)\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\leq c\,T^{1/4}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{-3/4}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\,.

Finally, for the zero-mode projection
P_{\leq 0}=P_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert P_{0}\bm{h}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert P_{0}\bm{g}(\cdot,t^{\prime})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,dt^{\prime}\leq c\,T\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}\,.

In total, we obtain Lemma
2.3
.
∎

4.
Bounds for single and double layer remainders

4.1.
Setup and tools

This section is devoted to the proofs of Lemmas
1.10
,
1.11
, and
1.12
regarding the mapping properties of the single and double layer remainder terms. We first require a more detailed understanding of the kernels (
37
) and (
38
) along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
as functions of the surface parameters
s
and
\theta
.

Throughout, we will be proving bounds for a single filament
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
with centerline
\bm{X}(s)
, as well as corresponding Lipschitz estimates for two nearby filaments
\Sigma_{\epsilon}^{(a)}
and
\Sigma_{\epsilon}^{(b)}
with centerlines
\bm{X}^{(a)}(s)
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}(s)
satisfying Lemma
1.1
. When comparing two nearby filaments, we will use the superscripts
(a)
and
(b)
to denote quantities belonging to filament
\Sigma_{\epsilon}^{(a)}
and
\Sigma_{\epsilon}^{(b)}
, respectively.

Given the single and double layer operators for an arbitrarily curved filament, we will be extracting the corresponding single and double layer operators about the straight filament
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
. We will continue to denote quantities defined along
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
using overline notation. For
\overline{\bm{x}},\overline{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
, we define

(195)

\overline{\bm{R}}:=\overline{\bm{x}}-\overline{\bm{x}}^{\prime}=(s-s^{\prime})\bm{e}_{z}+\epsilon(\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)-\bm{e}_{r}(\theta^{\prime}))=\textstyle(s-s^{\prime})\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})\,.

Defining
\widehat{s}:=s-s^{\prime}
and
\widehat{\theta}:=\theta-\theta^{\prime}
, we may work in terms of
s,\widehat{s},\theta,\widehat{\theta}
rather than
s,s^{\prime},\theta,\theta^{\prime}
, and write

(196)

\overline{\bm{R}}=\textstyle\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,,\qquad\widehat{s}=s-s^{\prime}\,,\;\widehat{\theta}=\theta-\theta^{\prime}\,.

Noting that
\overline{\bm{n}}(\overline{\bm{x}})=\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)
and
\overline{\bm{n}}(\overline{\bm{x}}^{\prime})=\bm{e}_{r}(\theta-\widehat{\theta})
, we may write

\displaystyle|\overline{\bm{R}}(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\widehat{s}^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime}=2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,.

Along a curved filament
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
, we similarly consider

\displaystyle\bm{R}:=\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}

as a function of
(s,\widehat{s},\theta,\widehat{\theta})
. Using the orthonormal frame ODEs (
3
), we note the following expansions with respect to arclength
s
:

(197)

\displaystyle\bm{X}(s)-\bm{X}(s-\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)-\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)-\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s-\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s)-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s-\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}(-\kappa_{1}(s)\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s))+\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm n_{1}}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s)-\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s-\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}(-\kappa_{2}(s)\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)-\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s))+\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm n_{2}}(s,\widehat{s})\,.

Given two filaments with centerlines
\bm{X}^{(a)}
,
\bm{X}^{(b)}
in
C^{2,\beta}(\mathbb{T})
, each of the remainder terms
\bm{Q}_{i}
in (
197
) satisfies

(198)

\left\lVert\bm{Q}_{i}^{(a)}\cdot\bm{e}_{j}^{(a)}-\bm{Q}_{i}^{(b)}\cdot\bm{e}_{j}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}\leq c\,\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\left\lVert\kappa_{\ell}^{(a)}-\kappa_{\ell}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}\,,\qquad i,j\in\{{\rm t},{\rm n_{1}},{\rm n_{2}}\}\,,

where the norms
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{Y}
are defined for functions of both
(s,\theta)
and
(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\in\mathbb{T}\times 2\pi\mathbb{T}
by any of the following:

(199)

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle:=\esssup_{s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta}}\left\lvert Q(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert,\quad\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{1}}:=\esssup_{\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta}}\left\lVert Q(\cdot,\cdot,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{2}}

\displaystyle:=\esssup_{s,\theta}\left\lVert Q(s,\theta,\cdot,\cdot)\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}\,.

Note that for
Q=Q(s,\theta)
we have
\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{1}}=\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}
, and for
Q=Q(\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})
we have
\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}_{2}}=\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}
.
For some combinations of
\bm{Q}_{i}\cdot\bm{e}_{j}
, we note the following more refined expansions:

(200)

\displaystyle\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}Q_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\,,

\displaystyle\bm{Q}_{{\rm n}_{j}}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{e}_{{\rm n}_{j}}(s)

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}Q_{{\rm tn}_{j}}(s,\widehat{s})\,,\quad j=1,2\,;

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q_{i}^{(a)}-Q_{i}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\left\lVert\kappa_{\ell}^{(a)}-\kappa_{\ell}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}\,,\quad i\in\{{\rm t},{\rm tn_{1}},{\rm tn_{2}}\}\,.

Using (
197
) and the definition (
5
) of
\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)
and
\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)
, we may write

(201)

\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r}(s-\widehat{s},\theta)=\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)\,,\qquad\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{\theta}(s-\widehat{s},\theta)=\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)\,,

where, using (
198
) and (
200
),
\bm{Q}_{r}
and
\bm{Q}_{\theta}
satisfy

(202)

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)

\displaystyle=-\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)+\widehat{s}Q_{0,1}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)\cdot\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})+\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}Q_{0,2}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\textstyle\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\textstyle\kappa_{3}\cos(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}Q_{0,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)^{2}+\kappa_{3}^{2}+\widehat{s}Q_{0,4}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}Q_{0,5}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})\,.

For each
Q_{0,j}
above, using the norms
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{Y}
defined in (
199
), we have

(203)

\left\lVert Q_{0,j}^{(a)}-Q_{0,j}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}\leq c\,\epsilon^{-\beta}\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\left\lVert\kappa_{\ell}^{(a)}-\kappa_{\ell}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}\,.

Here the
\epsilon^{-\beta}
arises due to the
\theta
-dependence of these terms along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, and we have
\beta=0
for the
Y=L^{\infty}
estimate.
In addition, we note that the following identities hold along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
:

(204)

\displaystyle\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta-\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,,\qquad\textstyle\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cdot\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)=\textstyle\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})

\displaystyle\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\theta,\widehat{s})-\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\theta-\widehat{\theta},\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2},\widehat{s})\,.

Using the above expansions, we may write (see
[
59
, Section 3]
):

(205)

\displaystyle\bm{R}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\bm{X}(s)-\bm{X}(s-\widehat{s})+\epsilon\big(\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\big)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,.

Recalling the form (
196
) of
\overline{\bm{R}}
, we have

(206)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{2}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{R,0}(s,\theta)+2\kappa_{3}\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{R,1}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{R,2}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,,

where
Q_{R,j}
are given by

(207)

\displaystyle Q_{R,0}(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=-2\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)+\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)^{2}+\epsilon\kappa_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle Q_{R,1}(s,\widehat{s})

\displaystyle=3Q_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})+\left\lvert\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle Q_{R,2}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2Q_{0,1}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)+4\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{0,2}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})

\displaystyle+2\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon Q_{0,4}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)

\displaystyle Q_{R,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\textstyle 4Q_{0,3}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})-4\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cdot\bm{Q}_{r}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)+4\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|\bm{Q}_{\theta}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})|^{2}\,.

Here we emphasize that
Q_{R,0}(s,\theta)
does not depend on
\widehat{s}
and
\widehat{\theta}
, as this will be used in the proof of Lemma
5.4
later on. Using the definition (
199
) of the norms
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{Y}
and the bounds (
203
), the remaining
Q_{R,j}
can be seen to satisfy

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q_{R,1}^{(a)}-Q_{R,1}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(b)})\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\left\lVert\kappa_{\ell}^{(a)}-\kappa_{\ell}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q_{R,j}^{(a)}-Q_{R,j}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{-\beta}\,c(\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(b)})\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\left\lVert\kappa_{\ell}^{(a)}-\kappa_{\ell}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{Y}\,,\quad j=2,3\,.

For two filaments with centerlines
\bm{X}^{(a)}
,
\bm{X}^{(b)}
in
C^{2,\beta}(\mathbb{T})
, we have

(208)

\displaystyle\bm{R}^{(a)}-\bm{R}^{(b)}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\widehat{s}\big(\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)}(s)-\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(b)}(s)\big)+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\big(\bm{e}_{\theta}^{(a)}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\bm{e}_{\theta}^{(b)}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\big)

\displaystyle-\widehat{s}^{2}\big(\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(a)}(s,\widehat{s})-\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(b)}(s,\widehat{s})\big)+\epsilon\widehat{s}\big(\bm{Q}_{r}^{(a)}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\bm{Q}_{r}^{(b)}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\big)\,.

Furthermore, we may write the differences

(209)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{R,0}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\widehat{\theta})\kappa_{3}+\widehat{s}^{4}Q_{R,1}+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}Q_{R,2}+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})Q_{R,3}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert|\overline{\bm{R}}|(\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert+|\overline{\bm{R}}|)}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(a)}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(b)}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon\widehat{s}^{2}(Q_{R,0}^{(a)}-Q_{R,0}^{(b)})+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}\sin(\widehat{\theta})(\kappa_{3}^{(a)}-\kappa_{3}^{(b)})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(a)}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(b)}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(a)}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(b)}\right\rvert)}

\displaystyle+\frac{\widehat{s}^{4}(Q_{R,1}^{(a)}-Q_{R,1}^{(b)})+\epsilon\widehat{s}^{3}(Q_{R,2}^{(a)}-Q_{R,2}^{(b)})+\epsilon^{2}\widehat{s}^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})(Q_{R,3}^{(a)}-Q_{R,3}^{(b)})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(a)}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(b)}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(a)}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(b)}\right\rvert)}\,.

We may use (
209
) in expanding differences of higher powers
k\in\mathbb{N}
as

(210)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{k}}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{k}}=\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|}\bigg)\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{\ell}|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{k-1-\ell}}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(a)}\right\rvert^{k}}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(b)}\right\rvert^{k}}=\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(a)}\right\rvert}-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(b)}\right\rvert}\bigg)\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(a)}\right\rvert^{\ell}\left\lvert\bm{R}^{(b)}\right\rvert^{k-1-\ell}}\,.

In addition, we note the following expansions for
\bm{R}
in the direction of the normal vectors
\bm{n}(\bm{x}^{\prime})
and
\bm{n}(\bm{x})
. Noting that
\bm{n}^{\prime}:=\bm{n}(\bm{x}^{\prime})=\bm{e}_{r}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})
and
\bm{n}=\bm{n}(\bm{x})=\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)
, we have

(211)

\displaystyle\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn^{\prime}}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})=\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime}+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn^{\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle\bm{R}\cdot\bm{n}

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn}(s,\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})=\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn}\,.

Furthermore, using (
202
), we have

(212)

\displaystyle\bm{R}\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rt}(s,\widehat{s},\theta,\widehat{\theta})=\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\bm{e}_{z}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta)+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rt}

\displaystyle\bm{R}\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\sin(\widehat{\theta})+\epsilon\widehat{s}\kappa_{3}\cos(\theta-\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm R\theta}(s,\widehat{s},\theta,\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle=\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}(\theta)+\epsilon\widehat{s}\kappa_{3}\cos(\theta-\widehat{\theta})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm R\theta}

\displaystyle\bm{R}\cdot\bm{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)\cdot\bm{\varphi}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\cdot\bm{\varphi}-\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{\varphi}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r}\cdot\bm{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot(\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi})-\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{\varphi}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r}\cdot\bm{\varphi}\,.

Here, given two curves
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
in
C^{2,\beta}
, each of the remainder terms appearing in (
211
) and (
212
) satisfies an estimate of the form

(213)
