\left\lVert(\mathcal{D}^{(a)}+\bm{V}^{(a)}-\mathcal{D}^{(b)}-\bm{V}^{(b)})[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

The proof of Lemma
5.2
appears in section
5.1
.
We will additionally rely on the following uniform bound for
(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}+\bm{V})^{-1}
with respect to the filament centerline shape.

Lemma 5.3

(Inverse completed double layer)
.

Let
0<\gamma<\beta<1
, and let
\epsilon>0
be fixed. Given any filament
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
with centerline
\bm{X}(s)\in C^{2,\beta}(\mathbb{T})
satisfying the non-self-intersection condition (
2
), the operator
(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}+\bm{V})^{-1}
is uniformly bounded with respect to the filament centerline shape. In particular, for
\bm{\varphi}\in C^{0,\gamma}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we have

(285)

\textstyle\left\lVert(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}+\bm{V})^{-1}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

for any
\gamma^{+}\in(\gamma,\beta]
.
Moreover, given two nearby filaments with centerlines
\bm{X}^{(a)}
,
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, we have

(286)

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle\left\lVert(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{(a)}+\bm{V}^{(a)})^{-1}[\bm{\varphi}]-(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{(b)}+\bm{V}^{(b)})^{-1}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

The proof of Lemma
5.3
is given in section
5.2
. Note that the
\epsilon
-dependence here is not explicit.

The final ingredient used in the proof of Lemma
1.13
involves the mapping properties of the hypersingular operator
\mathcal{T}
given by (
281
). To determine these mapping properties, we will first need the following auxiliary lemma. The bound below is a more general version of the result appearing in
[
59
, Lemma 4.3]
.

Lemma 5.4

(Hypersingular operator: estimating
\left\lvert\cdot\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}
seminorms)
.

Let
\ell,k,n
be nonnegative integers satisfying
\ell+k+3=n
and
\ell+k
is even. Suppose
\varphi(s,\theta)\in C^{1,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
and
g(s,\theta,s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\in C^{0,\alpha^{+}}(\Gamma_{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{\epsilon})
for any
0<\alpha<\alpha^{+}<1
. For
\bm{R}
as in (
205
), let
I_{\ell kn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})
denote

(287)

I_{\varphi;\ell kn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta}):=\frac{\widehat{s}^{\ell}(\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2}))^{k}\,g(s,\theta,s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})}{|\bm{R}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})|^{n}}\,\big(\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s,\theta)\big)\,.

Then for any
(s_{0},\theta_{0})\in[-1/2,1/2]\times[-\pi,\pi]
with
s_{0}^{2}+\theta_{0}^{2}\neq 0
we have

(288)

\displaystyle\bigg|{\rm p.v.}

\displaystyle\int_{-s_{0}-1/2}^{-s_{0}+1/2}\int_{-\theta_{0}-\pi}^{-\theta_{0}+\pi}I_{\varphi,\ell kn}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle-{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}I_{\varphi,\ell kn}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\big(\textstyle\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\big)\big(\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha^{+}}_{1}}+\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+\textstyle\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg)\sqrt{s_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}}^{\,\alpha}\,,

where
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha^{+}}_{1}}
and
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}
are as in (
199
).

The proof of Lemma
5.4
appears in Appendix
D
.
Using Lemma
5.4
, we may then show the final ingredient needed in the proof of Lemma
1.13
. In particular, we show the following bounds for the hypersingular operator
\mathcal{T}
given by (
281
).

Lemma 5.5

(Hypersingular operator bounds)
.

Let
0<\alpha<\beta<1
. Given a filament
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
with centerline
\bm{X}\in C^{2,\beta}
, we consider the hypersingular operator
\mathcal{T}
given by (
281
). Given
\bm{\varphi}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we may decompose
\mathcal{T}[\bm{\varphi}]
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[\bm{\varphi}]=\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}[\bm{\varphi}]+\mathcal{T}_{+}[\bm{\varphi}]

where

(289)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\textstyle\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{1}}

for any
\alpha^{+}\in(\alpha,\beta]
.
In addition, given two nearby curves
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, we have the Lipschitz estimate

(290)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\textstyle\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{T}_{+}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{1}}\,.

The proof of Lemma
5.5
appears in section
5.3
.

Given Lemmas
5.2
,
5.3
, and
5.5
, we may proceed to the proof of the main result of this section, Lemma
1.13
.

Proof of Lemma
1.13
: bounds on full Neumann boundary value.

Given
\theta
-independent Dirichlet data
\bm{v}(s)
, we may solve for the intermediate density
\bm{\varphi}(\bm{x})=\bm{\varphi}(s,\theta)
in the completed double layer representation (
274
) as

\displaystyle\bm{\varphi}(s,\theta)=2\bm{v}(s)-\mathcal{M}[\bm{v}(s)]\,,\qquad\textstyle\mathcal{M}=(\mathcal{D}+\bm{V})(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}+\bm{V})^{-1}\,.

Using Lemmas
5.2
and
5.3
, we may estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{M}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\textstyle\left\lVert(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}+\bm{V})^{-1}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,,\qquad 0<\gamma<\gamma^{+}<1\,.

In addition, for two curves
\bm{X}^{(a)}
,
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{M}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]-\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Using Lemma
5.5
, we may write
\bm{w}(s,\theta)
from (
282
) as

\displaystyle\bm{w}(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}[2\bm{v}(s)]-\mathcal{T}[\mathcal{M}[\bm{v}(s)]]

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}[\bm{v}(s)]+2\mathcal{T}_{+}[\bm{v}(s)]-\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}[\mathcal{M}[\bm{v}(s)]]-\mathcal{T}_{+}[\mathcal{M}[\bm{v}(s)]]\,.

For
\bm{v}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
, we have

\displaystyle 2\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}\,,

\displaystyle 2\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]-\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}\,,

while each of the remaining terms are smoother. In particular, for
0<\alpha<\gamma<\gamma^{+}<1
, we may estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}[\mathcal{M}[\bm{v}]]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\mathcal{M}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}[\mathcal{M}[\bm{v}]]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\epsilon^{-1-\gamma^{+}}\left\lVert\mathcal{M}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{1}}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

In addition, we obtain the Lipschitz estimates

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]-\mathcal{T}_{+}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{1}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}[\mathcal{M}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]]-\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}[\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\big[\mathcal{M}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]-\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\big]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}[\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]]-\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}[\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\left\lVert\mathcal{M}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]-\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}+\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\left\lVert\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}^{(a)}[\mathcal{M}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]]-\mathcal{T}_{+}^{(b)}[\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}^{(a)}\big[\mathcal{M}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]-\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\big]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}+\left\lVert\mathcal{T}_{+}^{(a)}[\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]]-\mathcal{T}_{+}^{(b)}[\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma^{+}}\bigg(\left\lVert\mathcal{M}^{(a)}[\bm{v}]-\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{1}}+\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\mathcal{M}^{(b)}[\bm{v}]\right\rVert_{C^{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Combining the above bounds, we obtain Lemma
1.13
.
∎

In the subsequent sections we prove each of the building block lemmas used in the proof of Lemma
1.13
. Section
5.1
contains the proof of Lemma
5.2
, section
5.2
contains the proof of Lemma
5.3
, and section
5.3
contains the proof of Lemma
5.5
.

5.1.
Proof of Lemma
5.2
: completed double layer

We begin by considering the double layer operator
\mathcal{D}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}[\bm{\varphi}]=\overline{\mathcal{D}}[\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]+\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}[\bm{\varphi}]

where
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
are as in (
64
). By Lemma
1.11
, we have that
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}
satisfies

(291)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-\gamma^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{D}}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-\gamma^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

To estimate
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
, we use the form (
38
) of the kernel
\bm{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
and the form (
211
) of
\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime}
to write

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{D}}[\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\overline{\bm{R}}\,(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime})\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot(\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\,\overline{\bm{R}}\,\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot(\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

Then, by Lemma
4.2
, we have

(292)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\overline{\mathcal{D}}[\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\overline{\mathcal{D}}[(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{\varphi}-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\,\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Next, taking
\theta
-derivatives along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
and using the expansions (
196
), (
211
), (
212
), (
249
), we may write

\displaystyle\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{\mathcal{D}}[\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4\pi}\,{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{\overline{\bm{R}}\,(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime})(\bm{e}_{\theta}\cdot\bm{\varphi})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}+\frac{\overline{\bm{R}}\,(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot(\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}))(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{5\overline{\bm{R}}\,(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot(\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}))(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime})(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{R}})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{7}}\bigg)\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4\pi}\,{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{-2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta})(\bm{e}_{\theta}\cdot\bm{\varphi})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{\sin(\widehat{\theta})(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta})(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{\varphi}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}\cdot\bm{\varphi})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{10\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\sin(\widehat{\theta})(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta})\,(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{\varphi}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}\cdot\bm{\varphi})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{7}}\bigg)\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

Using Lemma
4.4
, case (2), we then obtain

(293)

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{\mathcal{D}}[\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\overline{\mathcal{D}}[(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{\varphi}-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Here the additional
\epsilon^{-\gamma}
arises due to the dependence on the curved frame vector
\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})
in the above expansion.
Taking
s
-derivatives along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, we next have

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\overline{\mathcal{D}}[\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4\pi}\,{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{\overline{\bm{R}}\,(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime})(\bm{e}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{\varphi})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}+\frac{\overline{\bm{R}}\,(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot(\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}))(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{5\overline{\bm{R}}\,(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot(\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}))(\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{n}}^{\prime})(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{R}}\cdot\overline{\bm{R}})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{7}}\bigg)\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4\pi}\,{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{-2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta})(\bm{e}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{\varphi})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{5}}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt-\frac{10\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})(\widehat{s}+2\epsilon^{2}\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\sin(\widehat{\theta}))(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta})(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}\cdot\bm{\varphi}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}\cdot\bm{\varphi})}{|\overline{\bm{R}}|^{7}}\bigg)\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}\,.

Again using Lemma
4.4
, case (2), we have

(294)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\mathcal{D}}[\Phi^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\mathcal{D}}[(\Phi^{(a)})^{-1}\bm{\varphi}-(\Phi^{(b)})^{-1}\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

Here again we lose a factor of
\epsilon^{-\gamma}
from the single
\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta-\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})
term above.
Combining (
291
), (
292
), (
293
), and (
294
), we obtain

(295)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{D}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{D}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{D}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

It remains to estimate the completion flow
\bm{V}[\bm{\varphi}]
, given by (
275
). For
\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}
, it will be convenient to rewrite
\bm{R}_{X}=\bm{x}(s,\theta)-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})
in terms of
\widehat{s}=s-s^{\prime}
and
s,\theta
. Using the expansions (
197
), we may write
\bm{R}_{X}
as

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{X}(s,\widehat{s},\theta)=\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)-\widehat{s}^{2}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}(s,\widehat{s})+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)\,.

For two filaments
\bm{X}^{(a)}
,
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, we note the expansions

\displaystyle\bm{R}_{X}^{(a)}-\bm{R}_{X}^{(b)}

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}\big(\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(b)}-\widehat{s}(\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(b)})\big)+\epsilon(\bm{e}_{r}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{r}^{(b)})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\bm{R}_{X}^{(a)}|^{k}}-\frac{1}{|\bm{R}_{X}^{(b)}|^{k}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(-2\widehat{s}(\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(b)}\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(b)}-\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(a)}\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)})-2\epsilon(\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(b)}\cdot\bm{e}_{r}^{(b)}-\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(a)}\cdot\bm{e}_{r}^{(a)})

\displaystyle+\widehat{s}^{2}(|\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(b)}|^{2}-|\bm{Q}_{\rm t}^{(a)}|^{2})\bigg)\frac{\widehat{s}^{2}}{|\bm{R}_{X}^{(a)}||\bm{R}_{X}^{(b)}|(|\bm{R}_{X}^{(a)}|+|\bm{R}_{X}^{(b)}|)}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{|\bm{R}_{X}^{(a)}|^{\ell}|\bm{R}_{X}^{(b)}|^{k-1-\ell}}\,.

From
[
52
, Lemma 3.1]
, we have

(296)

\left\lvert\bm{R}_{X}\right\rvert\geq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\sqrt{\widehat{s}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\,;

in particular,
\frac{\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert}{|\bm{R}_{X}|}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})
.
Then, using the forms (
37
), (
276
) of
\mathcal{G}
and
\bm{l}_{\mathcal{R}}
as well as the fact that
\left\lvert\bm{R}_{X}\right\rvert\geq\epsilon
while
\left\lvert\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})\right\rvert=\epsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{V}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{V}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\bm{V}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Here we recall that
dS_{x^{\prime}}=\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}
.

We next take derivatives of
\bm{V}[\bm{\varphi}]
along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
. Here we use
\partial_{j}
to denote
\partial_{s}
or
\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}
. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{j}\bm{V}[\bm{\varphi}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\partial_{j}\mathcal{G}(\bm{x},\bm{X}(s^{\prime}))\,\bm{\varphi}(\bm{x}^{\prime})\,dS_{x^{\prime}}+\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\partial_{j}\bm{l}_{\mathcal{R}}(\bm{x},\bm{X}(s^{\prime}))\times\big((\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime}))\times\bm{\varphi}(\bm{x}^{\prime})\big)\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\,.

From the forms (
37
), (
276
) of
\mathcal{G}
and
\bm{l}_{\mathcal{R}}
, we may calculate

\displaystyle\partial_{j}\mathcal{G}(\bm{x},\bm{X}(s^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=-\frac{(\partial_{j}\bm{R}_{X}\cdot\bm{R}_{X}){\bf I}}{|\bm{R}_{X}|^{3}}+\frac{\partial_{j}\bm{R}_{X}\otimes\bm{R}_{X}+\bm{R}_{X}\otimes\partial_{j}\bm{R}_{X}}{|\bm{R}_{X}|^{3}}-3\frac{(\partial_{j}\bm{R}_{X}\cdot\bm{R}_{X})\bm{R}_{X}\otimes\bm{R}_{X}}{|\bm{R}_{X}|^{5}}\,;

\displaystyle\partial_{j}{\bm{l}}_{\mathcal{R}}(\bm{x},\bm{X}(s^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial_{j}\bm{R}_{X}}{|\bm{R}_{X}|^{3}}+3\frac{(\partial_{j}\bm{R}_{X}\cdot\bm{R}_{X})\bm{R}_{X}}{|\bm{R}_{X}|^{5}}

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\bm{R}_{X}

\displaystyle=(1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s,\theta))\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s)+\epsilon\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)\,,\qquad\textstyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{R}_{X}=\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)\,.

Using the above expressions as well as the form (
45
) of the
\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}
seminorm, we obtain the bounds

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{j}\bm{V}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma})\,\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg(\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2+\gamma}}\,dS_{x^{\prime}}+\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3+\gamma}}\,\epsilon\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\bigg)\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{j}\bm{V}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\partial_{j}\bm{V}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\gamma}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\gamma}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\gamma}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Combining the estimates for
\bm{V}
with the bounds for
\mathcal{D}
, we obtain Lemma
5.2
.
∎

5.2.
Proof of Lemma
5.3
: inverse double layer

The proof of the bound (
285
) relies on a compactness argument and is nearly identical to the Laplace setting
[
59
, Section 4.2]
.

Given fixed constants
\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}
and
c_{\Gamma}
, we begin by fixing a nonzero function
\bm{\varphi}=\bm{\varphi}(s,\theta)
in
C^{0,\gamma}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, where the space
C^{0,\gamma}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
is understood with respect to the norm (
45
); in particular, without reference to the filament centerline shape. We will show that

(297)

\textstyle\left\lVert(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}+\bm{V})^{-1}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\leq c_{\varphi}(\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

for any filament
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
with centerline curve
\bm{X}(s)
satisfying
\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma^{+}}}\leq\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}
and the non-self-intersection condition (
2
) with the fixed choice of
c_{\Gamma}
. Since
\bm{\varphi}
is arbitrary, the uniform boundedness principle then implies that the bound (
297
) holds independent of
\bm{\varphi}
.

To show (
297
), we fix
\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}=1
. Proceeding by contradiction, if the bound (
297
) does not hold, then there exists a sequence of curves
\bm{X}_{j}
,
j\in\mathbb{N}
, satisfying (
2
) and
\left\lVert(\bm{X}_{j})_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma^{+}}}\leq\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}
such that

\displaystyle\textstyle\left\lVert(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}+\bm{V}_{j})^{-1}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}>j\,.

Here
\mathcal{D}_{j}+\bm{V}_{j}
denotes the double layer operator and completion flow corresponding to curve
j
. We define

\displaystyle\bm{h}_{j}=\frac{\bm{\varphi}}{\left\lVert(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}+\bm{V}_{j})^{-1}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}}\,,\qquad\bm{g}_{j}=\frac{(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}+\bm{V}_{j})^{-1}[\bm{\varphi}]}{\left\lVert(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}+\bm{V}_{j})^{-1}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}}\,.

Since
\left\lVert\bm{g}_{j}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}=1
, we then have

(298)

\textstyle(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{j}+\bm{V}_{j})[\bm{g}_{j}]=\bm{h}_{j}\to 0\quad\text{in }C^{0,\gamma}\,.

Using Lemma
5.2
, we have that
(\mathcal{D}_{j}+\bm{V}_{j})[\bm{g}_{j}]
is smoother, in fact, bounded in
C^{1,\gamma}
uniformly in
j
. Thus (
298
) implies that along a subsequence
j_{\ell}
,

(299)

\bm{g}_{j_{\ell}}-2\bm{h}_{j_{\ell}}\to\bm{g}_{\infty}\qquad\text{strongly in }C^{0,\gamma}

for some limit
\bm{g}_{\infty}
with
\left\lVert\bm{g}_{\infty}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}=1
.

Since
\left\lVert(\bm{X}_{j})_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma^{+}}}\leq\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}
, there exists a subsequence of curves
\bm{X}_{j_{k}}
converging (after possible translation) strongly in
C^{2,\gamma}
,
0<\gamma<\gamma^{+}
, to a limit curve
\bm{X}_{\infty}(s)
satisfying (
2
). Using the form (
39
) of
\mathcal{D}
, for any
\bm{\psi}\in C^{0,\gamma}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{D}_{j_{k}}-\mathcal{D}_{\infty})[\bm{\psi}]

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\big(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\widehat{\bm{e}}_{r,j_{k}}\big)\big(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\widehat{\bm{e}}_{r,j_{k}}\big)\cdot\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}\big(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\widehat{\bm{e}}_{r,j_{k}}\big)\cdot\bm{\psi}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}{|\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\widehat{\bm{e}}_{r,j_{k}}|^{5}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,j_{k}}\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{4\pi}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{\big(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\widehat{\bm{e}}_{r,\infty}\big)\big(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\widehat{\bm{e}}_{r,\infty}\big)\cdot\bm{e}_{r,\infty}\big(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\widehat{\bm{e}}_{r,\infty}\big)\cdot\bm{\psi}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}{|\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\widehat{\bm{e}}_{r,\infty}|^{5}}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,\infty}\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,,

where we denote
\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}(s,s^{\prime})=\bm{X}_{j_{k}}(s)-\bm{X}_{j_{k}}(s^{\prime})
,
\widehat{\bm{e}}_{r,j_{k}}(s,\theta,s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})=\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s,\theta)-\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})
, and each of
\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j}
,
\bm{e}_{r,j}
, and
\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,j}
are defined along the curve
\bm{X}_{j}
. From the form of these expressions, we can see that
|(\mathcal{D}_{j_{k}}-\mathcal{D}_{\infty})[\bm{\psi}]|\to 0
as
j_{k}\to\infty
, since
\bm{X}_{j_{k}}\to\bm{X}_{\infty}
.
Similarly, using the form (
275
) of the completion flow
\bm{V}
, we have

\displaystyle(\bm{V}_{j_{k}}-\bm{V}_{\infty})[\bm{\psi}]

\displaystyle=\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}{|\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s,\theta)|}-\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,\infty}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}{|\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s,\theta)|}\bigg)\,\bm{\psi}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bigg(\frac{(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}})\otimes(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}})\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}{|\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s,\theta)|^{3}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad-\frac{(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,\infty})\otimes(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,\infty})\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,\infty}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})}{|\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s,\theta)|^{3}}\,\bigg)\bm{\psi}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}})}{|\widehat{\bm{X}}_{j_{k}}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s,\theta)|^{3}}\times\big(\bm{e}_{r,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\times\bm{\psi}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,j_{k}}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{(\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,\infty})}{|\widehat{\bm{X}}_{\infty}+\epsilon\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s,\theta)|^{3}}\times\big(\bm{e}_{r,\infty}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\times\bm{\psi}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon,\infty}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,,

and again we see that
(\bm{V}_{j_{k}}-\bm{V}_{\infty})[\bm{\psi}]\to 0
as
j_{k}\to\infty
. Together, we have
\left\lvert(\mathcal{D}_{j_{k}}+\bm{V}_{j_{k}})[\bm{\psi}]-(\mathcal{D}_{\infty}+\bm{V}_{\infty})[\bm{\psi}]\right\rvert\to 0
. Therefore, by a diagonalization argument, we have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{D}_{j}+\bm{V}_{j})[\bm{g}_{j}]\to(\mathcal{D}_{\infty}+\bm{V}_{\infty})[\bm{g}_{\infty}]

along a subsequence, and, using (
299
),
(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}_{\infty}+\bm{V}_{\infty})[\bm{g}_{\infty}]=0
. By injectivity of the completed double layer operator (see discussion below (
274
)), we then have
\bm{g}_{\infty}=0
, contradicting
\left\lVert\bm{g}_{\infty}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}=1
. We thus obtain (
285
).

We now turn to the Lipschitz bound (
286
). Consider two nearby filaments
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
.
We aim to bound the difference
\bm{v}^{(a)}-\bm{v}^{(b)}
where
\bm{v}^{(a)},\bm{v}^{(b)}
satisfy

\displaystyle\textstyle(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{(a)}+\bm{V}^{(a)})[\bm{v}^{(a)}]=\bm{\varphi}=(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{(b)}+\bm{V}^{(b)})[\bm{v}^{(b)}]\,.

Rewriting, we have

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{(a)}-\bm{v}^{(b)}

\displaystyle=2(\mathcal{D}^{(b)}+\bm{V}^{(b)})[\bm{v}^{(b)}]-2(\mathcal{D}^{(a)}+\bm{V}^{(a)})[\bm{v}^{(a)}]

\displaystyle=2(\mathcal{D}^{(b)}+\bm{V}^{(b)}-\mathcal{D}^{(a)}-\bm{V}^{(a)})[\bm{v}^{(b)}]+2(\mathcal{D}^{(a)}+\bm{V}^{(a)})[\bm{v}^{(b)}-\bm{v}^{(a)}]\,,

or, moving all
\bm{v}^{(a)}-\bm{v}^{(b)}
terms to the left and side,

\displaystyle\textstyle\bm{v}^{(a)}-\bm{v}^{(b)}=\big(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{(a)}+\bm{V}^{(a)}\big)^{-1}\big(\mathcal{D}^{(b)}+\bm{V}^{(b)}-\mathcal{D}^{(a)}-\bm{V}^{(a)}\big)[\bm{v}^{(b)}]\,.

Then, using the single filament bound (
285
) and Lemma
5.2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{v}^{(a)}-\bm{v}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\left\lVert\big(\frac{1}{2}{\bf I}+\mathcal{D}^{(a)}+\bm{V}^{(a)}\big)^{-1}\big(\mathcal{D}^{(b)}+\bm{V}^{(b)}-\mathcal{D}^{(a)}-\bm{V}^{(a)}\big)[\bm{v}^{(b)}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\big(\mathcal{D}^{(b)}+\bm{V}^{(b)}-\mathcal{D}^{(a)}-\bm{V}^{(a)}\big)[\bm{v}^{(b)}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\gamma^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\gamma^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\gamma}}\,.

∎

5.3.
Proof of Lemma
5.5
: hypersingular operator bounds

We use the expansions (
205
) and (
211
) to write the kernel
\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T}}
(
278
) in terms of
s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta}
as

\displaystyle\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\big(\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},0}+\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},1}+\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},2}\big)\,,

\displaystyle\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},0}

\displaystyle=2\frac{\bm{e}_{r}\otimes\bm{e}_{r}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}+3\frac{(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r})\otimes(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

\displaystyle\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},1}

\displaystyle=-3\frac{\widehat{s}^{2}\big[\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\otimes(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r})+(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r})\otimes\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\big]}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

\displaystyle-2\frac{\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{r}\otimes\bm{Q}_{r}+2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{r}\otimes\bm{e}_{\theta}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}-3\frac{(2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn^{\prime}})(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r})\otimes\bm{e}_{r}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

\displaystyle\quad+3\frac{(2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn})\bm{e}_{r}\otimes(\widehat{s}\bm{e}_{\rm t}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}+\epsilon\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

\displaystyle\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},2}

\displaystyle=6\frac{\widehat{s}^{4}\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\otimes\bm{Q}_{\rm t}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}+3\frac{\widehat{s}^{2}\big[(2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn^{\prime}})\,\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\otimes\bm{e}_{r}-(2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn})\,\bm{e}_{r}\otimes\bm{Q}_{\rm t}\big]}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

\displaystyle\quad-3\frac{(2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn})\big[(2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn^{\prime}}){\bf I}+(\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{r}+2\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta})\otimes\bm{R}\big]}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{5}}

\displaystyle\quad-3\frac{\big[(2\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{0,5})\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{2}-10(2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})+\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn})(2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})-\widehat{s}^{2}Q_{\rm Rn^{\prime}})\big]\bm{R}\otimes\bm{R}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{7}}\,.

Accordingly, for
j=0,1,2
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{j}[\bm{\varphi}]=-{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},j}\,\big(\bm{\varphi}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\bm{\varphi}(s,\theta)\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})\,d\widehat{\theta}ds\,.

We begin with bounds for
\mathcal{T}_{2}
. Using Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{2}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\left\lvert\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},2}\right\rvert\,\left\lvert\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\right\rvert\,d\widehat{\theta}ds

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\frac{1+\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-2}}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert}\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}ds\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Furthermore, using that the remainder terms
Q_{i}
above each satisfy an estimate of the form (
213
), and using the expansion (
210
), for two curves
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\left\lvert\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},2}^{(a)}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}-\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},2}^{(b)}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\right\rvert\,d\widehat{\theta}ds

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

In addition, by Lemma
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{2}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Here we use case (2) of Lemma
4.4
to estimate terms of the form
\frac{\sin^{2}(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})}{\left\lvert\bm{R}\right\rvert^{3}}
, treating one factor of
\sin(\frac{\widehat{\theta}}{2})
as a coefficient, which allows us to save a factor of
\epsilon^{-\alpha}
. We may use case (1) to estimate the remaining terms.

We next estimate
\mathcal{T}_{1}[\bm{\varphi}]
. We may first use Lemma
4.3
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{1}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},1}\big(\bm{\varphi}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\bm{\varphi}(s,\theta)\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\,d\widehat{\theta}ds\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha-1}\big(\max_{i}\left\lVert Q_{i}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

As usual, here we use
Q_{i}
as catch-all notation for the remainder coefficients in the above expansion. Similarly, using the expressions (
213
) and (
210
), for two curves
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\big(\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},1}^{(a)}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}-\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},1}^{(b)}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\big)\big(\bm{\varphi}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\bm{\varphi}(s,\theta)\big)\,d\widehat{\theta}ds\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

We next turn to
\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}
estimates. Using Lemma
4.4
, case (2), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{1}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},1}\,\big(\bm{\varphi}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\bm{\varphi}(s,\theta)\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\,d\widehat{\theta}ds\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\max_{i}\big(\left\lVert Q_{i}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha^{+}}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{i}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Finally, we estimate
\mathcal{T}_{0}[\bm{\varphi}]
. Here we will use that
\bm{\varphi}\in C^{1,\alpha}
and we may thus expand

(300)

\displaystyle\bm{\varphi}(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\bm{\varphi}(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{\varphi,1}+\epsilon\widehat{\theta}\bm{Q}_{\varphi,2}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{Q}_{\varphi,1}\right\rVert_{Y}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{Y}\,,\quad\left\lVert\bm{Q}_{\varphi,2}\right\rVert_{Y}\leq\textstyle c\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{Y}\,,

where the norms
Y
are as in (
199
) with
\alpha
in place of
\beta
. Note that since
\bm{\varphi}
is a function of just
(s,\theta)
,
\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\mu}}\leq\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}
for
\mu=1,2
.
Using (
300
) along with Lemma
4.3
, we obtain the
L^{\infty}
estimates

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{0}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},0}\,\big(\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{\varphi,1}+\epsilon\widehat{\theta}\bm{Q}_{\varphi,2}\big)\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\,d\widehat{\theta}ds\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\big(\left\lVert\bm{Q}_{\varphi,1}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}+\left\lVert\bm{Q}_{\varphi,2}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)\,.

In addition, for two curves
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{0}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{T}_{0}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\big(\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},0}^{(a)}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}-\bm{K}_{\mathcal{T},0}^{(b)}\,\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}\big)\big(\widehat{s}\bm{Q}_{\varphi,1}+\epsilon\widehat{\theta}\bm{Q}_{\varphi,2}\big)\,d\widehat{\theta}ds\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\alpha}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}}\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\textstyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)\,.

Next, using Lemma
5.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{0}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\big(\textstyle\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\big)\displaystyle\max_{i}\big(\left\lVert Q_{i}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha^{+}}_{1}}+\left\lVert Q_{i}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\textstyle\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)\displaystyle\max_{i}\left\lVert Q_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\textstyle\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)+c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-\alpha^{+}}\big(\textstyle\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\big)\,.

Here the coefficients
Q_{i}
include
\epsilon^{-1}\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}
,
\epsilon\bm{Q}_{r}
, and
\bm{e}_{r},\bm{e}_{\theta}
, which incur an additional
\epsilon^{-\alpha^{+}}
due to
\theta
-dependence.
Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}_{0}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]-\mathcal{T}_{0}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\textstyle\big(\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+c(\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\,\epsilon^{-\alpha^{+}}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\big(\textstyle\left\lVert\partial_{s}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\big)\,.

In addition to the stress
\bm{\sigma}[\mathcal{D}[\bm{\varphi}]]\bm{n}(\bm{x})=\mathcal{T}_{0}+\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}
, we need to estimate the stress
\bm{\sigma}[\bm{V}[\bm{\varphi}]]\bm{n}(\bm{x})
due to the completion flow, given by (
280
).

Using that
dS_{x}=\mathcal{J}_{\epsilon}\,d\theta ds
and
\left\lvert\bm{R}_{x}\right\rvert\geq\epsilon
while
|\bm{x}^{\prime}-\bm{X}(s^{\prime})|\leq\epsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{\sigma}[\bm{V}[\bm{\varphi}]]\bm{n}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}\frac{\left\lvert\bm{\varphi}\right\rvert}{\epsilon^{2}}\,dS_{x^{\prime}}\leq c(\kappa_{*})\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{\sigma}[\bm{V}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]]\bm{n}^{(a)}-\bm{\sigma}[\bm{V}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]]\bm{n}^{(b)}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{\sigma}[\bm{V}[\bm{\varphi}]]\bm{n}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{\sigma}[\bm{V}^{(a)}[\bm{\varphi}]]\bm{n}^{(a)}-\bm{\sigma}[\bm{V}^{(b)}[\bm{\varphi}]]\bm{n}^{(b)}\right\rvert_{\dot{C}^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\,\epsilon^{-1-\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2}}\left\lVert\bm{\varphi}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Combining the estimates for
\bm{\sigma}[\bm{V}]
with the estimates for
\mathcal{T}_{0}
,
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
, we obtain Lemma
5.5
.
∎

6.
Slender body NtD in Hölder spaces

Here we prove Lemma
1.14
regarding Hölder space estimates for the slender body Neumann-to-Dirichlet map
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}
about a curved filament. As in the Laplace setting
[
59
, section 5]
, we will rely on a Campanato-type argument.

We begin by recalling the
L^{2}
-based solution theory for the slender body boundary value problem (
11
), developed in
[
52
, Theorem 1.2]
:

Lemma 6.1

(
L^{2}
-based SB BVP solution theory)
.

Consider a filament
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
with centerline
\bm{X}(s)\in C^{2}(\mathbb{T})
. Given slender body Neumann data
\bm{f}(s)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
, the solution
(\bm{u},p)
to the slender body boundary value problem (
11
) belongs to
D^{1,2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})\times L^{2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})
, where

\displaystyle D^{1,2}(\Omega_{\epsilon}):=\{\bm{u}\in L^{6}(\Omega_{\epsilon})\;:\;\nabla\bm{u}\in L^{2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})\}\,,

and satisfies the bound

(301)

\left\lVert\bm{u}\right\rVert_{D^{1,2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})}+\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})}\equiv\left\lVert\nabla\bm{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})}+\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\epsilon})}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{1/2}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

Recalling the definition (
6
) of
r_{*}=r_{*}(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})
, we may define the curved annular region

(302)

\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}=\big\{\bm{x}\in\Omega_{\epsilon}\;:\;\epsilon<\text{ dist}(\bm{x},\Gamma_{0})<r_{*}\big\}

about
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
. Within
\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}
, the solution
(\bm{u},p)
to (
11
) then satisfies

(303)

\left\lVert\bm{u}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})}+\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma},\left\lvert\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}\right\rvert)\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert^{1/2}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

We consider the weak form of the slender body BVP (
11
) in
\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}
, given by

(304)

\int_{\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}}\bigg(2\mathcal{E}(\bm{u}):\nabla\bm{\xi}-p\,{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{\xi})\bigg)\,d\bm{x}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{f}(s)\cdot{\bm{\xi}}(s)\,ds\,,\quad\mathcal{E}(\bm{u}):=\frac{1}{2}(\nabla\bm{u}+\nabla\bm{u}^{\rm T})\,,

for any
\bm{\xi}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
satisfying
\bm{\xi}\big|_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}=\bm{\xi}(s)
and
\bm{\xi}\big|_{\partial\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}\backslash\Gamma_{\epsilon}}=0
.

Writing
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}
as
\bm{x}=\bm{X}(s)+r\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)
, we define a
C^{1,\beta}
change of variables

\displaystyle\Psi(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=s\bm{e}_{z}+r\overline{\bm{e}}_{r}(\theta)=s\bm{e}_{z}+r\cos\theta\,\bm{e}_{x}+r\sin\theta\,\bm{e}_{y}

mapping the curved annulus
\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}
to the straight annulus
\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
about the straight cylinder
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
. Here
(\bm{e}_{z},\bm{e}_{x},\bm{e}_{y})
are the standard Cartesian basis vectors. We may calculate

(305)

\nabla\Psi^{-1}=(1-r\widehat{\kappa})\bm{e}_{\rm t}\otimes\bm{e}_{z}+r\kappa_{3}\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)\otimes\bm{e}_{z}+\bm{e}_{\theta}(s,\theta)\otimes\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta}(\theta)+\bm{e}_{r}(s,\theta)\otimes\overline{\bm{e}}_{r}(\theta)\,,

as well as

(306)

\displaystyle\nabla\Psi(\nabla\Psi)^{\rm T}\circ\Psi^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+r^{2}\kappa_{3}^{2}}{(1-r\widehat{\kappa})^{2}}\bm{e}_{z}\otimes\bm{e}_{z}-\frac{r\kappa_{3}}{1-r\widehat{\kappa}}\big(\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta}(\theta)\otimes\bm{e}_{z}+\bm{e}_{z}\otimes\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta}(\theta)\big)

\displaystyle+\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta}(\theta)\otimes\overline{\bm{e}}_{\theta}(\theta)+\overline{\bm{e}}_{r}(\theta)\otimes\overline{\bm{e}}_{r}(\theta)

and
\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert^{-1}=1-r\widehat{\kappa}
.
We denote

(307)

\bm{A}(s,r,\theta)=\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert}\nabla\Psi(\nabla\Psi)^{\rm T}\bigg)\circ\Psi^{-1}\,,\qquad\bm{B}(s,r,\theta)=\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert}\nabla\Psi\bigg)\circ\Psi^{-1}\,,

and note that, using the additional
C^{3,\alpha}
regularity of
\bm{X}
, we have

(308)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c(\kappa_{*,\beta})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}(\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}))}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta})\,,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c(\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{B}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}))}\leq c(\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}})\,,

with smooth dependence on
\theta
.
Here we have used that, using (
3
), we may write

\displaystyle(\kappa_{1})_{s}=\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}+\kappa_{2}^{2}\,,\quad(\kappa_{2})_{s}=\bm{X}_{sss}\cdot\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}+\kappa_{1}^{2}\,,

from which we may obtain
\left\lVert\kappa_{1}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}\,,\,\left\lVert\kappa_{2}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}\leq c\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}
.

Let
\bm{\psi}=\bm{\xi}\circ\Psi^{-1}
and note that along the fiber surface,
\bm{\psi}\big|_{\bm{y}\in\Psi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})}=\bm{\xi}\big|_{\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{\epsilon}}
is still
\theta
-independent.
Letting
\overline{\bm{u}}=\bm{u}\circ\Psi^{-1}
and
\overline{p}=p\circ\Psi^{-1}
,
we may rewrite (
304
) in straightened variables
\bm{y}=\Psi(\bm{x})
as

(309)

\int_{\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})}\bigg((\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}\bm{A}+{\bm{B}}^{\rm T}\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi^{\rm T}):\nabla\bm{\psi}-\overline{p}\,{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}\bm{\psi})\bigg)\,d\bm{y}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{f}(s)\cdot{\bm{\psi}}(s)\,ds\,.

We will use the formulation (
309
) along with the analogous Campanato-type argument used in
[
59
]
to show that
\bm{u}=\overline{\bm{u}}\circ\Psi
satisfies Lemma
1.14
along
\Gamma_{\epsilon}
.

We will make use of a Campanato-type function space tailored to the slender body BVP, which was defined in
[
59
]
following the more classical constructions in
[
28
,
48
]
.
Given
\rho\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
, we define the following annular region about the straight cylinder
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
:

\displaystyle A_{\rho}(s_{0})

\displaystyle=\big\{\bm{y}=s\bm{e}_{z}+r\bm{e}_{r}(\theta)\in\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})\;:\;s_{0}-\rho<s<s_{0}+\rho\,,\;\epsilon\leq r<\rho+\epsilon\,,\;0\leq\theta<2\pi\big\}\,.

We emphasize that the volume of the region
A_{\rho}(s_{0})
scales like
\rho^{2}
as
\rho\to 0
.
Given
\bm{g}(\bm{y})
,
\bm{y}\in A_{\rho}(s_{0})
, we use the notation
\bm{g}_{s_{0},\rho}
to denote the mean of
\bm{g}
over
A_{\rho}(s_{0})
:

(310)

\bm{g}_{s_{0},\rho}=\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\bm{g}(\bm{y})\,d\bm{y}\,.

For
0<\alpha<1
, we define the seminorm
[\cdot]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}
and full norm
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}
by

(311)

\displaystyle\big[\bm{g}\big]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{s_{0}\in\mathbb{T},\,0<\rho\leq r_{*}/2}\rho^{-\alpha}\bigg(\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\bm{g}-\bm{g}_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\bigg)^{1/2}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}))}+\big[\bm{g}\big]_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}\,.

We may relate the function space defined by the norm
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}
to the Hölder space
C^{0,\alpha}
about the straight filament
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
in the following way.

Lemma 6.2

(Campanato-type norm bounds)
.

Consider
\bm{g}
in
\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
satisfying
\bm{g}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=\bm{g}(s)
, a function of arclength only.
Then

(312)

\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq c\,\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}}\leq c\,\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}))}\,.

Here the left-hand-side
C^{0,\alpha}
norm is taken over the filament centerline (
s\in\mathbb{T}
), while the right-hand-side
C^{0,\alpha}
norm is over the entire annular region
\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
.

The proof of Lemma
6.2
is given in
[
59
, Lemma 5.2]
.

We next consider a frozen-coefficients version of the slender body BVP (
309
). Taking
0<R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
, we consider the annular region
A_{R}(s_{0})
about
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
. Let
\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)=\bm{A}(s_{0},\epsilon,\theta)
,
\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)=\bm{B}(s_{0},\epsilon,\theta)
, and
\nabla\Psi_{0}(\theta)=\nabla\Psi(s_{0},\epsilon,\theta)
, and note that
\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)
,
\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)
, and
\nabla\Psi_{0}(\theta)
are smooth functions of
\theta
satisfying
\frac{1}{c(\kappa_{*})}\leq\left\lvert\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}{\bm{A}}_{0}\right\rvert,\left\lvert\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}{\bm{B}}_{0}\right\rvert,\left\lvert\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert\leq c(\kappa_{*})
for
c(\kappa_{*})>0
and
0\leq\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We consider
({\bm{h}},q)
belonging to the space

\displaystyle\bigg\{({\bm{h}},q)\in H^{1}\times L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))\,:\;{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)\bm{h})=0\,,\;\bm{h}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=\bm{h}(s)\,,\;\bm{h}\big|_{\partial A_{R}\backslash\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=\bm{u}\,,\;\int_{A_{R}}q\,d\bm{y}=0\bigg\}

and satisfying

(313)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg((\nabla\bm{h}\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)+\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)^{\rm T}\nabla\bm{h}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}(\theta)^{\rm T})

\displaystyle:\nabla\bm{\psi}-q\,{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)\bm{\psi})\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle=\bm{f}(s_{0})\cdot\int_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert\leq R}\bm{\psi}(s)\,ds

for any
\bm{\psi}\in H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
with
\bm{\psi}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=\bm{\psi}(s)
and
\bm{\psi}\big|_{\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=0
.
We may show that
(\bm{h},q)
satisfies the following proposition.

Proposition 6.3

(Higher regularity for
(\bm{h},q)
)
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
and any
0<\rho<R
, the solution
(\bm{h},q)
to (
313
) satisfies

(314)

\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{m+1}(A_{\rho}(s_{0}))}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{m}(A_{\rho}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\frac{1}{(R-\rho)^{m}}\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\big)\,.

Proof.

Fixing
R^{\prime}<R
, we begin by defining a cutoff function

(315)

\eta(\bm{x})=\begin{cases}1\,,&\bm{x}\in A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})\\
0\,,&\bm{x}\not\in A_{R}(s_{0})\,,\end{cases}\quad R^{\prime}<R\,,

with
\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{1}{R-R^{\prime}}
.
We would like to use
\partial_{k}(\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{h})
,
k=s,\theta
as test functions in (
313
) (this may be justified rigorously using finite differences), but first it will be convenient to correct for the divergence.
For
k=s,\theta
, we consider
\bm{d}_{k}\in H^{1}_{0}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
satisfying

(316)

\displaystyle{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{d}_{k})

\displaystyle={\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{h})\qquad\text{in }A_{R}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla\bm{d}_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{h})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,.

Such a
\bm{d}_{k}
exists by
[
24
, Section III.3]
, since
\bm{B}_{0}
is invertible. In addition, the constant appearing in the bound (
316
) is independent of
\epsilon
due to
[
52
, Appendix A.2.5]
, although we will not keep track of
\epsilon
-dependence throughout.
We further note that, since
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\bm{h})=0
in
A_{R}(s_{0})
, and
{\rm{div}\,}
commutes with
\partial_{s}
and
\partial_{\theta}
in cylindrical coordinates, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{h})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\eta^{2})\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}+\left\lVert\eta^{2}{\rm{div}\,}((\partial_{k}\bm{B}_{0})\bm{h})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\eta^{2}\partial_{k}{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\bm{h})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*})\left\lVert\eta\,\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,,

so, in particular, for
k=s,\theta
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla\bm{d}_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\eta\,\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,.

We then take
\partial_{k}(\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{h}-\bm{d}_{k})
,
k=s,\theta
, as a test function in (
313
). We have

(317)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg((\nabla\bm{h}\bm{A}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\bm{h}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T})

\displaystyle:\nabla\big(\partial_{k}(\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{h}-\bm{d}_{k})\big)-q\,{\rm{div}\,}\big(\bm{B}_{0}\partial_{k}(\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{h}-\bm{d}_{k})\big)\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle=\bm{f}(s_{0})\cdot\int_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert\leq R}\partial_{k}\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{h}(s)\,ds=0\,.

Before proceeding, we note that, using the expressions (
305
)-(
307
) for
\bm{A}
,
\bm{B}
, and
\nabla\Psi
, for any
\bm{v}\in L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
, we may write

(318)

\displaystyle(\nabla\bm{v}\bm{A}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\bm{v}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T})=(\nabla\bm{v}+\nabla\bm{v}^{\rm T})-\bm{E}_{0}(\nabla\bm{v})\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{E}_{0}\right\rvert\leq 2\max\bigg\{\left\lvert\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s_{0},\theta)\right\rvert,\left\lvert\epsilon\kappa_{3}\right\rvert\,,\frac{\left\lvert\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s_{0},\theta)\right\rvert+\epsilon^{2}\kappa_{3}^{2}}{\left\lvert 1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}\right\rvert(s_{0},\theta)}\bigg\}\left\lvert\nabla\bm{v}\right\rvert\leq\epsilon c(\kappa_{*})\left\lvert\nabla\bm{v}\right\rvert\,.

Furthermore, for any
\bm{v}
satisfying
\bm{v}=0
on
\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
, we may use the homogeneous Korn inequality in
A_{R}(s_{0})
to bound

(319)

\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\bm{v}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\mathcal{E}(\bm{v})\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,,\qquad\mathcal{E}(\bm{v})=\frac{1}{2}(\nabla\bm{v}+\nabla\bm{v}^{\rm T})\,,

where, by
[
52
, Lemma 2.6]
,
c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})
is independent of
\epsilon
.

Applying the above bounds, we may estimate
\eta\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}
,
k=s,\theta
, in
A_{R}(s_{0})
as

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla(\eta\partial_{k}\bm{h})\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\mathcal{E}(\eta\partial_{k}\bm{h})\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}\left\lvert\mathcal{E}(\partial_{k}\bm{h})\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\quad=c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}\mathcal{E}(\partial_{k}\bm{h}):\nabla(\partial_{k}\bm{h})\,d\bm{y}+c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\quad=c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}(\nabla(\partial_{k}\bm{h})\bm{A}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla(\partial_{k}\bm{h})\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}):\nabla(\partial_{k}\bm{h})\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\qquad+c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}\bm{E}_{0}(\partial_{k}\bm{h}):\nabla(\partial_{k}\bm{h})\,d\bm{y}+c(\Gamma,\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

For
\epsilon
sufficiently small (depending on
c_{\Gamma}
and
\kappa_{*}
), the middle term may be absorbed into the left hand side to yield

(320)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\underbrace{\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}(\nabla(\partial_{k}\bm{h})\bm{A}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla(\partial_{k}\bm{h})\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}):\nabla(\partial_{k}\bm{h})\,d\bm{y}}_{=:J_{0}}

\displaystyle+c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

To estimate
\eta\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}
in
A_{R}(s_{0})
, we will thus require a bound for the integral
J_{0}
, which we may obtain from (
317
) after some rearrangement.

Again, since we are using cylindrical coordinates,
\partial_{s}
and
\partial_{\theta}
commute with
\nabla
and
{\rm{div}\,}
, but we pick up additional terms in moving
\partial_{\theta}
throughout the coefficients
\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)
,
\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)
, and
\nabla\Psi_{0}(\theta)
. After commuting, integrating by parts, and using that
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}(\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{h}-\bm{d}_{k}))=0
for
k=s,\theta
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert J_{0}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg((\left\lvert\bm{A}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert)\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert\big(\left\lvert\nabla(\eta^{2})\right\rvert\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\nabla\bm{d}_{k}\right\rvert\big)

\displaystyle\quad+(\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{A}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\partial_{k}\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert)\left\lvert\nabla\bm{h}\right\rvert\big(\eta^{2}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\nabla(\eta^{2})\right\rvert\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\nabla\bm{d}_{k}\right\rvert\big)\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert q\right\rvert\bigg(\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert(\eta^{2}\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{d}_{k}\right\rvert)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert(\eta^{2}|\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}|+\left\lvert\nabla(\eta^{2})\right\rvert|\partial_{k}\bm{h}|+\left\lvert\nabla\bm{d}_{k}\right\rvert)\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})(R-R^{\prime})^{-1}\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}\bigg)\bigg(\left\lVert\eta\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert\eta\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}\bigg)\,,

since
\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{1}{R-R^{\prime}}
.
Combining with (
320
) and using Young’s inequality, over the smaller annulus
A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})
, we obtain the bounds

(321)

\left\lVert\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon,c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})(R-R^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\big)\,,\qquad k=s,\theta\,.

We next consider tangential derivatives
\partial_{k}q
,
k=s,\theta
, of the frozen-coefficient pressure
q
. We consider
\bm{g}_{k}\in H^{1}_{0}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
satisfying

(322)

\displaystyle{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{g}_{k})

\displaystyle=\partial_{k}(\eta^{2}q)\qquad\text{in }A_{R}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla\bm{g}_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\partial_{k}(\eta^{2}q)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}

Again, this may be justified by finite differences, and, since
\partial_{k}(\eta^{2}q)
has mean zero on
A_{R}(s_{0})
and
\bm{B}_{0}
is invertible, such a
\bm{g}_{k}
exists by
[
24
, Section III.3]
.

We now take
\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{g}_{k}
,
k=s,\theta
, as test functions in (
313
):

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg((\nabla\bm{h}\bm{A}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\bm{h}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}):\nabla(\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{g}_{k})-q\,{\rm{div}\,}\big(\bm{B}_{0}(\eta^{2}\partial_{k}\bm{g}_{k})\big)\bigg)\,d\bm{y}=0\,.

Commuting
\partial_{k}
through the equations (and noting that this picks up a factor of
\partial_{\theta}\bm{A}_{0}
,
\partial_{\theta}\bm{B}_{0}
, and
\partial_{\theta}\nabla\Psi_{0}
), after integrating by parts and using the definition (
322
) of
\bm{g}_{k}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{k}(\eta^{2}q)\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}(\left\lvert\bm{A}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert)\bigg(2\left\lvert\nabla\bm{h}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\bm{g}_{k}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla(\eta^{2})\right\rvert+\eta^{2}\left\lvert\nabla\bm{g}_{k}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rvert\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}\left\lvert\nabla\bm{g}_{k}\right\rvert\big(\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{A}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\partial_{k}\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert\big)\left\lvert\nabla\bm{h}\right\rvert\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert q\right\rvert\bigg(\eta^{2}\big(\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{g}_{k}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\bm{g}_{k}\right\rvert\big)+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\partial_{k}\bm{g}_{k}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla(\eta^{2})\right\rvert\bigg)\,d\bm{y}\,.

Using Young’s inequality and the bound (
322
), and recalling that
\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert\lesssim\frac{1}{R-R^{\prime}}
, over the smaller annulus
A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})
, we then have

(323)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{k}q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\big(\left\lVert\eta\nabla\partial_{k}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}+(R-R^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})(R-R^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}\big)\,.

for
k=s,\theta
.
Note that for
\partial_{s}q
, instead of (
322
), we may instead consider
\bm{g}_{s}\in H^{1}_{0}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))
satisfying

(324)

\displaystyle{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{g}_{s})

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\qquad\text{in }A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla\bm{g}_{s}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))}

where
(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}
is given by (
310
). Using
\partial_{s}\bm{g}_{s}
as our test function in (
313
), we may then estimate

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}(\left\lvert\bm{A}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert)\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\bm{g}_{s}\right\rvert\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\left\lVert\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\,.

In particular, over
A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})
, we may estimate

(325)

\left\lVert\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\,.

We now turn to the normal direction derivatives
(\partial_{rr}\bm{h},\partial_{r}q)
, for which we must use the strong form of the equations (
313
) within
A_{R}(s_{0})
.
First, using the form (
307
) of
\bm{B}_{0}=\bm{B}(s_{0},\epsilon,\theta)
and
\nabla\Psi
(
305
), we may write the condition
\partial_{r}{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\bm{h})=0
in cylindrical coordinates as

\displaystyle\partial_{rr}h_{r}=-\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert}\bigg(\frac{1}{r}h_{r}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\big(h_{\theta}+a_{1}(\theta)h_{z}\big)+a_{2}(\theta)\partial_{s}h_{z}\bigg)\bigg)

where
a_{1}(\theta)=-\frac{\epsilon\kappa_{3}}{1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s_{0},\theta)}
and
a_{2}(\theta)=\frac{1}{1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s_{0},\theta)}
.
In particular, by (
321
) and the boundedness of
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{rr}h_{r}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})(R-R^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}\big)\,.

Bounds for the remaining directions
\partial_{rr}(\bm{h}\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta})
,
\partial_{rr}(\bm{h}\cdot\bm{e}_{z})
, and
\partial_{r}q
come from the form of the frozen-coefficient straightened Stokes equations in
A_{R}(s_{0})
. We have that
(\bm{h},q)
satisfies

(326)

{\rm{div}\,}(\nabla\bm{h}\bm{A}_{0})+\bm{F}(\nabla\bm{h})+\bm{G}(\bm{h})=\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla q\qquad\text{in }A_{R}(s_{0})\,.

Here the lower order terms
\bm{F}
and
\bm{G}
arise from the frozen-coefficient symmetric gradient term
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\bm{h}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T})
and the divergence-free condition
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\bm{h})=0
.
Using the form (
307
) of
\bm{A}_{0}=\bm{A}(s_{0},\epsilon,\theta)
and
\bm{B}_{0}
, we may write the normal direction components of the equation (
326
) as

\displaystyle\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert{\rm{div}\,}(\nabla\bm{h}\bm{A}_{0})\cdot\bm{e}_{r}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}(r\partial_{r}h_{r})+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}(\partial_{\theta}h_{r}-h_{\theta})+\partial_{ss}h_{r}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert(\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla q)\cdot\bm{e}_{r}

\displaystyle=\partial_{r}q\,,

from which we may bound
\partial_{r}q
by
\partial_{rr}h_{r}
,
\partial_{\theta\theta}h_{r}
,
\partial_{ss}h_{r}
,
\nabla\bm{h}
, and
\bm{h}
to obtain a full gradient bound for
q
:

(327)

\left\lVert\nabla q\right\rVert_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})(R-R^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}\big)\,.

In the
\bm{e}_{\theta}
,
\bm{e}_{z}
directions, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert{\rm{div}\,}(\nabla\bm{h}\bm{A}_{0})\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}(r(\partial_{r}h_{\theta}+a_{1}\partial_{r}h_{z})+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}(\partial_{\theta}h_{\theta}+h_{r}+a_{1}\partial_{s}h_{z})+\partial_{ss}(h_{\theta}+a_{1}h_{z})\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert(\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla q)\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}q\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert{\rm{div}\,}(\nabla\bm{h}\bm{A}_{0})\cdot\bm{e}_{r}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}(r(a_{1}\partial_{r}h_{\theta}+a_{3}\partial_{r}h_{z})+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}(a_{1}\partial_{\theta}h_{\theta}+a_{1}h_{r}+a_{3}\partial_{s}h_{z})+\partial_{ss}(a_{1}h_{\theta}+a_{3}h_{z})\,,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\det\nabla\Psi\right\rvert(\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla q)\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=a_{2}\partial_{s}q+a_{1}\partial_{\theta}q\,,

where
a_{1}(\theta)
and
a_{2}(\theta)
are as above and
a_{3}(\theta)=\frac{1+\epsilon^{2}\kappa_{3}^{2}}{(1-\epsilon\widehat{\kappa}(s_{0},\theta))^{2}}
. Using (
326
), we obtain a system of two equations for the unknown directions
\partial_{rr}h_{\theta}
and
\partial_{rr}h_{z}
in terms of the remaining directions. Together, we may thus bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{rr}h_{\theta}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\,,\left\lVert\partial_{rr}h_{z}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})(R-R^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}\big)\,.

Combining the tangential regularity bound (
321
) with the pressure gradient bound (
327
) and the bounds for
\partial_{rr}\bm{h}
, we obtain the full
H^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))\times H^{1}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))
bound

(328)

\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})(R-R^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\big)\,.

This procedure may be iterated over smaller and smaller annuli, using test functions of the form
\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}(\eta_{1}^{2}(\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\bm{h}))
,
\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\partial_{m}(\eta_{2}^{2}(\partial_{k}\partial_{\ell}\partial_{m}\bm{h}))
, etc., where
\eta_{j}
are appropriate cutoffs and similar corrections for
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\cdot)
-freeness are made.
We thus obtain Proposition
6.3
.
∎

Recalling the notation
\bm{g}_{s_{0},R}
for the mean of a function
\bm{g}
over the annular region
A_{R}(s_{0})
, we may now use Proposition
6.3
to show that the solution
(\bm{h},q)
to (
313
) satisfies the following.

Corollary 6.4

(Caccioppoli inequality)
.

For
0<R^{\prime}<R
, the solution
(\bm{h},q)
to (
313
) satisfies

(329)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\frac{1}{(R-R^{\prime})^{2}}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{h}-(\partial_{s}\bm{h})_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

Proof.

Using that the coefficients
\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)
,
\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)
,
\nabla\Psi_{0}(\theta)
are independent of
s
, for
(\bm{h},q)
satisfying (
313
), we have that
(\partial_{s}\bm{h},\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R})
satisfies

(330)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg((\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)

\displaystyle+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}(\theta)(\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h})^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}):\nabla\bm{\psi}

\displaystyle-(\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R})\,{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)\bm{\psi})\bigg)\,d\bm{y}=0

for any
\bm{\psi}\in H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
with
\bm{\psi}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=\bm{\psi}(s)
and
\bm{\psi}\big|_{\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=0
. Furthermore, since adding a constant does not affect the
\theta
-independence of
\partial_{s}\bm{h}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}
, we have that
(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda},\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R})
also satisfies (
330
) for any constant
\bm{\lambda}
.

For
