0<R^{\prime}<R
, taking
\eta
to be a cutoff function as in (
315
), we will use
\bm{\psi}=\eta^{2}(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})
as a test function in (
330
) with
(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda},\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R})
. We then have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg(\big(\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}(\theta)\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}\big):\nabla\big(\eta^{2}(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-(\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R})\,{\rm{div}\,}\big(\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)\eta^{2}(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\big)\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg(\eta^{2}\big(\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}(\theta)\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}\big):\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\bm{A}_{0}(\theta)+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}(\theta)\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}\big):\big(\nabla(\eta^{2})\otimes(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\eta^{2}(\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R})\,{\rm{div}\,}\big(\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\big)-(\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R})\,\nabla(\eta^{2})\cdot\big(\bm{B}_{0}(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\big)\bigg)\,d\bm{y}\,.

Since
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}(\theta)(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda}))=0
, after using an analogous argument to (
320
) to bound
\eta\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})
by the first term in the final expression above, we may estimate

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\eta^{2}\left\lvert\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\eta\right\rvert\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda}\right\rvert\bigg(\left\lvert\eta\nabla(\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda})\right\rvert+\left\lvert\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert\bigg)\,d\bm{y}\,.

Using Young’s inequality, the support of
\eta
, and
\nabla\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{\left\lvert R-R^{\prime}\right\rvert}
from (
315
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c(\delta)c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})}{(R-R^{\prime})^{2}}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+\delta c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

for any small
\delta>0
. Using the estimate (
325
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,;

so, in particular,

(331)

\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq\frac{c(\delta)c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})}{(R-R^{\prime})^{2}}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{h}-\bm{\lambda}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+\delta c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

We now recall the following useful proposition.

Proposition 6.5

(Lemma 6.1 from
[
29
]
)
.

Let
Z(\rho)
be a bounded, nonnegative function in the interval
[R_{1},R_{2}]
. If for
R_{1}\leq R^{\prime}\leq R\leq R_{2}
we have

\displaystyle Z(R^{\prime})\leq M(R-R^{\prime})^{-m}+\delta Z(R)

for
M,m>0
and
0\leq\delta<1
, then

(332)

Z(R_{1})\leq c(m,\delta)M(R_{2}-R_{1})^{-m}\,.

Taking
\delta
sufficiently small, we may apply Proposition
6.5
to (
331
) with
Z(\rho)=\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}
. Taking
\bm{\lambda}=(\partial_{s}\bm{h})_{s_{0},R}
, we thus obtain (
329
).
∎

Finally, using Proposition
6.3
and Corollary
6.4
, we show that oscillation of
\partial_{s}\bm{h}
satisfies the following scaling.

Lemma 6.6

(Oscillation of
\partial_{s}\bm{h}
)
.

Consider
(\bm{h},q)
satisfying (
313
). For
0<\rho<R/2
, we have that
\partial_{s}\bm{h}
satisfies

(333)

\displaystyle\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{h}-(\partial_{s}\bm{h})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}\fint_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{h}-(\partial_{s}\bm{h})_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

Proof.

Using a Poincaré inequality over
A_{\rho}(s_{0})
, Sobolev embedding, Proposition
6.3
, estimate (
325
), and Corollary
6.4
, we may estimate

\displaystyle\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\partial_{s}{\bm{h}}-(\partial_{s}\bm{h})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon)\,\rho^{2}\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\,\rho^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bigg(\sup_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert\leq\rho,\,0\leq r\leq\rho}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}{\bm{h}}\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,d\theta

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\,\rho^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bigg(\sup_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert\leq R/2,\,0\leq r\leq R/2}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}{\bm{h}}\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,d\theta

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\,\rho^{2}\big(R^{-2}\left\lVert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R/2}(s_{0}))}^{2}+R^{2}\left\lVert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{2}(A_{R/2}(s_{0}))}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\rho^{2}\fint_{A_{3R/4}(s_{0})}\bigg(\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},3R/4}\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\,\rho^{2}\fint_{A_{3R/4}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\partial_{s}\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}\fint_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{h}-(\partial_{s}\bm{h})_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\,.

In the fourth inequality we have used scaling along each
(s,r)
slice in the annulus
A_{R/2}(s_{0})
to obtain the
R
-dependence of the Sobolev inequality. In the fifth inequality we have used that
(\partial_{s}\bm{h},\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},3R/4})
satisfies (
313
) with zero boundary data, and we may thus apply the bound from Proposition
6.3
at the level of
(\partial_{s}\bm{h},\partial_{s}q-(\partial_{s}q)_{s_{0},3R/4})
.
∎

Equipped with Lemmas
6.2
and
6.6
, we may proceed to the proof of Lemma
1.14
.

Proof of Lemma
1.14
.

Consider the weak solution
(\overline{\bm{u}},\overline{p})
to the slender body BVP satisfying (
304
) within the straightened region
\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
. Let
0<R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
, and consider the annular region
A_{R}(s_{0})\subset\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
.

Within
A_{R}(s_{0})
, we decompose
(\overline{\bm{u}},\overline{p})
as
\overline{\bm{u}}=\bm{h}+\bm{b}
and
\overline{p}=q+\zeta
, where
(\bm{h},q)
satisfies the frozen-coefficient slender body PDE (
313
).
Rewriting (
304
) as

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}}\big(\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}({\bm{A}}-{\bm{A}}_{0})+({\bm{B}}-{\bm{B}}_{0})^{\rm T}\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi^{\rm T}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}^{\rm T}(\nabla\Psi-\nabla\Psi_{0})^{\rm T}\big):\nabla\bm{\psi}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{A_{R}}\big((\nabla\bm{h}+\nabla\bm{b}){\bm{A}}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}(\nabla\bm{h}+\nabla\bm{b})^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}\big):\nabla\bm{\psi}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle-\int_{A_{R}}(\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R})\,{\rm{div}\,}((\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0})\bm{\psi})\,d\bm{y}-\int_{A_{R}}(q+\zeta-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R})\,{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\bm{\psi})\,d\bm{y}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bm{f}(s)\bm{\psi}(s)\,ds

for any
\bm{\psi}
with
\bm{\psi}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=\bm{\psi}(s)
and
\bm{\psi}\big|_{\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=0
, we may use that
(\bm{h},q)
satisfies (
313
) to obtain an equation for
{\bm{b}}
:

(334)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}}\big(\nabla\bm{b}{\bm{A}}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\bm{b}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}\big):\nabla\bm{\psi}\,d\bm{y}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(\bm{f}(s)-\bm{f}(s_{0})\big)\bm{\psi}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle-\int_{A_{R}}\big(\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}({\bm{A}}-{\bm{A}}_{0})+(\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0})^{\rm T}\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi^{\rm T}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}^{\rm T}(\nabla\Psi-\nabla\Psi_{0})^{\rm T}\big):\nabla\bm{\psi}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{A_{R}}(\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R})\,{\rm{div}\,}((\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0})\bm{\psi})\,d\bm{y}+\int_{A_{R}}(\zeta-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R})\,{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\bm{\psi})\,d\bm{y}\,.

If, in addition, we require that the test function
\bm{\psi}
satisfies
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\bm{\psi})=0
, we obtain the equation

(335)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}}\big(\nabla\bm{b}{\bm{A}}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\bm{b}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}\big):\nabla\bm{\psi}\,d\bm{y}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(\bm{f}(s)-\bm{f}(s_{0})\big)\bm{\psi}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle-\int_{A_{R}}\big(\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}({\bm{A}}-{\bm{A}}_{0})+(\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0})^{\rm T}\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi^{\rm T}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}^{\rm T}(\nabla\Psi-\nabla\Psi_{0})^{\rm T}\big):\nabla\bm{\psi}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle+\int_{A_{R}}(\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R})\,{\rm{div}\,}((\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0})\bm{\psi})\,d\bm{y}\,.

Noting that
\bm{b}=\overline{\bm{u}}-\bm{h}
already satisfies the boundary conditions
\bm{b}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=\bm{b}(s)
and
\bm{b}\big|_{\partial A_{R}(s_{0})\backslash\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}=0
, we would like to use
\bm{b}
as a test function above. It will be convenient to use (
335
), so we must first correct for the divergence. We consider
\bm{g}\in H^{1}_{0}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
satisfying

(336)

\displaystyle{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{g})

\displaystyle={\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{b})\qquad\text{in }A_{R}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{b})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,.

As before, such a
\bm{g}
exists due to
[
24
, Section III.3]
and
[
52
, Appendix A.2.5]
. Using that
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}\,\overline{\bm{u}})=0
and
{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{h})=0
in
A_{R}(s_{0})
, we may write

\displaystyle{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{b})=-{\rm{div}\,}((\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0})\,\overline{\bm{u}})\,,

so, making use of the extra regularity assumption (
308
) on the coefficient
\bm{B}
, we have

(337)

\left\lVert\nabla\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{b})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\,R^{\alpha}\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,.

We may now use
\bm{b}-\bm{g}
as a test function in (
335
). We first note that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{A_{R}}\big(\nabla\bm{b}{\bm{A}}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\bm{b}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}\big):\nabla\bm{b}\,d\bm{y}\right\rvert\leq\int_{A_{R}}(\left\lvert{\bm{A}}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert)\left\lvert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\bm{g}\right\rvert\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{A_{R}}(\left\lvert{\bm{A}}-{\bm{A}}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi-\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert)\left\lvert\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rvert(\left\lvert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\nabla\bm{g}\right\rvert)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{A_{R}}\left\lvert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert\big(\left\lvert\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\nabla(\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0})\right\rvert\big)\big(\left\lvert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\nabla\bm{g}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{b}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{g}\right\rvert\big)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left\lvert\bm{f}(s)-\bm{f}(s_{0})\right\rvert\left\lvert\bm{b}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}\right\rvert\,ds\,.

Using the decomposition (
318
) and the homogeneous Korn inequality (
319
), we may bound

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}}\left\lvert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(c_{\Gamma},\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R}}\big(\nabla\bm{b}{\bm{A}}_{0}+\bm{B}_{0}^{\rm T}\nabla\bm{b}^{\rm T}\nabla\Psi_{0}^{\rm T}\big):\nabla\bm{b}\,d\bm{y}\,.

Then, using the regularity of the coefficients
\bm{A}
,
\bm{B}
, and
\nabla\Psi
as well as the bound (
337
) for
\bm{g}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}}\left\lvert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(R^{\alpha}\left\lVert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+R^{2\alpha}\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-45.52458pt\quad+R^{\alpha}\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}(\left\lVert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}+R^{\alpha}\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})})+R^{1/2+\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{b}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|s-s_{0}|<R)}\bigg)\,.

By a scaling argument (see
[
59
, proof of Lemma 1.8]
), the following trace inequality holds in
A_{R}(s_{0})
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{b}\big|_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(|s-s_{0}|<R)}^{2}\leq c(\epsilon)\,R\left\lVert\nabla{\bm{b}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}^{2}\,.

Using Young’s inequality, we thus obtain the bound

(338)

\int_{A_{R}}\left\lvert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\,R^{2\alpha}\bigg(\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}+R^{2}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\bigg)\,.

Returning to the equation (
334
) for
\bm{b}
, we may estimate the pressure term
\zeta-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}
by choosing a test function
\bm{\psi}\in H^{1}_{0}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
satisfying

\displaystyle{\rm{div}\,}(\bm{B}_{0}\bm{\psi})

\displaystyle=\zeta-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\qquad\text{in }A_{R}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla\bm{\psi}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\zeta-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}\,.

Using this
\bm{\psi}
in (
334
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\zeta-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg((\left\lvert{\bm{A}}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert)\left\lvert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\bm{\psi}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert\left\lvert{\rm{div}\,}((\bm{B}-\bm{B}_{0})\bm{\psi})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+(\left\lvert{\bm{A}}-{\bm{A}}_{0}\right\rvert+\left\lvert{\bm{B}}-{\bm{B}}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{B}_{0}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\Psi-\nabla\Psi_{0}\right\rvert)\left\lvert\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rvert\left\lvert\nabla\bm{\psi}\right\rvert\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\leq c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\left\lVert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}+R^{2\alpha}\left\lVert\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}+R^{2\alpha}\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}\bigg)\,.

Combining with (
338
), we have

(339)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\zeta-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(R^{2\alpha}\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+R^{2\alpha}\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}+R^{2+2\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\bigg)\,.

Next, using Proposition
6.3
and recalling that
A_{\rho}
scales like
\rho^{2}
for small
\rho
, for
\rho<R/2
, we have that
\bm{h}=\overline{\bm{u}}-\bm{b}
and
q=\overline{p}-\zeta
satisfy

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\bigg(\left\lvert\nabla\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert q\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,d\bm{y}\leq c\,\rho^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\sup_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert\leq\rho,\,0\leq r\leq\rho}\bigg(\left\lvert\nabla\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert q\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,d\theta

\displaystyle\quad\leq c\,\rho^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\sup_{\left\lvert s-s_{0}\right\rvert\leq R/2,\,0\leq r\leq R/2}\bigg(\left\lvert\nabla\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\bm{h}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert q\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,d\theta

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},R)\,\rho^{2}\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{3}(A_{R/2}(s_{0}))}^{2}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{H^{2}(A_{R/2}(s_{0}))}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}^{2}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}^{2}\bigg)\,,

by a similar scaling argument to the proof of Lemma
6.6
. We thus have

(340)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{\rho})}^{2}+\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{\rho})}^{2}\leq 2\big(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{\rho})}^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{b}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{\rho})}^{2}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{\rho})}^{2}+\left\lVert\zeta-\overline{p}_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{\rho})}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}^{2}+\left\lVert q\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}\bigg)+2\left\lVert\bm{b}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{\rho})}^{2}+2\left\lVert\zeta-\overline{p}_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{\rho})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{2}\bigg(\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}^{2}+\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle+c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(R^{2\alpha}\big(\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}\big)+R^{2+2\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\bigg)\,,

where we have used the estimate (
339
).

We may now apply a useful proposition from
[
28
]
:

Proposition 6.7

(Lemma 5.13 from
[
28
]
)
.

Given
m>\nu>0
and a nondecreasing function
\Xi:\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfying

\displaystyle\Xi(\rho)\leq c_{1}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R}\bigg)^{m}+\omega\bigg)\Xi(R)+c_{2}R^{\nu}

for all
0<\rho\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
, for
\omega
sufficiently small (depending on
c_{1},m,\nu
), we in fact have

(341)

\Xi(\rho)\leq c_{3}\bigg(\frac{\Xi(R)}{R^{\nu}}+c_{2}\bigg)\rho^{\nu}

for all
0\leq\rho\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
.

Applying Proposition
6.7
to (
340
) with
\Xi(\rho)=\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{\rho})}^{2}+\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{\rho})}^{2}
,
m=2
, and
\nu=2-\delta
for
\delta>0
, for
R
sufficiently small we obtain

(342)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{\rho}(s_{0}))}^{2}+\left\lVert\overline{p}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{\rho}(s_{0}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(R^{-(2-\delta)}\big(\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}\big)+R^{2\alpha+\delta}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\bigg)\rho^{2-\delta}

for
0\leq\rho\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
.

We may now estimate the oscillation of
\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}
over
A_{\rho}(s_{0})
. For
0<\rho\leq R^{\prime}\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
, we have

(343)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}\leq\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}2\bigg(\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{h}-(\partial_{s}\bm{h})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{b}-(\partial_{s}\bm{b})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R^{\prime}}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\bm{h}-(\partial_{s}\bm{h})_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+4\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla\bm{b}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R^{\prime}}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*})\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla{\bm{b}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R^{\prime}}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle+c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\bigg((R^{\prime})^{2\alpha}\big(\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R^{\prime}})}+\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R^{\prime}})}^{2}\big)+(R^{\prime})^{2+2\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R^{\prime}}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\,(R^{\prime})^{2+2\alpha-\delta}\,M\,,

where
M=R^{-(2-\delta)}\big(\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(A_{R})}+\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(A_{R})}^{2}\big)+R^{\delta}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}
.

Using Proposition
6.7
with
\Xi(\rho)=\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}
,
m=4
, and
\nu=2+2\alpha-\delta
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\rho^{2+2\alpha-\delta}c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(R^{-(2+2\alpha-\delta)}\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+M\bigg)

for any
0\leq\rho\leq R\leq\frac{r_{*}}{2}
. Using Lemma
6.2
, we have
\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}\in C^{0,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}
for
\delta>0
small. Furthermore, using that
\overline{\bm{u}}\big|_{\Psi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})}
is independent of
\theta
, we also have
\partial_{\theta}\overline{\bm{u}}\big|_{\Psi(\Gamma_{\epsilon})\cap\partial A_{R}(s_{0})}\in C^{0,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}
. Using classical elliptic regularity theory, we then have
(\overline{\bm{u}},\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R})\in C^{1,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}(A_{R}(s_{0}))\times C^{0,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
with

(344)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}+\left\lVert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha-\frac{\delta}{2}}(A_{R}(s_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\big(\left\lVert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rVert_{H^{1}(\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}))}+\left\lVert\overline{p}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}}))}+\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\big)\,.

In particular, we thus have
(\nabla\overline{\bm{u}},\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R})\in L^{\infty}(A_{R}(s_{0}))\times L^{\infty}(A_{R}(s_{0}))
and we may estimate

\displaystyle\int_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg(\left\lvert\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c\,R^{2}\sup_{\bm{y}\in A_{R}(s_{0})}\bigg(\left\lvert\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\bigg)\,.

The estimate (
343
) may thus be improved to

(345)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\kappa_{*},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(\frac{\rho}{R^{\prime}}\bigg)^{4}\int_{A_{R^{\prime}}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},R^{\prime}}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\,(R^{\prime})^{2+2\alpha}\,M_{2}\,,

where
M_{2}=\sup_{\bm{y}\in A_{R}(s_{0})}\big(\left\lvert\nabla\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\overline{\bm{u}}\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\overline{p}-\overline{p}_{s_{0},R}\right\rvert^{2}\big)+\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}
. By Proposition
6.7
, we then have

(346)

\displaystyle\fint_{A_{\rho}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{2\alpha}\,c(\epsilon,\left\lVert\bm{X}_{ss}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\bigg(R^{-2\alpha}\fint_{A_{R}(s_{0})}\left\lvert\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}-(\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}})_{s_{0},\rho}\right\rvert^{2}\,d\bm{y}+M_{2}\bigg)\,.

By covering the region
\Psi(\mathcal{O}_{r_{*}})
with annuli
A_{R}(s_{0})
,
s_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
, we thus have that
\partial_{s}\overline{\bm{u}}
belongs to the Campanato-type space
\mathcal{A}^{2,\alpha}
(
311
). Using Lemma
6.2
along with
\bm{u}=\overline{\bm{u}}\circ\Psi
, we obtain that
\bm{u}\big|_{\Gamma_{\epsilon}}=\bm{u}(s)\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
. The estimate (
75
) follows from the bounds (
344
) and (
303
).

Finally, we show the Lipschitz estimate (
76
). Given two nearby filaments with centerlines
\bm{X}^{(a)}
,
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfying Lemma
1.1
, we aim to bound the difference
\bm{v}^{(a)}(s)-\bm{v}^{(b)}(s)
for
\bm{v}^{(a)}(s),\bm{v}^{(b)}(s)
satisfying

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(a)})^{-1}[\bm{v}^{(a)}](s)=\bm{f}(s)=(\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(b)})^{-1}[\bm{v}^{(b)}](s)\,.

Rearranging, we may write

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{(a)}-\bm{v}^{(b)}=\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\big[(\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(b)})^{-1}[\bm{v}^{(b)}]-(\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(a)})^{-1}[\bm{v}^{(b)}]\big]\,.

We may then apply the estimate (
75
) for
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(b)}
, and the Lipschitz estimate (
31
) for
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{-1}
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{v}^{(a)}-\bm{v}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(a)}\big[(\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(b)})^{-1}[\bm{v}^{(b)}]-(\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(a)})^{-1}[\bm{v}^{(b)}]\big]\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(a)}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\,\left\lVert(\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(b)})^{-1}[\bm{v}^{(b)}]-(\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}^{(a)})^{-1}[\bm{v}^{(b)}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(a)}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}},\kappa_{*,\alpha^{+}}^{(b)},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{v}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq c(\epsilon,\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(a)}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}},\|\bm{X}_{ss}^{(b)}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}},c_{\Gamma})\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha^{+}}}\left\lVert\bm{f}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\,.

∎

Appendix A
Proof of Lemma
1.1
: nearby curves

Given the two curves
\bm{X}^{(a)}(s)
,
\bm{X}^{(b)}(s)
satisfying (
9
), we begin by framing both curves with a corresponding Bishop frame
(\bm{e}_{\rm t},\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1},\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{2})

[
9
]
, which, given an initial choice of normal vector
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}(0)
, satisfies the ODE

(347)

\frac{d}{ds}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{e}_{\rm t}\\
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}\\
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{2}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}&\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\\
-\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}&0&0\\
-\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}&0&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{e}_{\rm t}\\
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}\\
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\,.

Here for curve (a) we choose the initial normal vector
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(a)}(0)
arbitrarily, while
\widetilde{\bm{e}}^{(b)}_{1}(0)
is chosen to satisfy

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{e}}^{(b)}_{1}(0)=\inf_{\bm{v}\perp\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(b)}(0),\,\left\lvert\bm{v}\right\rvert=1}\left\lvert\bm{v}-\widetilde{\bm{e}}^{(a)}_{1}(0)\right\rvert\,;

i.e. at the closest pair of points
\bm{X}^{(a)}(0)
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}(0)
, we choose the normal vectors to be as close as possible. Given that
\bm{X}^{(a)}
and
\bm{X}^{(b)}
satisfy (
9
), the difference in initial normal vectors must then satisfy

(348)

\left\lvert\widetilde{\bm{e}}^{(a)}_{1}(0)-\widetilde{\bm{e}}^{(b)}_{1}(0)\right\rvert\leq\delta\,.

Although the pure Bishop frame is not necessarily periodic in
s
, its coefficients
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}
control the coefficients from the periodicized frame (
3
).
In particular, using the Bishop frame (
347
), we define the angle
-\pi\leq\phi<\pi
to be

\displaystyle\phi=\arccos(\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}(0)\cdot\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}(1))\,,

i.e. the mismatch from periodicity.
Take
\kappa_{3}=-\phi
and note that for the curves (a) and (b) we have

(349)

\left\lvert\kappa_{3}^{(a)}-\kappa_{3}^{(b)}\right\rvert\leq c\,\left\lVert\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Furthermore, given
\kappa_{3}
as above, we may define

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s)\\
\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s)\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\cos(s\kappa_{3})&-\sin(s\kappa_{3})\\
\sin(s\kappa_{3})&\cos(s\kappa_{3})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}(s)\\
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{2}(s)\end{pmatrix}\,.

It may be seen that
(\bm{e}_{\rm t}(s),\bm{e}_{\rm n_{1}}(s),\bm{e}_{\rm n_{2}}(s))
satisfy the ODE (
3
) with

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}(s)\\
\kappa_{2}(s)\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\cos(s\kappa_{3})&-\sin(s\kappa_{3})\\
\sin(s\kappa_{3})&\cos(s\kappa_{3})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}(s)\\
\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}(s)\end{pmatrix}\,.

For both
\ell=1,2
, we then have

(350)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\kappa_{\ell}^{(a)}-\kappa_{\ell}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(b)})\left\lvert\kappa_{3}^{(a)}-\kappa_{3}^{(b)}\right\rvert+c\left\lVert\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}+c\left\lVert\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(b)})\left(\left\lVert\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}+\left\lVert\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}\right)\,.

It thus suffices to show the desired bound (
10
) for the coefficients
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}
,
\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}
of the pure Bishop frame. For both curves (a) and (b), we may rewrite the ODEs (
347
) for
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{2}
as

(351)

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{\bm{e}}}_{1}

\displaystyle=-(\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}\cdot\dot{\bm{e}}_{\rm t})\,\bm{e}_{\rm t}

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{\bm{e}}}_{2}

\displaystyle=-(\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{2}\cdot\dot{\bm{e}}_{\rm t})\,\bm{e}_{\rm t}\,,

where we use
\dot{\bm{e}}
to denote
\frac{d}{ds}\bm{e}
.
Letting
\bm{w}=\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(b)}
, we then have

(352)

\displaystyle\dot{\bm{w}}

\displaystyle=-(\bm{w}\cdot\dot{\bm{e}}_{\rm t}^{(a)})\,\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-(\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(b)}\cdot(\dot{\bm{e}}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-\dot{\bm{e}}_{\rm t}^{(b)}))\,\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-(\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(b)}\cdot\dot{\bm{e}}_{\rm t}^{(b)})\,(\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(b)})\,.

In particular, using that
\left\lVert\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-\bm{e}_{\rm t}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\beta}}\leq\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\beta}}=\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{ds}\left\lvert\bm{w}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)})\left\lvert\bm{w}\right\rvert^{2}+\delta\left\lvert\bm{w}\right\rvert+c(\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\delta\left\lvert\bm{w}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\big(\left\lvert\bm{w}\right\rvert^{2}+\delta^{2}\big)\,.

Using Grönwall’s inequality and (
348
), we then have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{w}\right\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\big(\left\lvert\bm{w}(0)\right\rvert^{2}+\delta^{2}\big)\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\,\delta^{2}\,.

Returning to the ODE (
352
) for
\bm{w}
, we also obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\dot{\bm{w}}\right\rvert\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)})\left\lvert\bm{w}\right\rvert+\delta+c(\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\delta\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\,\delta\,.

In total, recalling that
\delta=\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\beta}}
and
\bm{w}=\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(b)}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{1}}\leq c(\kappa_{*}^{(a)},\kappa_{*}^{(b)})\,\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\beta}}\,.

We may then use the ODEs (
347
), (
351
) to obtain bounds on the coefficients
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{(b)}=(\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(b)})\cdot\dot{\bm{e}}_{\rm t}^{(a)}+\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1}^{(b)}\cdot(\dot{\bm{e}}_{\rm t}^{(a)}-\dot{\bm{e}}_{\rm t}^{(b)})\,,

and thus

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\beta}}\leq c(\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(a)},\kappa_{*,\beta}^{(b)})\,\left\lVert\bm{X}^{(a)}-\bm{X}^{(b)}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\beta}}\,.

Using the ODE (
351
) for
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{2}
, the same bound may be shown for
\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{(b)}
. Then by (
349
) and (
350
), we obtain Lemma
1.1
.
∎

Appendix B
Calculation of straight single and double layer symbols

Here we derive the expressions stated in Lemma
1.7
for the single and double layer operators
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
about the straight filament
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
.

Proof of Lemma
1.7
.

For the straight filament, periodicity of the operators
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
is enforced by considering only densities
\bm{\varphi}(s,\theta)
which are 1-periodic in
s
. For such a density
\bm{\varphi}
, we write
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
as

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[\bm{\varphi}](s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\overline{\mathcal{G}}(s,\theta,s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,\bm{\varphi}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{D}}[\bm{\varphi}](s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\overline{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{D}}(s,\theta,s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,\bm{\varphi}(s^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,,

where the kernels
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
and
\overline{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{D}}
will be written in more detail below. Recalling the general form of the Stokeslet
\mathcal{G}
(
37
) and double layer kernel
\bm{K}_{\mathcal{D}}
(
38
), we note that about
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
we may write

(353)

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{x}}-\overline{\bm{x}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\textstyle(s-s^{\prime})\bm{e}_{z}+2\epsilon\sin(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\bm{e}_{\theta}(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})

\displaystyle(\overline{\bm{x}}-\overline{\bm{x}}^{\prime})\cdot\bm{e}_{r}(\theta^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\textstyle-2\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})

\displaystyle\textstyle\bm{e}_{\theta}(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\sin(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})\bm{e}_{x}+\cos(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})\bm{e}_{y}\,.

Here again we use overline notation to emphasize that
\overline{\bm{x}}
lies along the straight filament
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
.
Given a continuous density
\bm{\varphi}
along
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
, we may use (
353
) to write out the integrand (
37
) of the single layer operator about
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon}
as

(354)

\displaystyle 8\pi(\overline{\mathcal{G}}\bm{\varphi})\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\frac{(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{z})}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}}+\frac{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{z})}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+\frac{2\epsilon(s-s^{\prime})\sin(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big(-\sin(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{x})+\cos(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{y})\big)}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}

\displaystyle 8\pi(\overline{\mathcal{G}}\bm{\varphi})\cdot\bm{e}_{x}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\frac{(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{x})}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}}-\frac{2\epsilon(s-s^{\prime})\sin(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\sin(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{z})}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+\frac{4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big(\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{x})-\sin(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})\cos(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{y})\big)}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}

\displaystyle 8\pi(\overline{\mathcal{G}}\bm{\varphi})\cdot\bm{e}_{y}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\frac{(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{y})}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}}+\frac{2\epsilon(s-s^{\prime})\sin(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\cos(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{z})}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+\frac{4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big(\cos^{2}(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{y})-\sin(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})\cos(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{x})\big)}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}\,.

Similarly, using (
353
) in (
38
) the integrand of the straight double layer operator may be written

(355)

\displaystyle(\overline{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{D}}\bm{\varphi})\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=-\textstyle\frac{3}{\pi}\frac{\epsilon^{2}(s-s^{\prime})\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big(-\sin(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{x})+\cos(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{y})\big)}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}

\displaystyle-\textstyle\frac{3}{4\pi}\frac{2\epsilon(s-s^{\prime})^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{z})}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}

\displaystyle(\overline{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{D}}\bm{\varphi})\cdot\bm{e}_{x}

\displaystyle=-\textstyle\frac{6}{\pi}\frac{\epsilon^{3}\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big(\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{x})-\sin(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})\cos(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{y})\big)}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}

\displaystyle+\textstyle\frac{3}{\pi}\frac{\epsilon^{2}(s-s^{\prime})\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\sin(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{z})}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}

\displaystyle(\overline{\bm{K}}_{\mathcal{D}}\bm{\varphi})\cdot\bm{e}_{y}

\displaystyle=-\textstyle\frac{6}{\pi}\frac{\epsilon^{3}\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big(\cos^{2}(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{y})-\sin(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})\cos(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{x})\big)}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}

\displaystyle-\textstyle\frac{3}{\pi}\frac{\epsilon^{2}(s-s^{\prime})\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})\cos(\frac{\theta+\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\bm{\varphi}\cdot\bm{e}_{z})}{\sqrt{(s-s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}\,.

Before computing anything involving (
354
) and (
355
), we make note of a series of identities involving integrals of the second-kind modified Bessel functions
K_{0}
and
K_{1}
. In particular, the following identities hold:

(356)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle K_{0}(z\sin(\frac{\theta}{2}))\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\textstyle 2\pi I_{0}(\frac{z}{2})K_{0}(\frac{z}{2})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\sin(\frac{\theta}{2})\,K_{1}(z\sin(\frac{\theta}{2}))\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\textstyle\pi\big(I_{0}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2})-I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{0}(\frac{z}{2})\big)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta}{2})\,K_{0}(z\sin(\frac{\theta}{2}))\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\textstyle\pi\big(I_{0}(\frac{z}{2})K_{0}(\frac{z}{2})-I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2})\big)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta}{2})\,K_{1}(z\sin(\frac{\theta}{2}))\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\textstyle\pi\big(I_{0}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2})-I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{0}(\frac{z}{2})\big)-\frac{2\pi}{z}I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta}{2})\,K_{0}(z\sin(\frac{\theta}{2}))\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\textstyle\pi(I_{0}(\frac{z}{2})K_{0}(\frac{z}{2})-I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2}))+\frac{\pi}{z}(I_{0}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2})-I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{0}(\frac{z}{2}))

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle-\frac{4\pi}{z^{2}}I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\sin^{5}(\frac{\theta}{2})\,K_{1}(z\sin(\frac{\theta}{2}))\,d\theta

\displaystyle=\textstyle\pi(I_{0}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2})-I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{0}(\frac{z}{2}))+\frac{\pi}{z}(I_{0}(\frac{z}{2})K_{0}(\frac{z}{2})-3I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2}))

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle+\frac{4\pi}{z^{2}}(I_{0}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2})-I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{0}(\frac{z}{2}))-\frac{16\pi}{z^{3}}I_{1}(\frac{z}{2})K_{1}(\frac{z}{2})\,.

Here
I_{0}
and
I_{1}
are first-kind modified Bessel functions. The first integral identity is shown in
[
59
]
; the rest may be seen by differentiating the first identity with respect to
z
, using that
K_{0}^{\prime}(z)=-K_{1}(z)
and
K_{1}^{\prime}(z)=-K_{0}(z)-\frac{K_{1}(z)}{z}
.

Equipped with the expressions (
354
) and (
355
) and the identities (
356
), we may show Lemma
1.7
.
We begin with the expression (
47
), i.e. how the double layer acts on
\theta
-independent functions
\bm{v}(s)=e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}
tangent to the filament centerline. Using (
355
) and (
356
), we may calculate

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{D}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}]\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\frac{\epsilon\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})(s^{\prime})^{2}}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bigg(\textstyle\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{1}\big(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big)-4\pi\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{0}\big(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big)\bigg)\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}

\displaystyle=-\left(-4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}\big(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1}\big)+\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\big(I_{0}K_{1}-I_{1}K_{0}\big)\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{D}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}]\cdot\bm{e}_{r}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}\,s^{\prime}\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle 4\pi i\epsilon^{2}k^{2}\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{1}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}

\displaystyle=-i\left(4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert I_{1}K_{1}\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\,.

Here each
I_{j}
,
K_{j}
in the final expressions is evaluated at
2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert
. We also note that
\overline{\mathcal{D}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}]\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}=0
.

To calculate the expression (
49
) for how
\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}
acts on zero modes in
\theta
, we first determine how
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
acts on functions of the form
e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}
and
e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{r}
. Using (
354
) and (
356
), for the
\bm{e}_{z}
direction we may calculate

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}]\cdot\bm{e}_{r}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\frac{2\epsilon\,s^{\prime}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle i\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}

\displaystyle=-i\pi\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})\,e^{2\pi iks}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}]\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\bigg(\frac{2}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}}-\frac{4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}\bigg)\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})\,K_{1}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\bigg)\,e^{2\pi iks}

\displaystyle=\epsilon\left(I_{0}K_{0}+\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\,.

For the
\bm{e}_{r}
direction, we may calculate

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{r}]\cdot\bm{e}_{r}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\bigg(\frac{1-2\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}}-\frac{4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}\bigg)\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\left(1-2\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})\right)\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{1}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}

\displaystyle=\epsilon\left(I_{1}K_{1}+\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})\right)e^{2\pi iks}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{r}]\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\frac{2\epsilon\,s^{\prime}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle i\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}

\displaystyle=i\pi\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})\,e^{2\pi iks}\,.

Each
I_{j}
and
K_{j}
in the final expressions is again evaluated at
2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert
. We further note that
\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}]\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}=\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{r}]\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}=0
.

In total, we obtain the following expression for the action of
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
on the relevant zero modes in
\theta
:

(357)

\overline{\mathcal{S}}\begin{pmatrix}e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}\\
e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{r}\end{pmatrix}=\epsilon\begin{pmatrix}I_{0}K_{0}+\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})&i\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})\\
-i\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})&I_{1}K_{1}+\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{z}\\
e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{r}\end{pmatrix}\,.

Here
I_{j}=I_{j}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert)
and
K_{j}=K_{j}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert)
. Inverting the matrix (
357
), we obtain the expression (
49
) and (
50
) for the multiplier
\bm{M}_{\rm S,t}^{-1}(k)
in the tangential direction along the filament.

We next consider
\theta
-independent data in directions normal to the filament centerline. We consider data of the form
e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{x}
and note that, by symmetry, data of the form
e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{y}
behaves analogously. Note that, using the identities
\bm{e}_{x}=\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r}-\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}
and
\bm{e}_{y}=\sin\theta\bm{e}_{r}+\cos\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}
, functions of this form actually correspond to one-modes in
\theta
.

We begin by calculating the expressions (
51
) for the double layer multipliers.
Using the form (
355
) of the integrand of
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
as well as the identities (
356
), we may calculate

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{D}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{x}]\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{4\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\frac{4\epsilon^{2}\,s^{\prime}\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,\cos\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle i4\pi\epsilon^{2}k^{2}\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{1}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta

\displaystyle=i\left(4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}(I_{0}K_{1}-I_{1}K_{0})-2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert I_{1}K_{1}\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{D}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{x}]\cdot\bm{e}_{x}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{4\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\frac{8\epsilon^{3}\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big(\sin^{2}\theta-\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\sin^{2}\theta-\cos^{2}\theta)\big)}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,5}}\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle 4\pi\epsilon^{2}k^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{2}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle 4\pi\epsilon^{2}k^{2}\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{2}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}(\sin^{2}\theta-\cos^{2}\theta)

\displaystyle=-\left(\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+2I_{1}K_{1}\right)e^{2\pi iks}\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(I_{1}K_{1}-I_{0}K_{0})+\textstyle 3\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+2I_{1}K_{1}\right)e^{2\pi iks}\cos^{2}\theta\,.

Here we have also used the Bessel function identity
K_{2}(z)=K_{0}(z)+2\frac{K_{1}(z)}{z}
. As before, in the final expressions, each
I_{j}
and
K_{j}
is evaluated at
2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert
. Altogether, the double layer operator
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
can be seen to act on
\theta
-independent functions in directions normal to the filament centerline via

(358)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{D}}[e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{x}]

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathcal{D}}[e^{2\pi iks}\big(\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r}-\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}\big)]=\left(Q_{N}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r}-Q_{O}\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}+iQ_{P}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{z}\right)e^{2\pi iks}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{N}

\displaystyle=4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert^{2}(I_{1}K_{1}-I_{0}K_{0})+\textstyle 3\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+2I_{1}K_{1}

\displaystyle Q_{O}

\displaystyle=-\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})-2I_{1}K_{1}

\displaystyle Q_{P}

\displaystyle=4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}(I_{0}K_{1}-I_{1}K_{0})-2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert I_{1}K_{1}\,.

We next turn to the single layer operator. Given the form of the expressions arising in the double layer operator, we will need to calculate a full matrix-valued multiplier for
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
in order to determine how
\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}
acts on functions of the form (
358
). Recalling the series of identities (
356
) as well as the form (
354
) of the integrand of
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
, we begin by calculating

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[

\displaystyle e^{2\pi iks}\bm{e}_{x}]\cdot\bm{e}_{x}=\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r}]\cdot\bm{e}_{x}-\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}]\cdot\bm{e}_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bigg(\textstyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}}+\frac{4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big(\sin^{2}\theta-\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})(\sin^{2}\theta-\cos^{2}\theta)\big)}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}\bigg)\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{1}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle\qquad\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})K_{1}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}(\cos^{2}\theta-\sin^{2}\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon}{2}(I_{0}K_{0}+I_{1}K_{1})\,e^{2\pi iks}\sin^{2}\theta+\epsilon\left(\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{0}K_{1}-I_{1}K_{0})+\frac{1}{2}(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos^{2}\theta\,.

As usual,
I_{j}=I_{j}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert)
and
K_{j}=K_{j}(2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert)
in the final expressions. We may also calculate

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[

\displaystyle e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r}]\cdot\bm{e}_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\bigg(\frac{8\epsilon^{2}(\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-\sin^{6}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}+\frac{4\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-4\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}}\bigg)\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\bigg(\frac{4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-8\epsilon^{2}\sin^{6}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}-\frac{1-4\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})+4\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}}\bigg)\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,\cos^{2}\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bigg(\textstyle\frac{\epsilon}{\pi}(\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))

\displaystyle\qquad+\textstyle 2\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert(\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-\sin^{5}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))K_{1}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\bigg)\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bigg(\textstyle\frac{\epsilon}{4\pi}(1-4\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})+4\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))

\displaystyle\qquad-\textstyle\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert(\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-2\sin^{5}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))K_{1}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\bigg)\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos^{2}\theta

\displaystyle=\textstyle\epsilon\left(\frac{3}{4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+\frac{3}{4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}I_{1}K_{1}-\frac{1}{2}(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon\left(\textstyle\frac{3}{4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+\frac{3}{4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}I_{1}K_{1}-\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{0}K_{1}-I_{1}K_{0})-(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos^{2}\theta\,,

from which we may then obtain

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}]\cdot\bm{e}_{x}

\displaystyle=\epsilon\left(\textstyle\frac{3}{4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+\frac{3}{4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}I_{1}K_{1}-I_{0}K_{0}\right)e^{2\pi iks}\,\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\left(\textstyle-\frac{3}{4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})-\frac{3}{4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}I_{1}K_{1}+\frac{1}{2}(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos^{2}\theta\,.

In the
\bm{e}_{z}
direction, we additionally have

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r}]\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\frac{2\epsilon\,s^{\prime}(\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-2\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,\cos\theta

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle i\epsilon^{2}k\big(-\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})+2\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big)K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta

\displaystyle=-\epsilon i\left(\textstyle\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{1}-I_{0}K_{0})+\frac{1}{2}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}I_{1}K_{1}\right)e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}[e^{2\pi iks}\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}]\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\frac{4\epsilon\,s^{\prime}(\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,\cos\theta

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle i2\epsilon^{2}k\big(-\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})+\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})\big)K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta

\displaystyle=-\epsilon i\left(\textstyle\frac{1}{2}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}I_{1}K_{1}\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\,,

\displaystyle\overline{S}[e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{z}]\cdot\bm{e}_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\bigg(\frac{2(1-2\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}}-\frac{4\epsilon^{2}(\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-2\sin^{4}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))}{\sqrt{(s^{\prime})^{2}+4\epsilon^{2}\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})}^{\,3}}\bigg)\,\,e^{2\pi ik(s-s^{\prime})}\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}ds^{\prime}\,\cos\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle(1-2\sin^{2}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))K_{0}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,\epsilon\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\textstyle\epsilon^{2}\left\lvert k\right\rvert(\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2})-2\sin^{3}(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))K_{1}(4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert\sin(\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{2}))\,d\theta^{\prime}\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta

\displaystyle=\epsilon\left(\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{0}K_{1}-I_{1}K_{0})\right)\,e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\,.

To summarize, we may write the action of
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
on the vectors
(\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r},\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta},\cos\theta\bm{e}_{z})
associated with the
\bm{e}_{x}
direction as

(359)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r}\\
e^{2\pi iks}\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}\\
e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{z}\end{pmatrix}=\epsilon\begin{pmatrix}Q_{H}&Q_{I}&-iQ_{J}\\
Q_{I}&Q_{K}&-iQ_{L}\\
iQ_{J}&iQ_{L}&Q_{M}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{r}\\
e^{2\pi iks}\sin\theta\bm{e}_{\theta}\\
e^{2\pi iks}\cos\theta\bm{e}_{z}\end{pmatrix}\,,

\displaystyle Q_{H}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\frac{3}{4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})-\frac{3}{4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}I_{1}K_{1}+\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{0}K_{1}-I_{1}K_{0})+(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})

\displaystyle Q_{I}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\frac{3}{4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})-\frac{3}{4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}I_{1}K_{1}+\frac{1}{2}(I_{0}K_{0}-I_{1}K_{1})

\displaystyle Q_{J}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{1}K_{1}-I_{0}K_{0})+\frac{1}{2}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}I_{1}K_{1}

\displaystyle Q_{K}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\bigg(\frac{3}{4\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+\frac{3}{4\pi^{2}\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}I_{1}K_{1}-I_{0}K_{0}\bigg)

\displaystyle Q_{L}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\frac{1}{2}(I_{1}K_{0}-I_{0}K_{1})+\frac{1}{2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert}I_{1}K_{1}

\displaystyle Q_{M}

\displaystyle=\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert(I_{0}K_{1}-I_{1}K_{0})\,,

with each
I_{j}
,
K_{j}
evaluated at
2\pi\epsilon\left\lvert k\right\rvert
. Inverting the matrix (
359
), we arrive at the expression (
54
), (
55
) for
\bm{M}_{\rm S,n}^{-1}(k)
stated in Lemma
1.7
. The same expression for the
y
-direction follows by symmetry.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Proposition
2.1
: commutator estimate

Here we show the commutator estimate (
87
) for the multiplier
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{2}
. We will rely on the tools of section
3.1
. Before proving Proposition
2.1
, we show the following auxiliary lemma.

Lemma C.1

(Order one commutator estimate)
.

Let
\widehat{\bm{M}}(\xi)
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, be a matrix-valued
W^{2,\infty}_{\rm loc}
function satisfying

(360)

\left\lvert\phi_{j}(\xi)\widehat{\bm{M}}(\xi)\right\rvert\leq b_{0}\,2^{j}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{2}(\phi_{j}(\xi)\widehat{\bm{M}}(\xi))\right\rvert\leq b_{1}\,2^{-j}

for
\phi_{j}(\xi)
as in (
105
).
Given matrix-valued
\bm{A}\in C^{n+1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
and vector-valued
\bm{g}\in C^{n,\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
,
0\leq n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
0<\alpha<1
, the commutator
T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}(\bm{A}\bm{g})-\bm{A}(T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g})
satisfies

(361)

\left\lVert T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}(\bm{A}\bm{g})-\bm{A}(T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g})\right\rVert_{C^{n,\alpha}}\leq c\,(\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}+b_{1})\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{C^{n+1,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{n,\alpha}}\,.

Proof.

Following
[
5
, Chapter 2.8]
, we begin by defining a decomposition of the product
\bm{A}\bm{g}
into three pieces capturing interactions among different modes in the Littlewood-Paley decomposition (
106
). We write

(362)

\bm{A}\bm{g}=\mathcal{T}_{LH}(\bm{A},\bm{g})+\mathcal{T}_{HL}(\bm{A},\bm{g})+\mathcal{T}_{HH}(\bm{A},\bm{g})\,,

where the low-high, high-low, and high-high interactions, respectively, are given by

(363)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{LH}(\bm{A},\bm{g})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 1}P_{j}\bm{A}\,P_{\leq j-2}\bm{g}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{HL}(\bm{A},\bm{g})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 1}P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\,P_{j}\bm{g}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{HH}(\bm{A},\bm{g})

\displaystyle=P_{\leq 0}\bm{A}\,P_{\leq 0}\bm{g}+\sum_{\left\lvert i-j\right\rvert\leq 1,\,i+j\geq 1}P_{j}\bm{A}\,P_{i}\bm{g}\,.

Using the definitions (
363
), we decompose the commutator expression (
361
) into three parts:

(364)

\displaystyle T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}(\bm{A}\bm{g})-\bm{A}(T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g})

\displaystyle=J_{LH}+J_{HL}+J_{HH}\,,

\displaystyle J_{LH}

\displaystyle=T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\mathcal{T}_{LH}(\bm{A},\bm{g})-\mathcal{T}_{LH}(\bm{A},T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g})

\displaystyle J_{HL}

\displaystyle=T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\mathcal{T}_{HL}(\bm{A},\bm{g})-\mathcal{T}_{HL}(\bm{A},T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g})

\displaystyle J_{HH}

\displaystyle=T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\mathcal{T}_{HH}(\bm{A},\bm{g})-\mathcal{T}_{HH}(\bm{A},T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g})\,.

We first compute the low-high interactions
J_{LH}
. For
k\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle P_{k}J_{LH}

\displaystyle=P_{k}\bigg(T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\sum_{j=k-2}^{k+2}P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\,P_{j}\bm{g}-\sum_{j=k-2}^{k+2}P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\,P_{j}(T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\bm{g})\bigg)

\displaystyle=P_{k}\bigg(P_{k-5\leq\ell\leq k+5}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\sum_{j=k-2}^{k+2}P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\,P_{j}\bm{g}-\sum_{j=k-2}^{k+2}P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\,P_{k-5\leq\ell\leq k+5}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}P_{j}\bm{g}\bigg)\,.

Let
\bm{M}_{k}
denote the physical space kernel corresponding to the operator
P_{k-5\leq j\leq k+5}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}
. For each
j
, we may write

\displaystyle P_{k-5\leq\ell\leq k+5}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\,P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\,P_{j}\bm{g}-P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\,P_{k-5\leq\ell\leq k+5}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\,P_{j}\bm{g}

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg(\bm{M}_{k}(s-s^{\prime})\,P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}(s^{\prime})\,P_{j}\bm{g}(s^{\prime})-P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}(s)\,\bm{M}_{k}(s-s^{\prime})\,P_{j}\bm{g}(s^{\prime})\bigg)\,ds^{\prime}\,.

We may then estimate

(365)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bm{M}_{k}(s-s^{\prime})\,\big(P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}(s^{\prime})-P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}(s)\big)\,P_{j}\bm{g}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\lvert\bm{M}_{k}(s-s^{\prime})\right\rvert\left\lvert s-s^{\prime}\right\rvert\left\lVert\partial_{s}(P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\left\lVert\bm{M}_{k}\,\left\lvert s\right\rvert\,\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\,2^{j}\left\lVert P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

We note that (
360
) implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\lvert\bm{M}_{k}(s)\right\rvert\left\lvert s\right\rvert\,ds

\displaystyle\leq R_{k}\int_{\left\lvert s\right\rvert\leq R_{k}}\left\lvert\bm{M}_{k}\right\rvert\,ds+\int_{\left\lvert s\right\rvert>R_{k}}\left\lvert\bm{M}_{k}\right\rvert\left\lvert s\right\rvert\,ds

\displaystyle\leq R_{k}\int_{\left\lvert s\right\rvert\leq R_{k}}\left\lvert\bm{M}_{k}\right\rvert\,ds+\bigg(\int_{\left\lvert s\right\rvert>R_{k}}\left\lvert\bm{M}_{k}\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert s\right\rvert^{4}\,ds\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\int_{\left\lvert s\right\rvert>R_{k}}\left\lvert s\right\rvert^{-2}\,ds\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq R_{k}\left\lVert\bm{M}_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}+c\,R_{k}^{-1/2}\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{2}\widehat{\bm{M}}_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq c\,R_{k}\,2^{k}\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}+c\,R_{k}^{-1/2}\,2^{-k/2}b_{1}\,,

where we have used Lemma
3.1
. Choosing
R_{k}\sim 2^{-k}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\lvert\bm{M}_{k}(s)\right\rvert\left\lvert s\right\rvert\,ds\leq c\,(\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}+b_{1})\,,

so, in particular,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bm{M}_{k}(s-s^{\prime})\,\big(P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}(s^{\prime})-P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}(s)\big)\,P_{j}\bm{g}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq c\,(\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}+b_{1})\,2^{j}\left\lVert P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Summing in
j
and applying
P_{k}
,
k\geq 1
, we thus obtain

\displaystyle 2^{k(n+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{k}J_{LH}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,(\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}+b_{1})\,2^{k(n+\alpha)}\sum_{j=k-2}^{k+2}2^{j}\left\lVert P_{\leq j-2}\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,(\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}+b_{1})\,\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{C^{1}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{n,\alpha}}\,.

For
P_{\leq 0}J_{LH}
, we have
\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}J_{LH}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,(\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}+b_{1})\,\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
.

We next consider the high-low interactions
J_{HL}
in (
364
). Similar to before, for
k\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle P_{k}J_{HL}

\displaystyle=P_{k}\bigg(P_{k-5\leq\ell\leq k+5}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\sum_{j=k-2}^{k+2}P_{j}\bm{A}P_{\leq j-2}\bm{g}-\sum_{j=k-2}^{k+2}P_{j}\bm{A}P_{k-5\leq\ell\leq k+5}T_{m}P_{\leq j-2}\bm{g}\bigg)\,.

For each
j
, the analogous integral to (
365
) is then

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg(\bm{M}_{k}(s-s^{\prime})\,P_{j}\bm{A}(s^{\prime})\,P_{\leq j-2}\bm{g}(s^{\prime})-P_{j}\bm{A}(s)\,\bm{M}_{k}(s-s^{\prime})\,P_{\leq j-2}\bm{g}(s^{\prime})\bigg)\,ds^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\left\lVert\bm{M}_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{\leq j-2}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}\,2^{k}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{\leq j-2}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,,

where we have used Lemma
3.1
to bound
\left\lVert\bm{M}_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}
.
Summing in
j
and applying
P_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle 2^{k(n+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{k}J_{HL}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,2^{k(n+\alpha)}\sum_{j=k-2}^{k+2}c\,\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}\,2^{k}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{\leq j-2}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}\,\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{C^{n+1,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

We additionally have
\left\lVert P_{\leq 0}J_{HL}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}\,\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
.

Similarly, for
J_{HH}
, for
k\geq 3
we have

\displaystyle P_{k}J_{HH}

\displaystyle=P_{k}\bigg(P_{k-5\leq\ell\leq k+5}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}\sum_{j\geq k-2,\,\left\lvert i-j\right\rvert\leq 1,\,i+j\geq 1}P_{j}\bm{A}\,P_{i}\bm{g}-\sum_{j\geq k-2,\,\left\lvert i-j\right\rvert\leq 1,\,i+j\geq 1}P_{j}\bm{A}\,P_{k-5\leq\ell\leq k+5}T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}P_{i}\bm{g}\bigg)\,.

For each
j,i
satisfying
j\geq k-2
,
\left\lvert i-j\right\rvert\leq 1
,
i+j\geq 1
, as in (
365
), we must estimate the integral

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg(\bm{M}_{k}(s-s^{\prime})\,P_{j}\bm{A}(s^{\prime})\,P_{i}\bm{g}(s^{\prime})-P_{j}\bm{A}(s)\,\bm{M}_{k}(s-s^{\prime})\,P_{i}\bm{g}(s^{\prime})\bigg)\,ds^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\left\lVert\bm{M}_{k}\right\rVert_{L^{1}}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{i}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}\,2^{k}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{i}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\,.

Again summing in
j
and
i
and applying
P_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle 2^{k(n+\alpha)}\left\lVert P_{k}J_{HH}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}\,2^{k(n+\alpha)}\sum_{j\geq k-2,\,\left\lvert i-j\right\rvert\leq 1,\,i+j\geq 1}2^{k}\left\lVert P_{j}\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert P_{i}\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}\sum_{j\geq k-2}2^{k(n+1+\alpha)}2^{-j(n+1+\alpha)}\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{C^{n+1,\alpha}}2^{-j\alpha}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c\,\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}\,\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{C^{n+1,\alpha}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Furthermore, for
P_{\leq 2}J_{HH}
we have
\left\lVert P_{\leq 2}J_{HH}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\,\sqrt{b_{0}b_{1}}\,\left\lVert\bm{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\left\lVert\bm{g}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
.

In total, we obtain Lemma
C.1
.
∎

We may now prove Proposition
2.1
by showing that the operator
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{2}
satisfies the assumptions (
360
) of Lemma
C.1
.

Proof of Proposition
2.1
.

Recalling the definition (
186
) of the multiplier
\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}(\xi)
, we may write
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\epsilon}\partial_{s}^{2}
as
T_{\widehat{\bm{M}}}
where

(366)

\widehat{\bm{M}}(\xi)=\widehat{\bm{M}}_{\epsilon}^{(1)}(\xi)\xi^{2}\,.

Using Lemmas
3.4
and
3.5
, we immediately have that

(367)

\left\lvert\phi_{j}(\xi)\widehat{\bm{M}}(\xi)\right\rvert\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,2^{j}\,,\qquad\left\lvert\partial_{\xi}^{2}(\phi_{j}(\xi)\widehat{\bm{M}}(\xi))\right\rvert\leq c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert\,2^{-j}\,.

We may thus apply Lemma
C.1
with
b_{0}=b_{1}=c\,\epsilon^{-1}\left\lvert\log\epsilon\right\rvert
to obtain Proposition
2.1
.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Lemma
5.4
: hypersingular bounds

Using that
\varphi\in C^{1,\alpha}(\Gamma_{\epsilon})
, we may expand
\varphi
about
(s,\theta)
as

(368)

\displaystyle\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s,\theta)

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\widehat{s}\,\partial_{s}\varphi(s,\theta)-\epsilon\widehat{\theta}\,\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi(s,\theta)

\displaystyle+\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})+|\epsilon\widehat{\theta}|^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,,

where

(369)

\left\lVert Q_{\varphi,s}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq c\left\lVert\partial_{s}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,,\qquad\left\lVert Q_{\varphi,\theta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq\textstyle c\left\lVert\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}}\,.

Similarly, we may expand
\varphi
about
(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)
as

(370)

\varphi(s-\widehat{s},\theta-\widehat{\theta})-\varphi(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)=(s_{0}+\widehat{s})H_{\varphi,s}+\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})H_{\varphi,\theta}\,,

but make note of two different expressions for the remainder terms
H_{\varphi}
. The first is given by

(371)

\displaystyle(s_{0}+\widehat{s})H_{\varphi,s}

\displaystyle=-(s_{0}+\widehat{s})\partial_{s}\varphi(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)

\displaystyle+\left\lvert s_{0}+\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})H_{\varphi,\theta}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)

\displaystyle+\left\lvert\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,,

i.e. the expansion (
368
) evaluated at
(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})
. The second is given by

(372)

\displaystyle(s_{0}+\widehat{s})H_{\varphi,s}

\displaystyle=-(s_{0}+\widehat{s})\partial_{s}\varphi(s,\theta)+\left\lvert s_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s,\theta,s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)

\displaystyle+\left\lvert\widehat{s}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,s}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})

\displaystyle\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})H_{\varphi,\theta}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\epsilon(\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{\theta}\varphi(s,\theta)+\left\lvert\epsilon\theta_{0}\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}(s,\theta,s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta)

\displaystyle+|\epsilon\widehat{\theta}|^{1+\alpha}Q_{\varphi,\theta}(s,\theta,\widehat{s},\widehat{\theta})\,.

In both (
371
) and (
372
), the terms
Q_{\varphi,\mu}
are as in (
369
).

Let
\widehat{I}_{\varphi}
denote the quantity of interest, i.e.

\displaystyle\widehat{I}_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\bigg|{\rm p.v.}\int_{-1/2-s_{0}}^{1/2-s_{0}}\int_{-\pi-\theta_{0}}^{\pi-\theta_{0}}I_{\varphi,\ell kn}(s_{0}+s,\theta_{0}+\theta,s_{0}+\widehat{s},\theta_{0}+\widehat{\theta})\,\epsilon\,d\widehat{\theta}d\widehat{s}
